THE PHILOSOPY OF THE PRODUCT | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

1. THE PHILOSOPHY OF THE PRODUCT
The world is constantly changing and so are you. To meet the changing needs you face as you
go through life, Liberty has developed the Lifestyle Protector product. Flexible enough to give
you exactly the cover that you need to match your circumstances today, and to adapt to your
changing needs as they unfold into the future.
The philosophy at the heart of the Lifestyle Protector
product is:
•

•

It allows you to tailor your policy to meet your own
specific needs, whether you are looking for personal
protection early in your working career, for family
protection, or even for special benefits that apply
uniquely to the self-employed.
It offers you a choice of cover periods to meet your
specific needs. Whole of Life cover provides long-term
security for as long as the policy is in force, while benefits
with cease ages and term provide cover for a chosen
period of time.

•

It offers you a choice of premium patterns to meet your
different cashflow needs.

•

It provides comprehensive cover for your every risk
requirement. It gives you protection against:

•

−

the risk of dying and the financial impact on your
surviving family and dependants

−

the risk of losing your income through some form of
disability or impairment that prevents you from
being able to earn a living

−

the risk of being diagnosed with a critical illness
and the financial impact it has on your lifestyle

−

the risk of losing valuable benefits due to temporary
financial difficulty, or being denied access to
benefits due to a deterioration in your health.

It gives you the opportunity to adjust your policy as your
circumstances change over time. It has been designed in
such a way that new options and benefits can easily be
added to existing policies, ensuring that you do not need
to buy a new policy to get access to the latest options.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR STRUCTURE

Covers the risk of being

from some form of critical

this cover with specific female
and child-related critical illness
benefits.

Covers the risk of losing
or being denied access to
appropriate levels of cover in
the future, either because of a
temporary inability to make
the premium payments for
some reason, or because your
health circumstances may have
changed.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

2. GENERIC STRUCTURE
Basic structure
Lifestyle Protector is structured so that:

7.

You have a choice of four premium patterns:
−

Each family member can select the benefits that they
require, provided that both the first and the second lives
assured select at least one core risk protection benefit
each.

Level Premium Pattern is ideal for individuals and
families who want long-term cover with a stable
premium.

−

Age Rated Premium Pattern is ideal for individuals
and businesses who would prefer the lowest
premiums possible initially but with higher premium
increases in future.

3.

A claim for one member of the family will not affect the
other family members’ benefits.

−

4.

The benefit range is flexible and comprehensive,
including a variety of benefits and cover periods to meet
your specific needs.

Renewable Premium Pattern (Closed to New
Business July 2022) is ideal for individuals or
businesses who expect their income to grow
significantly over the period until the premium
review date, but have cashflow constraints now.

−

Fixed Increasing Premium Pattern is ideal for
individuals and businesses who would prefer low
premiums initially and with the premiums increasing
at a known, fixed rate into the future.

1.

The whole family’s risk requirements can be provided
under one policy.

2.

5.

•

The minimum cover period for a policy is 5 years as
per legislation.

•

The maximum cover period can be selected to fit
your risk cover needs.

Only one admin charge of R15 p.m. is levied where
the whole family is covered under a single policy. This
enhances the cost-effectiveness of the cover.

8. Long-term premium guarantees give peace of mind in
the future.

6. It provides an optional, cost-effective and transparent
savings benefit to protect your risk benefits in the event
of temporary financial difficulties.

Occupation categories
Different occupations have different risks associated with them. It is important that these are taken into account in determining
the premium to charge for any Loss of Income Protection benefits. As an example, the loading for an accountant will be
lower than that of a plumber, reflecting the different risks associated with these two occupations.
It is important that you inform Liberty of any change in occupation following the issuing of a policy.

Loans and surrenders
Loans and surrenders are only allowed on the Financial Protector – please refer to the benefits section for more details on
this benefit. The remaining Lifestyle Protector benefits are pure risk benefits, with no loans or surrenders available as no cash
value element exists.

GENERIC STRUCTURE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Term Benefits

Extending the term of the benefits

Term benefits are designed to meet your specific needs
more closely. Many clients consider the need for risk cover
when they buy a new property, take out a loan or have a
child. While Whole of life cover is preferred by some, mostly
because of the long term security of protection it provides,
other clients may simply wish to have cover in place for a
limited term (to match their financial commitments).
Benefits with a fixed term can only be chosen on a separate
Lifestyle Protector (Term) policy. Benefits with a Whole of
Life cover period or fixed cease ages cannot be selected on
a Lifestyle Protector (Term) policy. All the benefits chosen
on a Lifestyle Protector (Term) policy will have the same
term.

Outside of the conversion option, the term of the policy can
also be extended at any time prior to the last three months
of the policy (through a servicing change request) subject to:
• The revised outstanding term being at least 5 years
following extension

Term benefits will only be available on the Level Premium
Pattern.
Conversion options
Conversion options will be offered on term benefits at the
end of the policy or on certain life events. The conversion
option will allow the clients to exercise it on the following
specified events within 3 months:
• Following you getting married;
•

Following you becoming a parent;

•

Following you or your spouse purchasing a new home; or

•

Prior to the policy end date.

The conversion option allows the cover period to be
extended at a benefit level on:
•

The existing policy
−

•

Term extension where the revised term is between
5 and 25 years

A new policy
−

­Term extension to Whole of Life cover or to end of
the month of the Life Assured’s: 60th, 65th or 70th
birthday, as per current benefit rules

−

­Term extension where the term is between 5 and
25 years

Note that the conversion option does not allow for the
combined sum assured on the original policy and converted
policy to exceed the sum assured on the original policy
before conversion.
Note that once the full sum assured of conversion option is
exercised, the original policy must cease.

•

Underwriting

•

New business rates applicable at the time of term
extension

•

Business rules (as described in the sections above)
EXAMPLE 1

A Lifestyle Protector policy is structured with
R1 000 000 Life Cover (Term) benefit with a 10-year
term. Three years later, the policyholder requests to
increase the term of the policy by another 3 years. There
are 10 years still remaining following the extension and
thus the extension can go ahead. The policyholder will be
subject to further underwriting. The policyholder will be
charged a new premium based on his age next birthday
at the date of conversion and the applicable 10-year rates
at the time of conversion.

EXAMPLE 2

A Lifestyle Protector policy is structured with
R1 000 000 Life Cover (Term) benefit with a 10-year
term. Eight years later, the policyholder requests to
increase the term of the policy by another 2 years. The
extension cannot be allowed because the remaining
term after the extension would be 4 years.
For more details on the conversion option and term
extension, please refer to your policy document.

GENERIC STRUCTURE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Term with PayOut feature
You have the option to choose the PayOut feature on
policies with term benefits with a minimum cover period
of seven years. This option is only available to natural
policyholders and must be selected at policy inception. A
lump sum cash amount equal to a percentage of the nominal
value of all premiums received on qualifying benefits will be
paid at the policy end date.
To receive the PayOut amount, at least one term benefit
needs to remain in force at the end of the policy term with
no outstanding premiums at this date. The PayOut amount
will be paid at the end of the policy term even if there has
been a non-death related claim on one of the benefits on
the policy and the policy has not ceased. Therefore, if a claim
is made on certain benefits which may reduce the cover
(and thus premiums) for the benefits, the PayOut would
still pay out on the expiry of the policy term, provided that
all premiums are paid to date and the policy remains in force
until the term expiry date of the policy.
Liberty reserves the right to adjust the percentage of the
nominal value of future premiums that will be allocated
towards the PayOut lump sum cash amount at the premium
review dates.
EXAMPLE

A Lifestyle Protector policy with a cover period of 7 years
is structured with R1 000 000 Life Cover (Term) benefit
with PayOut and R1 000 000 Absolute Protector
(Term) benefit with PayOut. Say the total premium
on the policy is R500 per month for the entire policy
duration. After 5 years, the life assured claims for
an event which qualifies for a 50% payment under
Absolute Protector.
R700 000 is paid to the policyholder. Therefore, there is
cover remaining of R700 000 Life Cover (Term) and
R700 000 Absolute Protector (Term). The premium
for the policy reduces to R250 per month for the
remaining 2 years of the policy term. After a further
2 years, i.e. at the end of the 7-year policy term, the
following will be paid to the policyholder as a PayOut
amount:
R500 x 12 x 5 + R250 x 12 x2 = R36 000.
If the term of the policy has been extended, the payment
of PayOut amount will only occur at the end of the revised
policy term. It is possible to cancel the PayOut feature at any
stage during the policy but no PayOut will be payable. This
will happen through a servicing change request. When the
PayOut feature is deselected, it cannot be added back at
later time.
The PayOut feature will end and not become payable:
• If there are any contractual premiums outstanding on
the Lifestyle Protector policy at the end date;
•

If the Lifestyle Protector policy lapses before the end

date or the applicable term benefit has lapsed before
the end date of the Lifestyle Protector policy;
•

If the Lifestyle Protector policy is ceded absolutely or
collaterally;

•

If the policyholder exercises full conversion to a policy
with a Whole of Life cover period prior to the last 3
months of the policy end date; or

•

On the death of the policyholder or life assured prior to
the Lifestyle Protector policy reaching the end date
specified in the policy summary.

The policyholder must cancel the PayOut feature on the
policy and hence forfeit the PayOut lump sum cash amount:
• If the Sums Assured on policies with the PayOut feature
are reduced; or
•

If the benefit cover periods on policies with the
PayOutfeature are reduced.

The PayOut lump sum cash amount will however not be
forfeited if the reduction in sum assured is due to the partial
conversion of a benefit under this policy using its conversion
option prior to the policy end date.

The Professional at Liberty
Liberty recognises that professional people earn recognition.
An exclusive package known as “The Professional at Liberty”
is available for professional people. This package provides
more generous benefits to suit the unique needs of a
professional.
To be eligible for this package you must be a professional
and practice fulltime in your profession. Spouses who
are not classified as professionals may be added onto the
Professional policy of their spouse.
The following benefits are available under “The Professional
at Liberty” package:
•

A 7-day backdated waiting period is available on
the Income Protector benefit for self-employed
professionals. You will receive money for the period
starting on the first day ofthe life assured being
temporarily or permanently occupation disabled or
impaired, after a waiting period of seven days has
expired. This is extremely valuable for professionals who
will receive no income from the first day of disability.

•

Professionals qualify for a 10% premium discount on
Income Protector benefits.

•

Certain Professionals will qualify for an “Own occupation”
lump sum disability benefit at the cost of the “Own
or any reasonable occupation” lump sum disability
definition.

GENERIC STRUCTURE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Risk Revealer

How do the discounts work?

What is Risk Revealer?

The discounts are calculated based on the Risk Revealer
category to which you’ve been assigned, which is in turn,
determined by the answers you submit.

Risk Revealer is a web-based tool that allows you to
calculate your particular level of lifestyle and accidental risk.
You are asked to complete a series of questions that centre
around your lifestyle, family life and the travel choices you
make every day.

•

Benefit

•

Premium pattern

What do I get out of it?

•

Age of the policy

There are two main benefits to you:
1.

Better understanding of your lifestyle and
accidental risk
Risk Revealer is meant to help demystify your levels
of lifestyle and accidental risk. This is done through
providing you with a personalised dial showing your
level of risk on a risk scale and through the use of a
personalised avatar.
As questions are answered, your avatar starts taking on
the details that are provided, for example age, gender
and your BMI. Once completed, you will see your avatar,
personalised further to include other lifestyle factors
such as your exercise details, the transport you use, etc.
To make it even more interactive, you will even see your
risk relative to the average risk of everyone else that has
used Risk Revealer.

2. Premium discounts
Provided that your particular level of lifestyle and
accidental risk is low enough, you may be provided with
a premium rate discount for an initial period of time on
certain qualifying benefits on the purchase of a new
Lifestyle Protector policy.
How does Risk Revealer work?
Based on the answers you submit, a Risk Revealer report
and report number will be generated and you will be assigned
a Risk Revealer category based on your level of risk.
This Risk Revealer report number can then be used as an
input to a new Lifestyle Protector quotation to determine
your levels of discounts for the various benefits that you
have selected. As such, your answers are considered material
information and inaccurate information may be treated as
misrepresentation and/or non-disclosure that may affect
benefits and premiums. So it is important to answer all
questions honestly and accurately.
The Risk Revealer category will not change over time. The
category issued at inception will be applied for the life of
the policy.

The discounts vary by:

Benefit
Risk Revealer focuses on lifestyle and accidental risks,
which affect the premium on certain benefits more than
others. As a result of this, certain benefits do not qualify for
a discount, and benefits that do qualify for a discount have
differing discount levels applicable to them. Risk Revealer is
subject to all underwriting requirements being met.
Premium pattern
Currently, Risk Revealer is only available on the Age Rated,
Level and Fixed Increasing Premium Patterns.
Age of the policy
The Risk Revealer discounts reduce over time. Currently,
this is over a 3 year period. As such, the Risk Revealer
discount will be 0% from the 4th year of the policy.
How do loadings and exclusions affect Risk Revealer
discounts?
Risk Revealer discounts do not apply if loadings or
exclusions apply and Risk Revealer may be excluded on
your policy if the information disclosed as part of Risk
Revealer is not consistent with the information disclosed as
part of your policy application.

GENERIC STRUCTURE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Do I get a Risk Revealer discount on benefits that I add
after policy inception?

Offer of Additional Cover (OAC)

If benefits are added onto a policy that already has a Risk
Revealer discount, the original Risk Revealer category
for that life assured, determined at policy inception, will be
applied. Furthermore, the level of discount applied to that
benefit will be based on the reduced Risk Revealer discount
based on the age of the policy.

What is Offer of Additional Cover?

Can my Risk Revealer report support multiple policies?
You may use your Risk Revealer report to support more
than one policy. However, the Risk Revealer report must
be less than six months old to use it on a new Lifestyle
Protector policy.
Can I get a Risk Revealer discount on an existing policy?
No. Risk Revealer cannot be added to existing policies or
policies that are replacements of existing Liberty policies.
Where can I find Risk Revealer?
You can find out your level of lifestyle and accidental risk and
see if you qualify for a discount on a new Lifestyle Protector
policy by visiting www.LibertyRiskRevealer.co.za.

Offer of Additional Cover gives you the hassle-free
opportunity to increase your risk cover on certain lump sum
benefits within 24 months of being fully underwritten – free
of medical underwriting other than completing a simple
declaration of health confirming that there have been no
changes to your health since your policy was issued.
Why would I want to increase my cover?
The main benefits for qualifying clients are:
1. Your financial needs may have changed
OAC allows you to take out additional cover on a like-forlike basis without much hassle, especially if your financial
needs have changed – for example, due to a life-changing
event such as getting married, becoming a parent,
purchasing a home or increasing your share in a business.
2. Changes in what you can afford
You may have initially purchased lower levels of cover
than required at the time you took out your policy due to
affordability but now you are in a better financial position
to increase your cover.
How do I qualify for OAC?
Based on the medical information that Liberty receives as
part of the application, Liberty’s underwriting department
will decide whether the life assured qualifies for OAC. If the
life assured qualifies, the OAC option will automatically be
added and this will be communicated.
OAC is not offered on term policies.

GENERIC STRUCTURE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Do I need to pay anything extra for OAC?

What other OAC rules do I need to be aware of?

No, the OAC option will be granted to you free of charge
if you qualify. Should you exercise the option to purchase
additional benefits, you will pay additional premiums at that
stage for the additional cover purchased.

Occupational, avocational and financial underwriting still
applies when you exercise your OAC option. Liberty’s then
applicable premium rules, benefit maximum limits and
business rules apply when exercising your OAC option. No
internal replacements (i.e. the replacement of an existing
cover with new cover) will be allowed when exercising the
offer.

How much extra cover can I buy through OAC?
You can buy up to 100% of the original cover amount of
life cover benefits and certain lump sum benefits that you
originally purchased.
However, the total amount of additional cover that you can
purchase through the option, aggregated across all nonaccelerated benefits, is R5 million.
How do I exercise my OAC option?
The OAC option must be exercised within 24 months of the
policy being issued. If you have qualified for OAC, you can
exercise anywhere between 1% and the maximum amount
of additional cover offered. Once you have exercised the OAC
option, it will cease (even if you do not take up the maximum
amount of additional cover available through the option).
You must be 55 years or younger (age next birthday) at the
date that you exercise your OAC option.
On exercising the OAC option, you will be required to sign
a declaration that your health has not changed since the
inception of the policy. However, you will not be sent for
further medical underwriting.
You may exercise your OAC option either by:
•

Increasing the cover amount on your existing policy; or

•

Taking out a new policy.

EXAMPLE

Consider Zinah, a 26 year old who purchased a
Lifestyle Protector with R1 000 000 Life Cover and
R1 000 000 Living Lifestyle. At application stage, she
qualified for and was granted the OAC option. Seventeen
months later, Zinah has just had a new-born baby boy
and thus went through a life-changing event. Zinah has
the option through OAC to increase her Life Cover by
an additional R1 000 000 as well as her Living Lifestyle
by an additional R1 000 000. She may also choose to
take out this additional cover on a new policy, rather than
increasing the cover amounts on her existing policy.

EXAMPLE

Consider Samuel, a 44 year old who purchased
R4 000 000 Life Cover and R2 000 000 Absolute
Protector Plus. At application stage, he qualified for
and was granted the OAC option. Now ten months later,
Samuel wants to increase his share in a business and
needs additional cover as part of the change in business
interests. Samuel can exercise his total OAC option to
the maximum of R5 000 000 by increasing cover on his
existing policy or taking out a new policy.
As an example, Samuel can exercise his OAC option as
follows:
Increase cover on existing policy
•

OAC option of R4 000 000 Life Cover

•

OAC option of R1 000 000 Absolute Protector
Plus

Or
Take out additional cover as a new policy
•

OAC option of R3 000 000 Life Cover

•

OAC option of R2 000 000 Absolute Protector
Plus

Other combinations are also possible, provided that the
total increased cover is less than R5 000 000.

PREMIUMS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

3. PREMIUMS
Not only does Lifestyle Protector offer you flexibility as to which benefits you select, it also
provides you flexibility in the way that you pay for your cover.
You may prefer to pay a fixed premium for the duration
of your cover. Alternatively, you may wish to pay a lower
premium initially that will increase in future. You also have
the choice of whether you want to pay the premium every
month or every year.
Lifestyle Protector offers you pure risk benefits such as
Life Cover and Capital Disability. The premium for the pure
risk cover is based on the cost of these benefits. You are able
to add a savings benefit, the Financial Protector, to the
pure risk benefits and enjoy the advantage of having a single
policy with a savings component and a pure risk component.
The premiums for the Financial Protector work in a slightly
different manner and have different limits compared to the
premiums for the pure risk benefits – please refer to the
Financial Protector section for details of these differences.

Premium pattern
All your benefits are provided for under a single policy.
Premium patterns are therefore chosen at policy level.
This means that you select a premium pattern for all your
family’s benefits at the start of your policy. If your Second
Life Assured is covered under the same policy, the same
premium pattern applies to his/her benefits. Premium
Patterns available are:
•

Level

•

Renewable - Closed to New Business July 2022

•

Age Rated

•

Fixed Increasing

PREMIUM

Payment method
You can choose to pay your premiums by:
•
•

Debit order, or
Stop order.

Premium frequency
You have the following options:
•

Monthly

•

Annually

Premium frequencies are selected at policy level.

The minimum monthly and annual premium for your risk
benefits and admin charge is shown below.

•

The Financial Protector has its own premium
requirements. Its premium is not included in the

The appreciation of the US Dollar against the Rand is

The CPI increase percentage will be calculated for the
12-month period ending 3 months prior to the policy
anniversary.

EXAMPLE

Mr Smith, aged 40, purchases a Living Lifestyle Plus
benefit with a sum assured of R1 000 000, increasing
annually with inflation (as measured by CPI). His initial
premium is R249 p.m.

At the next policy anniversary CPI was 25% p.a. over the
period. The maximum benefit increase allowed is 20% p.a.
and his cover is increased to R1 260 000 (R 1 050 000 x
(1+ 20%)). His premium then increases to R323.56 p.m.

At the following policy anniversary CPI was 3.5% over
the period. The minimum benefit increase is 5% p.a., and
his cover increases to R1 050 000. Mr Smith’s premium
increases to R262.45 p.m. to account for the increased
benefit amount.

On the following policy anniversary, CPI was 10% over
the period. This is between the minimum and maximum
increases allowed and his cover increases to R1 386 000
(R1 260 000 x (1 + 10%)). His monthly premiums increase
to R363.35.

PREMIUMS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Policies with small premiums
If the premium for all your risk benefits is below the specified level, you pay more per unit of cover for your risk benefits than
when the premium for your risk benefits is above this specified level. The addition to or removal of risk benefits from your
policy, or the expiry of risk benefits, may lead to your premium falling above or below this level. This may lead to different
premiums for the same benefit before and after the change.
The same applies if the premium for your Second Life Assured’s risk benefits is above or below the specified level.
EXAMPLE

Consider Mrs Pillay, a 35-year old who purchased a Lifestyle Protector policy with R1 000 000 of Life Cover and
Absolute Protector. She selects the Level Premium Pattern for her policy. Her total premium is R260 per month. The
following premiums are quoted:
BENEFIT

PREMIUM

Life Cover

R130 p.m.

Absolute Protector

R115 p.m.

Admin charge

R15 p.m.

Five years later she adds Living Lifestyle with a sum assured of R700 000 to her policy. Her total premium is now R400
per month. The following premiums are quoted:
BENEFIT

PREMIUM

Life Cover

R125 p.m.

Absolute Protector

R110 p.m.

Living Lifestyle

R150 p.m.

Admin charge

R15 p.m.

The changes in the premiums quoted for the Life Cover and Absolute Protector are only as a result of the larger total
premium per month resulting from the addition of Living Lifestyle. This results in a removal of the small case loading
levied previously.

PREMIUMS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Discounts are also given if the total premium for certain core risk benefits is above a specified level. This can again lead to
different risk premiums for certain benefits before and after the addition or removal of benefits to your policy, or the expiry of
benefits.

EXAMPLE

Consider Mr Mokoena, a 50-year old male. He has a Lifestyle Protector with Life Cover, Living Lifestyle and Capital
Disability Plus selected. His total monthly premium is R3 395 per month. The following premiums are quoted:
BENEFIT

PREMIUM

Life Cover

R930 p.m.

R500 p.m.

Capital Disability Plus

R1 950 p.m.

R15 p.m.

premiums are now quoted:
BENEFIT

PREMIUM

Life Cover

R1 000 p.m.

Living Lifestyle

R500 p.m.

Admin charge

R15 p.m.

The changes in the premiums charged for the Life Cover increase is because the large case discount which is applied
reduces as a result of the removal of the Capital Disability Plus benefit. The Living Lifestyle premium does not change
because it is not a core risk protection benefit and is therefore not affected by large case discounts.

PREMIUMS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

PREMIUM REVIEW

Premium Review and Guarantee Period
Each Lifestyle Protector policy starts with an initial policy
premium. Future premiums will follow the selected
Premium Pattern and are guaranteed for a set period,
known as the Premium Guarantee Period. During this
period, premiums will not be subject to a Premium Review
and will be guaranteed to change only in line with the
chosen Premium Pattern.
At the end of each Premium Guarantee Period, a Premium
Review takes place. Any necessary adjustments will apply from
this point until the next scheduled Premium Review Date.
How does a Premium Review work for Benefits with an
initial 10-year guarantee period
Most benefits have an initial 10-year premium guarantee,
meaning that for the first 10 years, your premiums will
remain unchanged except for:
•
•

Automatic premium increases based on the chosen
premium pattern and ABI (Annual Benefit Increases), or
Changes to your cover made during the policy term.

Some low risk lives will also qualify for a 25% cap on their first
premium guarantee review. This means that premiums will
not increase by more than 25% from the premium guarantee
review. This will be clearly outlined in the policy document
provided to the client.
After the first 10 years, premiums will be reviewed annually.
Example:
•
•

Policy Start Date: January 1, 2020.
First Premium Review: January 1, 2030
− Initial 10-year guarantee period applies until January
1, 2030. Thereafter, policy is annually reviewable.

•
−

Second Premium Review: January 1, 2031
Benefits are reviewed annually.

How Does Liberty Review Premiums?
Premium rates will be reviewed at specified premium review
dates.
At premium review dates, Liberty will investigate to what
extent the pricing assumptions used to calculate the
premiums varied from the actual experience. If there is a
variation between the expected and actual experience,
or a change in Liberty’s expectation of future experience,
Liberty may adjust the future premiums, as well as the future
automatic premium increases that will apply in respect of any
automatic benefit increases. The pricing assumptions include
but are not limited to the expected:
•
•
•
•
•

Expenses in administrating the policies,
The level of lapses over the lifetime of policies in general,
Investment returns on the assets backing the policies,
Claims experience for similar policies and industry claims
experience; and
The average age and demographics of the lives assured
on the product as a whole.

If your premiums increase at a Premium Review Date, Liberty
will provide alternative options for you to choose from, for
example benefits can be reduced to an appropriate level to
continue providing valuable cover.
Note: Changes to your cover or premium pattern after the
policy start date may affect your premium and guarantee
review periods.

PREMIUMS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

PREMIUM PATTERNS

Level Premium Pattern
What is the Level Premium Pattern?
Under the Level Premium Pattern, premiums are
calculated to sustain the benefits for the full cover period.
The premiums are calculated to remain level for a selected
amount of cover for the cover period.
How do benefit increases affect the Level Premium
Pattern?
Any automatic benefit increases selected will result in the
amount of cover for a benefit increasing each year. These
increases will be accompanied by associated automatic
premium increases to allow for the increase in cover.
How do premium guarantees affect the Level Premium
Pattern?
For all benefits, apart from the exceptions listed, the
premiums quoted at inception of the policy are guaranteed
to remain unchanged (apart from the automatic premium
increases and unless the selected cover changes) for the first
ten years until the first premium review date.
In the event where the term selected is less than 10 years,
the premium guarantee of 10 years will still apply from the
start date of the policy in the event of a term extension.
At the premium review date, Liberty will investigate to
what extent the pricing assumptions used to calculate the
premiums varied from the actual experience. If there is a
variation between the expected and actual experience, or a
change in Liberty’s expectation of future experience, Liberty
may adjust the future premiums and may adjust the future
automatic premium increases.

In addition, based on your risk profile, we may offer you
an additional premium guarantee as follows:
At the first premium review date, the revised premiums per
R 10 000 of cover applicable to each age and until the next
premium review date will not increase by more than 25%,
from those premiums quoted at inception of the policy. The
25% limit on the increase of the premiums that applies at
the first premium review date does not apply at subsequent
premium review dates. Please consult your quotation and/
or policy document to determine if you qualify for the
additional guarantee.
At the first 10-year premium review date, Liberty will set a
revised premium review date annually.
Should your premium increase and you do not accept
the increased premiums, options will be provided to you
by Liberty. For example, benefits can be reduced to an
appropriate level to continue providing cover.
How do premium guarantees affect the Age Rated
Premium Pattern?
Under the Age Rated Premium Pattern the premium, per
R 10 000 of cover, at each age is guaranteed to remain
unchanged from that quoted at inception of the policy
(unless the selected cover changes or cover increases due
to an automatic benefit increase (ABI)) for the first ten years,
until the first premium review date.

PREMIUMS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

EXAMPLE

Consider Mr Smith, a 40-year-old male who is a nonsmoker and selects a R1 000 000 Life Cover benefit and
no ABIs. He chooses the Level Premium Pattern.

Due to his risk profile, Mr Smith qualifies for the additional
premium guarantee. As a result, Liberty guarantees that
his premium will not exceed R365 per month when the
price is reviewed at the first premium review date (for the
same sum assured).

Mr Smith’s initial premium is R292 per month. This
premium is guaranteed for the first 10 years and is
expected to be sufficient for the remainder of his policy.

PREMIUMS | OCTOBER 2025

Exceptions
Different premium patterns and guarantees to those
described above will apply to the following benefits:
Renewable Life Cover benefit - Closed to New Business
July 2022
When the Renewable Life Cover benefit is selected on
the Level Premium Pattern, the Renewable Premium
Pattern will apply to this benefit. Please see the Renewable
Premium Pattern section for more details.
EduCator benefit
The EduCator benefit premium is increased every year on
the policy anniversary in line with the Education Inflation
Rate as determined by Liberty. The premium for this benefit
is not guaranteed and Liberty reserves the right to increase
premiums each year at a rate in excess of the Education
Inflation Rate increase. If the revised premiums are not
accepted, the EduCator benefit will cease.
Retrenchment Protector and Premium Protector –
Retrenchment benefits
Premiums for these benefits are not guaranteed, and Liberty
reserves the right to increase premiums for these benefits
each year. Should your premium increase and you do not
accept the increased premiums, options will be provided to
you by Liberty. For example, benefits can be reduced to an
appropriate level to continue providing cover.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Income Protector, Impairment Income, Overhead
Expenses Protector, Bonus Cover, Guaranteed
Dependant Protector, Living Lifestyle Protector and
Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector
The premiums quoted at inception of the policy are
guaranteed to remain unchanged, apart from the automatic
premium increases and unless the selected cover changes, for
the first five years following the start date for these benefits.
At the first premium review date, the premiums payable will
be reviewed, and the new premiums will be guaranteed for
a further five years. At each subsequent premium review
date, the premiums payable will be revised and the new
premiums will be guaranteed for a further one year until the
next premium review date, which will be set annually. At
premium review dates, Liberty will investigate to what extent
the pricing assumptions used to calculate the premiums
varied from the actual experience. If there is a variation
between the expected and actual experience, or a change
in Liberty’s expectation of future experience, Liberty may
adjust the future premiums and future automatic premium
increases. Should your premium increase and you do not
accept the increased premiums, options will be provided to
you by Liberty. For example, benefits can be reduced to an
appropriate level to continue providing cover.
Medical Premium Protector benefit - closed to new
business March 2024
The Medical Premium Protector premiums will be
adjusted every year on the policy anniversary to allow for the
medical scheme contribution inflation rate as determined
by Liberty and any automatic premium increases associated
with the premium pattern selected.

PREMIUMS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Age Rated Premium Pattern
What is the Age Rated Premium Pattern?
Under the Age Rated Premium Pattern, the premiums are
based on the age of the Life Assured and the sum assured
each year. The premium in any particular year is therefore
calculated based on the level of risk and level of cover in
that particular year. The risk of a claim occurring typically
increases with age, and the Age Rated premiums typically
increase each year to cover this increased risk, even if the
amount of cover remains unchanged.
The Age Rated Premium Pattern applies to the premium
for all benefits except for those listed as exceptions.

How do benefit increases affect the Age Rated Premium
Pattern?
Any automatic benefit increases selected will result in the
amount of cover for a benefit increasing each year. These
increases will be accompanied by associated automatic
premium increases to allow for the increase in cover.
Automatic premium increases in respect of automatic
benefit increases apply over and above the age-associated
premium increases that would otherwise have applied even
if no automatic benefit increases had been selected.

EXAMPLE

Consider Mr Jones, a 50-year-old non-smoker. He selects Life Cover for R1 000 000 on the Age Rated Premium Pattern.

Retrenchment Protector and Retrenchment Premium
Protector benefits
Premiums for these benefits are not guaranteed, and Liberty
reserves the right to increase premiums for these benefits
each year. Should your premium increase and you do not
accept the increased premiums, options will be provided to
you by Liberty. For example, benefits can be reduced to an
appropriate level to continue providing cover.
Income Protector, Impairment Income, Overhead
Expenses Protector, Bonus Cover, Guaranteed
Dependant Protector, Living Lifestyle Protector and
Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector
The premiums quoted at inception of the policy are
guaranteed to remain unchanged, apart from the automatic
premium increases and unless the selected cover changes, for
the first five years following the start date for these benefits.
At the first premium review date, the premiums payable will
be reviewed, and the new premiums will be guaranteed for a
further five years. At each subsequent premium review date,
the premiums payable will be revised and the new premiums
will be guaranteed for a further one year until the next
premium review date, which will be set annually. At premium
review dates, Liberty will investigate to what extent the
pricing assumptions used to calculate the premiums varied
from the actual experience. If there is a variation between

the expected and actual experience, or a change in Liberty’s
expectation of future experience, Liberty may adjust the
future premiums and future automatic premium increases.
Should your premium increase and you do not accept
the increased premiums, options will be provided to you
by Liberty. For example, benefits can be reduced to an
appropriate level to continue providing cover.
Medical Premium Protector benefit - closed to new
business March 2024
The Medical Premium Protector premiums will be
adjusted every year on the policy anniversary to allow for the
medical scheme contribution inflation rate as determined
by Liberty and any automatic premium increases associated
with the premium pattern selected.

Renewable Premium Pattern - Closed to
New Business July 2022
What is the Renewable Premium Pattern?
Under the Renewable Premium Pattern, the premiums
are calculated to remain level for the first 15 years for the
selected level of cover. After the first 15 years and every five
years thereafter, the premiums are increased for the same
level of cover.
The Renewable Premium Pattern applies to the premium
for all benefits except for those listed as exceptions below.
How do benefit increases affect the Renewable
Premium Pattern?
Any automatic benefit increases selected will result in the
level of cover for a benefit increasing each year. These
increases will be accompanied by associated automatic
premium increases to allow for the increase in cover.

PREMIUMS | OCTOBER 2025

How do premium reviews work under the Renewable
Premium Pattern?
At premium review dates, Liberty will review the premium
payable. The revised premium quoted will be based on the
Life Assured’s age and sum assured at the premium review
date. The revised premiums will be set to be sufficient to
maintain the level of cover for a further five years and will
typically be materially higher than the premiums prior to the
premium review date.
At premium review dates Liberty may also change any fixed
relationship between future automatic premium increases
and automatic benefit increases that may exist based on its
view of the cost of such relationship at that time.
At each premium review date Liberty will set a revised
premium review date in a further five years’ time, and will
guarantee the revised premiums to the next premium
review date.
Should your premium increase and you do not accept
the increased premiums, options will be provided to you
by Liberty. For example, benefits can be reduced to an
appropriate level to continue providing cover.
Exceptions
Different premium patterns and guarantees to those
described above will apply to the following benefits:
EduCator benefit

Fixed Increasing Premium Pattern

The EduCator benefit premium is increased every year on
the policy anniversary in line with the Education Inflation
Rate as determined by Liberty. The premium for this benefit
is not guaranteed and Liberty reserves the right to increase
premiums each year at a rate in excess of the Education
Inflation Rate increase. If the policyholder does not accept
the revised premium, the EduCator benefit will cease.

What is the Fixed Increasing Premium Pattern?

Retrenchment Protector and Premium Protector –
Retrenchment benefits

How do benefit increases affect the Fixed Increasing
Premium Pattern?

Premiums for these benefits are not guaranteed, and Liberty
reserves the right to increase premiums for these benefits
each year. Should your premium increase and you do not
accept the increased premiums, options will be provided to
you by Liberty. For example, benefits can be reduced to an
appropriate level to continue providing cover.

Any automatic benefit increases selected will result in the
amount of cover for a benefit increasing each year. These
increases will be accompanied by associated automatic
premium increases to allow for the increase in cover.
Automatic premium increases in respect of automatic
benefit increases apply over and above the fixed percentage
premium increases that would otherwise have applied even
if no automatic benefit increases had been selected.

Under the Fixed Increasing Premium Pattern, premiums
are calculated to sustain the benefits for the full cover period.
The premiums are calculated to increase, at each policy
anniversary, by a fixed percentage rate for a selected level
amount of cover for the cover period.

Is the premium increase always equal to the fixed
increase selected?
No, even if you have no automatic benefit increase your
overall premium increase might be different to the fixed
increase selected for a range of reasons. For example, the
effect of small case loadings, large case discounts, any other
discounts that apply to your policy, or because certain
benefits on your policy do not follow the Fixed Increasing
Premium Pattern (see the exceptions section for more
detail) may mean that your premium increase might be
slightly different to the general fixed increase selected on
your policy.
How do premium guarantees affect the Fixed Increasing
Premium Pattern?
For all benefits, apart from the exceptions listed, the
premiums quoted at inception of the policy are guaranteed
to remain unchanged, unless the selected cover changes, for
the first ten years following the start date of the policy until
the first premium review date.
In addition, based on your risk profile, we may offer you
an additional premium guarantee as follows

At the first premium review date, the revised premiums per
R 10 000 of cover applicable to each age and until the next
premium review date will not increase by more than 25%,
from those premiums quoted at inception of the policy. The
25% limit on the increase of the premiums that applies at
the first premium review date does not apply at subsequent
premium review dates. Please consult your quotation and/
or policy document to determine if you qualify for the
additional guarantee.
At the first 10-year premium review date, Liberty will set a
revised premium review date annually. Should your premium
increase and you do not accept the increased premiums,
options will be provided to you by Liberty. For example,
benefits can be reduced to an appropriate level to continue
providing cover.

Premium pattern switching is currently permitted. Apart
from changes in your current and future premiums, such a
change may:
•

Result in changes in the general terms and conditions of
your policy, including how and when premiums can be
reviewed;

•

Result in the latest terms and conditions (and therefore
cover provided) for certain benefits replacing your
existing terms and conditions;

•

Require you to change the benefits you have on your
policy as not all benefits are available on all premium
patterns; and

•

Be subject to underwriting.

The above reflects Liberty’s current business rules on
premium pattern switches. These are not guaranteed and
your ability to switch premium patterns and the effect of
a premium pattern switch on your policy will depend on
Liberty’s business rules at the time.

What is the difference between the
different premium patterns?
The initial premium and the progression of that initial premium
are the main differences between the premium patterns.
The Level Premium Pattern’s initial premium is higher than
that of the Age Rated Premium Pattern, Fixed Increasing
Premium Pattern and the Renewable Premium Pattern(1).
The Age Rated and Fixed Increasing premiums will
however increase as you age and will eventually exceed the
Level premium.
The Renewable Premium(1) will increase after 15 years and
at this stage will most likely exceed the Level, Age Rated
and Fixed Increasing Premium Pattern. In the next 5 years
thereafter it is possible that as you age the Age Rated and
Fixed Increasing premium increases and could exceed the
Renewable Premium Pattern(1) again.
Another important difference is the premium guarantees
that apply to the premium patterns.

premium patterns.

Guaranteed Insurability Benefit
Guaranteed Insurability Benefits allow the Life Assured to
take out/amend existing cover with relaxed underwriting
requirements, and are applicable to the following events/
benefits:
•

Future Protector

•

Divorce and death on Lifestyle Protector

•

EduCator

•

Future Income Assessment: Income Protector and
Retrenchment Protector

•

Medical Premium Protector - closed to new
business March 2024r

Please see the individual sections for more detail.
Divorce
Where the spouse and the Life Assured become legally
divorced, or permanently separated, a spouse covered
under the Lifestyle Protector policy will have the option of
taking out a separate policy, subject to some extra ratings
and limitations as on the existing policy, where they will be
the policyholder. There will be no underwriting necessary
at that stage, unless there are changes to benefits. The
spouse’s policy will be calculated based on the spouse’s age
and personal details at the date of exercising this option.
Premium guarantees will then apply from the date that this
policy is issued. Your policy will then continue with your
own benefits. The minimum premium requirement for the
spouse’s new policy will be waived.
Once the GIB option has been exercised by the spouse, a
new policy will be issued in their name. The spouse will then
be removed from the Life Assured’s policy on which the GIB
option has been exercised.
Death
Should the First Life Assured die, the policy may
automatically continue in the Spouse Life Assured’s name,

but only if there is a spouse insured under this policy, under
the existing terms and conditions without evidence of
insurability. The policy will be subject to the same loadings
and limitations that already applied to the spouse on the
existing policy.

When does cover commence?
The policyholder will have full cover once the underwriting
terms granted by the Underwriting Department have been
accepted and the commencement date of the policy is
within 30 days of that date.
Where Life Cover or Renewable Life Cover (closed to New
Business July 2022) has been selected, the policyholder will
be covered for up to R400 000 (allowing for any existing
cover) in Immediate Life Cover. Should a claim arise, the
terms and conditions of the Immediate Life Cover will apply.
The cover will be available for the lesser of 30 days and the
period until the Underwriting Department has issued the
terms of the contract. This is subject to following additional
conditions:
•

The applicant must be younger than 60

•

Death which is directly or indirectly a result of a
condition which existed prior to the application will not
be covered

•

Death as a result of suicide will not be covered

•

All questions in the application form must have been
correctly and completely answered

•

No application for cover (or increase in cover) has ever
been declined, deferred or accepted on special terms or
conditions for the applicant by any insurer

•

The applicant does not engage or intend engaging in
hazardous activities, including occupations or pursuits.

•

The cover will only be valid if the first premium would
have been honoured had the applicant not died. The
first premium will be deducted from the benefits paid.

BENEFITS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

4. BENEFITS
The Four Pillars of Protection
There are four main areas of financial risk that you and your family are likely to face as you deal
with the changes, the unexpected events and the unavoidable eventualities that we are all
confronted with as we go through life.
These areas of risk are:
•

•

•

•

The risk of dying and the financial impact this event will
have on your surviving family, sometimes starting with
the onset of an expensive terminal illness and extending
to the inevitable costs of family funerals.
The risk of losing your income through some form of
disability or impairment that prevents you from being
able to earn a living.

To address these very different needs, Liberty has designed
the Lifestyle Protector policy around the four pillars of risk
protection:
1.

Life Protection

2.

Loss of Income Protection

3.

Lifestyle Protection

4.

Policy Protection

The risk of being burdened with the costs of surviving
and recuperating from some form of critical illness or
unexpected traumatic event.

Core Risk Protection Benefits

The risk of losing or being denied access to appropriate
levels of cover in the future, either because of a
temporary inability to make the premium payments
for some reason, or because your health condition may
have deteriorated.

Core risk protection benefits are benefits that form the basis
of Lifestyle Protector. Lifestyle Protector requires
that at least one core risk protection benefit be selected by
each life assured. The diagram below shows the core risk
protection benefits.
The structure of Lifestyle Protector means that you need
not select Life Cover under the policy. You only need to
select at least one core risk benefit. The amount of your
cover under these core risk protection benefits may exceed
the amount of any life cover selected. This provides you
with ultimate flexibility to structure your risk benefits to your
individual needs.

Accelerated Benefits versus
Non-Accelerated Benefits
Accelerated Benefits
Both accelerated and non-accelerated disability and critical
illness benefits are available under Lifestyle Protector.
It is important to understand the difference between an
accelerated and a non-accelerated benefit in order to
determine which is most appropriate for your needs. Any
claim under an accelerated benefit reduces the total of your
Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1) under the policy
by the amount of the claim. Accelerated benefits cost less
than their equivalent non-accelerated benefits, which do
not reduce the Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1) on
claim. Since the accelerated benefits reduce your Life

Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1), the maximum level
of cover that you may select on your accelerated benefits
is equivalent to the total of the sums assured on your Life
Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1). The diagram below
shows the accelerated benefits.
Note that Life Cover and/or Renewable Life Cover(1) must
be selected if you select accelerated benefits.
A claim under an accelerated benefit will reduce the total
of your Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1) by the
amount of the claim. (Life Cover will always be accelerated
before Renewable Life Cover(1).

BENEFITS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

LIFE PROTECTION
Life Cover
(with Death Income
feature)

Life Cover provides your beneficiaries with a lump sum benefit should you pass away.

Adapted Life Cover

Adapted Life Cover provides cover for lives with substandard health who aren’t eligible for a Life cover
policy due to medical reasons. This solution is available to eligible clients only

Renewable Life Cover (1)

Renewable Life Cover (1) provides your beneficiaries with a lump sum benefit should you pass away.

Renewable Life

Renewable Life provides your beneficiaries with a lump sum benefit should you pass away.

Funeral Benefit

The Funeral Benefit provides an immediate payout towards funeral costs in the event of a death in your
family.

EduCator

EduCator will cover the tuition costs and other educational allowances associated with your children’s
education in the event that you should die or become permanently disabled.

The Death Income feature allows the policyholder to nominate 0% to 100% of each beneficiary’s
allocated Life Cover benefit lump sum to be paid as an income for a specified minimum term.

LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION
Lump Sum Range
Capital Disability and
Capital Disability Plus

Capital Disability and Capital Disability Plus pay you a lump sum if you are occupationally disabled as
defined in the policy document. There are two definitions of occupational disability:
• Own occupation disability (OOD)
• Own or any reasonable occupation disability (OD) - not available on Term benefits.
If you have selected the Whole of Life cover period, once you are retired the nature of your cover will
change. After retirement, if you become permanently impaired as defined in the policy document, you will
receive a monthly benefit which increases each year with inflation (as measured by CPI capped at 10.5%)
for the remainder of your life. The amount of your monthly benefit is a proportion of the sum assured and
depends on the severity of your impairment.
No post-retirement claims will be accepted on Term benefits.

Impairment and
Impairment Plus

Impairment and Impairment Plus pay you a lump sum if you are permanently impaired. The proportion
of the sum assured payable depends on the severity of your impairment.
If you have selected the Whole of Life cover period, once you are retired the nature of your cover will
change. After retirement, if you become permanently impaired as defined in the policy document, you will
receive a monthly benefit which increases each year with inflation (as measured by CPI capped at 10.5%)
for the remainder of your life. The amount of your monthly benefit is a proportion of the sum assured and
depends on the severity of your impairment.

Absolute Impairment and
Absolute Impairment
Plus

Absolute Impairment and Absolute Impairment Plus pay you the full sum assured if you are
permanently impaired as defined in the policy document.

Absolute Protector and
Absolute Protector Plus

Absolute Protector and Absolute Protector Plus pay you a lump sum if you are permanently
impaired or if you are unable to perform your occupational duties as defined in the policy document. The
proportion of the sum assured payable depends on whether or not you are occupationally disabled, and
on the severity of your impairment.
If you have selected the Whole of Life cover period, once you are retired the nature of your cover will
change. After retirement, if you become permanently impaired as defined in the policy document, you will
receive a monthly benefit which increases each year with inflation (as measured by CPI capped at 10.5%)
for the remainder of your life. The amount of your monthly benefit is a proportion of the sum assured and
depends on the severity of your impairment.
Post-retirement Term benefits will only be admitted on permanent impairment, where the amount
payable will be dependent on the severity specified in the Impairment Benefit Categories grid.

Vital Living and Vital
Living Plus

Vital Living and Vital Living Plus pay you the full sum assured if you are unable to perform a number of
specified daily activities, are permanently impaired or are diagnosed with cancer as defined in the policy
document.
The Vital Living and Vital Living Plus have built in conversion options which allow the benefit to convert
into more comprehensive lump sum disability and critical illness benefits on certain specified events as
well as at the end of the cover period. The conversion will be free of underwriting.

BENEFITS | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION (CONTINUED)
Income Range
Income Protector

The Income Protector pays a monthly claim payment, after the expiry of the waiting period, if:
• You are temporarily or permanently occupationally disabled;
• You are permanently impaired - the percentage of the benefit that is paid will be 50%, 75% or 100%
depending on the severity of the impairment;
• You suffer one of the conditions covered by the Guaranteed Payment Periods (7-day backdated and
1-month waiting period only); or
• You are partially occupationally disabled. A proportionate monthly claim payment will be determined
with respect to the partial loss of income and the disability.
You have flexibility in choosing the amount of cover you want to be paid (up to 100% of your after-tax
monthly income) within the first 24 months after a claim event, and thereafter. The Income Protector sum
assured is therefore made up of two sum assureds as follows:
• cover amount payable monthly within the first 24 months - This is the selected sum assured that
will be payable in the first 24 months from a claim event, after the expiry of the waiting period.
• cover amount payable monthly after 24 months - This is the selected sum assured that will be
payable after the 24 months since the claim event, and up until the selected cover period.
You can insure your income from dual occupations if applicable. The sum assured specified must equal
the combined cover for both your primary and secondary occupation. You must then specify how the
sum assured will be split between your primary and secondary occupation.
The following two benefits will be included free of charge:
• The Child Illness Protector benefit provides monthly claim payments (capped at R30 000 per
month) for up to 12 months, if your child is diagnosed with a terminal illness which results in the child’s
life expectancy being 12 months or less.
• The Claim Booster benefit pays up to an additional 33.33% of the Income Protector cover amount
payable monthly after 24 months. The Income Protector claim amount that would be payable
including your Claim Booster benefit, is subject to a maximum of 100% of your after-tax insurable
monthly income.

Impairment Income

The Impairment Income pays a monthly claim payment, if:
You are permanently impaired - the percentage of the benefit that is paid will be 50%, 75% or 100%
depending on the severity of the impairment.
Once 100% of the Sum Assured is in-claim, no further claims will be admitted.

Overhead Expenses
Protector

The Overhead Expenses Protector provides up to 23 monthly claim payments of up to 75% of business
overheads if you are temporarily or permanently unable, due to illness or injury, to perform the duties of
your own occupation.

Bonus Cover

The Bonus Cover benefit is payable for total and permanent occupational disability only. The sum
assured will be specified and limited to 100% of the average cash performance bonuses you have earned
in the previous 3 years. The first payment will be made immediately following the 24-month waiting
period. The benefit will pay up to 7 annual payments.
The Bonus Cover benefit will only be available on primary occupations and is not available on dual
occupations.

Retrenchment Protector

The Retrenchment Protector benefit pays the monthly sum assured (capped at R30 000 per month),
after the expiry of a 1 month waiting period, for up to 6 months if you are retrenched from full time
employment. A 12-month payment period is available on the Loan Protection package only. Please refer to
the Loan Protection package Annexure for more details.

Guaranteed Dependant
Protector

The Guaranteed Dependant Protector pays up to 12 times the Income Protector sum assured as a
lump sum to your beneficiaries where you die within 12 months from the first claim payment of an Income
Protector claim that is being paid at the time of your death. The Income Protector sum assured used will
refer to the cover amount payable monthly within the first 24 months, or if this cover is not in place, the
cover amount payable monthly after 24 months.
The multiple of the Income Protector sum assured that is payable will be between 1 and 12, depending
on the number of monthly claim payments that have already been paid for the Income Protector claim in
payment at the time of death.

Living Lifestyle Protector

The Living Lifestyle Protector pays up to 24 times the Income Protector sum assured as a monthly
payment or lump sum if you suffer a critical illness covered by the benefit. The Income Protector sum
assured used will refer to the cover amount payable monthly within the first 24 months, or if this cover is
not in place, the cover amount payable monthly after 24 months. The multiple of the sum assured that is
paid out will be either 3 or 24 based on the severity of the condition.

Living Lifestyle
Dependant Protector

The Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector pays out 6 times the Income Protector sum assured as a
monthly payment or lump sum if your spouse or your child suffers a critical illness covered by the benefit.
The Income Protector sum assured used will refer to the cover amount payable monthly within the first
24 months, or if this cover is not in place, the cover amount payable monthly after 24 months.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

LIFESTYLE PROTECTION
Living Lifestyle and
Living Lifestyle Plus (with
Top-Up Option and/or
Extended Option)

Living Lifestyle and Living Lifestyle Plus pay you a lump sum if you are diagnosed with a specified
critical illness.

Female Living Lifestyle

Female Living Lifestyle covers specific pregnancy related complications and congenital birth defects
which may have a significant financial impact.

Child Living Lifestyle

Term benefits will automatically include Top-Up and Extended.

POLICY PROTECTION
Future Protector

Future Protector is an option to purchase additional Life Cover, Renewable Life Cover(1), Living
Lifestyle, Living Lifestyle Plus, and lump sum Loss of Income Protection cover in future, without
further underwriting other than a negative HIV test.

Financial Protector

Financial Protector is an independent savings benefit that can be used to pay your premiums should
you be unable to do so.

Medical Premium
Protector (2)
(Closed to new business
January 2024)

The Medical Premium Protector benefit will pay out the monthly medical scheme contributions, of the
nominated dependants, directly to the medical scheme, on behalf of the policyholder or main member
in the event of a claim. A claim will become payable in the event of death, disability or impairment and
certain specified critical illnesses suffered by the life assured (which are included in the disability or
impairment definition). This will cover the medical scheme contributions of all the dependants under a
registered medical scheme, including the main member, adult dependants and child dependants. The life
assured will choose a level of cover required, based on the contributions of the medical scheme plan that
they belong to. An overall maximum of 17 dependants will be allowed.

Premium Protector –
Death

Premium Protector – Death waives the premiums due on your family’s benefits on your death. (On your
death, all your family’s benefits under the policy are protected and kept in force by this premium waiver.)

Premium Protector –
Disability

Premium Protector – Disability waives your premiums if you are disabled. This includes the premiums
due for any of your Second Life Assured or children’s benefits under the policy.

Premium Protector –
Retrenchment

Premium Protector – Retrenchment waives your premiums for up to 12 months if you are retrenched. This
includes the premiums due for any of your Second Life Assured or children’s benefits under the policy.

(1)

The Renewable Life Cover option is closed to New Business from July 2022.

(2)

Medical Premium Protector - closed to new business March 2024

What is the general structure of the benefits?
The risk protection pillar works as follows:
•

You select your benefits and the level of cover required for each benefit (subject to the benefit limits).

•

You are charged a premium that is appropriate to each benefit, your level of cover and your individual circumstances.

•

The benefit is paid if the insured event occurs and a valid claim is admitted.

The benefit ceases at the end of the term, the expiry age, or on full payout of the benefit.
GENERAL EXCLUSIONS
General exclusions for all
benefits except Life Cover,
Renewable Life Cover and
Funeral Benefit

In addition to any specific exclusions set out in the applicable benefit terms and conditions, no benefits
will be paid if a claim arose directly or indirectly from the Life Assured or Policyholder’s:
• Wilful and material violation of any criminal law;
• Exposure to atomic energy, nuclear fission or reaction, biological or chemical warfare agents;
• Refusal to seek or follow medical advice as suggested by an appropriate registered medical specialist
for the given condition;
• Attempted suicide or deliberate self-inflicted injury or illness
• Regular participation in hazardous or risky hobbies, sports or activities will also be excluded. Regular
participation is defined as participating in an activity more than once a year.
In addition to these general exclusions and specific exclusions set out under the Life Assured details,
there are also specific exclusions relating to each benefit selected as well as specific amounts payable
depending on the severity levels of illness, disability or impairment or the type of illness.

Funeral Benefit

No exclusions are applicable to the Funeral Benefit.
SUICIDE EXCLUSION FOR LIFE COVER AND RENEWABLE LIFE COVER(1)

Death within two years of
the Start Date
(1)

If, any time prior to two years after the Start Date or two years after the date of any revival of the policy,
the Life Assured dies by his or her own deliberate and intentional act (e.g. suicide), the policy will be void in
respect of this Life Assured and no benefits will be payable.

The Renewable Life Cover option is closed to New Business from July 2022.

BENEFITS | OCTOBER 2025

What is the rationale behind some of the general
exclusions on my policy?
“Wilful and material violation of any criminal law”
Liberty does not believe that claims should be paid where
the injury or illness is as a result of the policyholder breaking
the law. It is for this reason that we have excluded claims that
result from wilful and material violation of any criminal law.
“Exposure to atomic energy, nuclear fission or reaction,
biological or chemical warfare agents”
Liberty’s reinsurers will not cover claims that result from
exposure to atomic energy, nuclear fission or reaction,
biological or chemical hazards and biological or chemical
warfare agents. For this reason, one of these events has
the potential to impact on Liberty’s solvency which would
impact all policyholders negatively. As a result, Liberty needs
to follow our reinsurers on this exclusion.
“Refusal to seek or follow medical advice as suggested
by an appropriate registered medical specialist for the
given condition”
This exclusion is included to ensure that claims are not paid
where a policyholder refuses to undergo the necessary
treatment prescribed by a medical practitioner that could
reasonably be expected to result in partial or full recovery
from the injury or illness. This is to avoid policyholders
refusing to undergo treatment in order to get a large lump
sum payout, as this is not the intention of these benefits.
Paying such claims would ultimately also result in all
policyholders paying more for this cover.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

“Attempted suicide, or self-inflicted injury or illness”
This exclusion is to avoid any claimable events arising as a
result of a client’s own wilful and deliberate action. Again
this exclusion is to prevent anti-selective behaviour from
a policyholder in order to get a large lump sum payout. If
this were not the case then people in dire financial straits
could take out disability cover, pay one premium, disable
themselves and get a large payout. This is not good risk
management practice, and would impact the overall claims
experience, resulting in higher premiums being paid by all
policyholders.
“Regular participation in hazardous or risky hobbies,
sports or activities will also be excluded. Regular
participation is defined as participating in an activity
more than once a year.”
Participation in hazardous sports and activities results in an
increase in the probability of accidental injury. The risk of a
policyholder becoming disabled is therefore higher than for
someone that does not participate in these activities. It is
unfair that policyholders that don’t expose themselves to
these additional risks of injury should pay the same premium
as those who do.
For more details on the exclusions on your policy please
refer to your policy document.

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What type of cover does the Life Cover
benefit provide?
In the event of your death, Liberty pays your nominated
beneficiaries a lump sum.
What is the Terminal Illness Benefit?
In the event that you are diagnosed with a terminal illness,
e.g. severe cancer and you are likely to die within a year, you
have the option to exercise the Terminal Illness option.
If you exercise the Terminal Illness option, you will be paid
100% of the Life Cover sum assured. The Life Cover
benefit as well as any attached accelerated benefits will
cease following the payment of the Terminal Illness option.
If you do not exercise the Terminal Illness option, 100%
of your Life Cover sum assured will be paid to your
beneficiaries on your death.
If you have selected both Life Cover and Renewable Life
Cover(1) under the Level Premium Pattern, then you can
exercise the Terminal Illness option on:
•

Both Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1)

•

Either Life Cover or Renewable Life Cover

•

Neither

(1)

What is the Immediate Expenses Benefit?
The Immediate Expenses Benefit is a free benefit which is
available to you if you have selected Life Cover.
While the benefit comes at no additional cost, you still need
to select the Immediate Expenses Benefit to be included
on your policy. You will also need to nominate a specified
beneficiary who will receive the benefit.
In the event of death and provided the Immediate Expenses
Benefit was selected, the benefit will pay the lesser of:
•

R125 000; and

•

10% of the Life Cover benefit.

After receiving all the claims documentation, the specified
beneficiary will receive the benefit within 48 hours.
The Immediate Expenses Benefit will reduce the Life Cover
benefit payable to the nominated beneficiaries as indicated
in the policy schedule.
The Immediate Expenses Benefit is only payable once per
Life Assured.
If you have selected both the Life Cover benefit and the

Renewable Life Cover(1) benefit on your policy, the sums
assured will be added together to determine the immediate
payment.
A waiting period of two years will be applied from inception
of the Life Cover benefit. The Immediate Expenses benefit
can be added after Life Cover has been taken out and the
waiting period will apply from the date that the Life Cover
Benefit was taken out.
In the event that the Life Cover claim is repudiated, Liberty
reserves the right to recover the amount paid on this benefit.
How do accelerated benefits affect my Life
Cover benefit?
Living Lifestyle, Absolute Protector, Capital Disability,
Impairment, Absolute Impairment and Vital Living
are accelerated benefits on Lifestyle Protector. Claiming
under these benefits will reduce the sum assured of your
Life Cover benefit and/or your Renewable Life Cover(1),
and may reduce the sum assured of your other accelerated
benefits as these may never be greater than the total of your
Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1) benefits.
Claiming under your non-accelerated benefits will not affect
your Life Cover.
Accelerated and non-accelerated benefits are discussed
in detail under the Accelerated Benefits versus NonAccelerated Benefits section.
(1)

The Renewable Life Cover option is closed to New Business from July 2022.

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What is the Death Income feature?
The Death Income feature allows the policyholder to
nominate a portion of each beneficiary’s allocated Life
Cover benefit lump sum to be paid as an income for a
specified minimum term. The beneficiary may elect to use
a greater portion of their allocated Life Cover benefit lump
sum towards the annuity or specify a longer term.
It is important to note that the income payable will be
facilitated through the purchase of a suitable Liberty
investment product. The income is not guaranteed and will
be determined when the investment product is purchased
and a portion of the income may attract tax based on the
regulations at that time.
If the provision of an investment product is uneconomic
for you or Liberty due to market conditions, or as a result of
circumstances applicable to Liberty, then we will notify the
beneficiary and pay the full nominated portion as a lump
sum.
EXAMPLE 1

Mrs Chetty bought R3 000 000 in Life Cover to ensure
their bond is paid off and to continue providing for her
family should she pass away. She bought the EduCator
benefit to take care of her five-year old daughter’s
education in the event of death. However she was still
concerned about the other monthly needs of her family
such as groceries. She decides to use the Death Income
feature on her Life Cover benefit. In doing so, she
specified that she would like 40% of the Life Cover lump
sum allocated to her husband be used to purchase an
income to be paid for at least 10 years.
A year later, while returning from a seminar, Mrs Chetty’s
car was involved in a road accident and she passed away.
Her husband could spend 60%, R1 800 000 (paid as
a lump sum) to settle the outstanding bond and debts
while R1 200 000 was used to purchase an investment
product that provided the family with a monthly income
for the next 10 years.

EXAMPLE 2

Mr Botha bought R2000 000 in Life Cover to provide
for his family should he pass away. He has a wife and 2
children, aged 18 and 21. He would like for 50% of the Life
Cover to be paid to his wife and the balance to be equally
shared between his children. He prefers if his children
are mostly paid out with a monthly income for at least
5 years. He therefore decided to use the Death Income
feature as follows:
Beneficiary

Beneficiary
split

Proportion
Minimum
allocated to income term
income

Mrs Botha

50%

-

-

Danie Botha

25%

90%

Jan Botha

25%

80%

Two years later, Mr Botha unfortunately passes away
from a heart attack. R1 000 000 gets paid to his wife.
Danie gets R50 000 as a lump sum and R600 000
was used to purchase a Liberty investment product that
provided him with a monthly income for the next 5 years.
Jan would have received R100 000 as a lump sum and
R 400 000 used to purchase a Liberty investment to
pay an income for 5 years. However, he decides to invest
the full R700 000 in a Liberty investment that will pay
him a monthly income for a longer term of 7 years.
