BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

Aggregation Rules

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

• In the event that the policyholder/life assured has other Adapted Life Cover benefits or any equivalent
benefit, benefit aggregation rules will apply and limited to R5 000 000.
• The oldest policy pays the full sum assured and the others pay the balance, which might mean that this
benefit or part thereof might not be payable if the maximum Adapted Life Cover amount has already
been paid by the other Insurer. In the instance that the other insurer does not allow for aggregation,
Liberty will pay for the shortfall even if this policy is older.

Exclusion List

1. The standard two-year suicide exclusion will apply. Please refer to the life cover benefit annexure for
details.
2. Wilful and material violation of any criminal law.
3. Participation in any form of aviation other than as a fare-paying passenger. The travel as a fare-paying
passenger must be in accordance with the regulations under the South African Civil Aviation Authority
(SACAA).
4. War, invasion, hostile activities, foreign aggression, enemy action (whether war is declared or not),
civil war, rebellion, strikes, demonstrations, revolution, insurrection, military or unlawful usurpation of
power, or a deed by persons acting on behalf of or in connection with any organisation whose activities
are intended to bring down a de jure or de facto government by force, or to influence it by means of
terrorism or force, faction fighting rioting or civil commotion.
5. Service in the armed forces or as auxiliaries thereof in any country where any of the occurrences
mentioned in 4 above are taking place.
6. Exposure to atomic energy, nuclear fission or reaction, biological or chemical warfare agents.
7. Regular participation in hazardous or risky hobbies, sports or activities will also be excluded. Regular
participation is defined as participating in an activity more than once a year. Currently, the list of
dangerous activities includes the following, but note that it is not limited to these activities only: Rock
climbing/mountaineering.Fighting (except in genuine self-defense). Underwater diving, which includes
scuba diving snorkelling, cave diving, potholing,sinkhole diving, wreck diving.Aviation, which includes
private aviation (fixed wing and helicopter), microlight, ballooning,sailplane, parasailing, gyrocopting,
gliding and hang-gliding, paragliding, aerobatics,crop-dusting, game spotting, parachuting, skydiving
and skysurfing, kitesurfing. Speed contests, which includes motorsport racing, powerboat racing,
motorcycle racing.

Benefit Description

Renewable Life Cover pays a lump sum in the event of your death. The benefit has a level premium
guaranteed for 15 years. At the end of the 15-year period, the benefit may continue free of underwriting.
A new premium will be calculated to provide the required cover for a further five years, based on your age
and sum assured at this date. This exercise will be repeated every five years thereafter. Rating factors will
be as at the commencement of the policy.

Benefit Type

Renewable Life Cover is a core risk protection benefit and is available as a non-accelerated benefit only.

Allowed

The Renewable Life Cover benefit is renewable after 15 years. A new premium will be quoted. If this is
accepted the benefit will continue for the next five years. Thereafter, the premium will be reviewed every
five years. If the premium increases are accepted, the benefit can continue for Whole of Life.

Smoker status

Two cessions are available:
• Absolute cession – in this instance you cede your whole policy, i.e. all your benefits (including your family
benefits). You will cease being the owner of the policy and the cessionary will become the new owner.
• Collateral cession – a cession in security, whereby you may cede your Renewable Life Cover or those
of your Second Life Assured independently. This cession does not change ownership of the policy, but
any benefits paid whilst the cession is in force will be paid to the cessionary.

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Aggregation Rules

In the event that a policyholder has another Adapted Life Cover benefit or equivalent benefit with any
other insurer, aggregation rules will be applied. The oldest policy pays the full sum assured and the others
pay the balance, which might mean that this benefit or part thereof might not be payable if the maximum
Adapted Life Cover or equivalent benefit amount has already been paid by the other Insurer. In the
instance that the other insurer does not allow for aggregation, Liberty will pay for the shortfall even if this
policy is older.

Exclusion List

1. The standard two-year suicide exclusion will apply. Please refer to the life cover benefit annexure for
details.
2. Wilful and material violation of any criminal law.
3. Participation in any form of aviation other than as a fare-paying passenger. The travel as a fare-paying
passenger must be in accordance with the regulations under the South African Civil Aviation Authority
(SACAA).
4. War, invasion, hostile activities, foreign aggression, enemy action (whether war is declared or not),
civil war, rebellion, strikes, demonstrations, revolution, insurrection, military or unlawful usurpation of
power, or a deed by persons acting on behalf of or in connection with any organisation whose activities
are intended to bring down a de jure or de facto government by force, or to influence it by means of
terrorism or force, faction fighting, rioting or civil commotion. Service in the armed forces or as
5. uxiliaries thereof in any country where any of the occurrences mentioned in 4 above are taking place.
6. Exposure to atomic energy, nuclear fission or reaction, biological or chemical warfare agents.
7. Regular participation in hazardous or risky hobbies, sports or activities will also be excluded.

What type of cover does the Renewable Life Cover benefit
provide?
In the event of your death Liberty pays your nominated
beneficiaries a lump sum.

both Renewable Life Cover and the Life Cover exist on
the policy and only Renewable Life Cover ceases, the
accelerated benefits will continue, to the extent that they
don’t exceed the Life Cover, as they will then accelerate the
Life Cover.

What other benefits can I select if I have the Renewable
Life Cover benefit?

If I have the Life Cover benefit and the Renewable
Life Cover benefit on my policy, to which one will my
accelerated benefits apply?

Renewable Life Cover is a core risk protection benefit that
can be taken as a stand-alone benefit or with any other
benefit currently available on Lifestyle Protector, including
the Life Cover benefit. Since it is life cover, accelerated
benefits may also be selected if this benefit is chosen.
How do accelerated benefits affect my Renewable Life
Cover benefit?
Living Lifestyle, Capital Disability, Impairment, Absolute
Impairment, Absolute Protector and Vital Living are
accelerated benefits on the Lifestyle Protector. Claiming
under these benefits will reduce the sum assured of your
Renewable Life Cover and/or your Life Cover, and may
reduce the sum assured of your other accelerated benefits,
as these may never be greater than the total of your Life
Cover and Renewable Life Cover benefits.
Claiming under your non-accelerated benefits will not
affect your Renewable Life Cover. Accelerated and
non-accelerated benefits are discussed in detail under the
Accelerated Benefits versus Non-Accelerated Benefits
section.
What happens to my accelerated benefits if I do not
extend my Renewable Life Cover benefit after 15 years?
If the Renewable Life Cover is not extended after the initial
15-year period, the accelerated benefits will also cease. If

If both Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover have been
selected, then the accelerated benefits will accelerate the Life
Cover first and then the Renewable Life Cover if required.
How do the premium guarantees on the Renewable Life
Cover benefit differ from the guarantees on my other
benefits?
Renewable Life Cover will have a 15-year premium
guarantee period. At the end of the initial 15-year guarantee
period, a new premium will be calculated that will be
guaranteed for another five years. This will be based on your
age and sum assured at this date. Your cover will continue at
its previous level provided this premium is paid. This process
will be repeated every five years. The Renewable Life
Cover will be renewed free of underwriting.
You will have the option at this date to reduce the sum
assured rather than increase the premium.
If the Renewable Life Cover is extended, the accelerated
benefits will be extended too. If the sum assured for the
Renewable Life Cover benefit is reduced, the accelerated
benefit sums assured must be reduced too if these are now
greater than the Renewable Life Cover sum assured. If the
Level Premium Pattern is selected for the policy, the
accelerated benefits’ premiums will be subject to the normal
premium review every 10 years.

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

What is the Terminal Illness Benefit?
In the event that you are diagnosed with a terminal illness, e.g.
severe cancer, and you are likely to die within a year, you have
the option to exercise the Terminal Illness option.
If you exercise the Terminal Illness option, you will be paid
100% of the Renewable Life Cover sum assured. The
Renewable Life Cover as well as any attached accelerated
benefits will cease following the payment of the Terminal
Illness option.
If you do not exercise the Terminal Illness option, 100% of
your Renewable Life Cover sum assured will be paid to your
beneficiaries on your death.
If you have both Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover you
may exercise the Terminal Illness option on either one of the
benefits, or on both.
What is the Immediate Expenses benefit?
The Immediate Expenses Benefit is a free benefit which is
available to you if you have selected Renewable Life Cover.
While the benefit comes at no additional cost, you still need
to select the Immediate Expenses Benefit to be included
on your policy. You will also need to nominate a specified
beneficiary who will receive the benefit.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

In the event of death and provided the Immediate Expenses
Benefit was selected, the benefit will pay the lesser of:
•

R125 000; and

•

10% of the Renewable Life Cover benefit.

After receiving all the claims documentation, the specified
beneficiary will receive the benefit within 48 hours. The
Immediate Expenses Benefit will reduce the Renewable
Life Cover benefit payable to the nominated beneficiaries as
indicated in the policy schedule.
The Immediate Expenses Benefit is only payable once per
Life Assured.
If you have selected both the Life Cover benefit and the
Renewable Life Cover benefit on your policy, the sums assured
will be added together to determine the immediate payment.
A waiting period of two years will be applied from inception
of the Renewable Life Cover benefit. The Immediate
Expenses benefit can be added after Renewable Life
Cover has been taken out and the waiting period will apply
from the date that the Renewable Life Cover benefit was
taken out.
In the event that the Renewable Life Cover claim is
repudiated, Liberty reserves the right to recover the amount
paid on this benefit.

FUNERAL BENEFIT

A lump sum is paid in the event of the death of a family member.

Benefit Type

Claims under this benefit will not reduce the cover under any other benefit.

Cover Period

Adult Lives: The cover period is Whole of Life
Children:
Until attainment of age 18, or date of marriage if earlier.

Benefit Limits

Minimum: R1 000 (1 unit)
Maximum: R125 000 (100 units) (subject to legislation)
The maximum is aggregated over all policies with Liberty.
Not more than five claims per family are allowed under this benefit.

What is the Funeral Benefit?
The Funeral Benefit provides you and your family with the
peace of mind that a payment towards funeral costs will be
made in the event of a death in your family.
To which life assured does the Funeral Benefit belong?
Only the First Life Assured is allowed to select the Funeral
Benefit.
In the event of you dying, your Second Life Assured will
become the First Life Assured if he/she is also a life assured
under the same Lifestyle Protector policy. Claiming under
this benefit will not reduce the cover provided to the rest of
the family.

EXAMPLE

Mr and Mrs Jones have two children. They purchase a
Lifestyle Protector and both have core risk protection
benefits. Mr Jones takes out a Funeral Benefit of
R20 000 for his family. Mr Jones passes away. Liberty
pays R20 000 to Mr Jones’s family.
Mrs Jones then becomes the First Life Assured under
this benefit. This ensures that Mrs Jones and her
children are protected should any of them die. The
benefit will cease on the death of Mrs Jones.

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

Do I have to have Life Cover to select the Funeral
Benefit?
No, as long as you have a core risk protection benefit, you
may select the Funeral Benefit. In the event that you die
and your Second Life Assured has a core risk benefit under
the same policy, your Second Life Assured becomes the First
Life Assured and the benefit continues.
How does the waiting period affect my cover?
Claims in respect of the death of the Life Assured may be
subject to a waiting period that will limit claims as follows:
•

If the benefit is added after the Start Date of the policy,
no claim will be accepted within 6 months of the
commencement of the benefit unless death is as a result
of an accident or other unnatural cause.

Claims in respect of the death of the Life Assured’s Spouse
may be subject to a waiting period that will limit claims as
follows:
•

•

If the Life Assured’s Spouse is not the Second Life
Assured on this policy, no claim will be accepted within
6 months of the commencement of the benefit unless
death is as a result of an accident or other unnatural
cause; or
If the Life Assured’s Spouse is the Second Life Assured on
this policy and this benefit is added after the Start Date
of the policy, no claim will be accepted within 6 months
of the commencement of the benefit unless death is as a
result of an accident or other unnatural cause.

Claims in respect of the death of the Life Assured’s Child will
be subject to a waiting period that will limit claims as follows:
•

No claim will be accepted within 6 months of the
commencement of the benefit unless death is as a result
of an accident or other unnatural cause.

The waiting periods described above will also apply
where the Sum Assured has been increased other than by
automatic benefit increases. The waiting period will only

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

apply to the increased portion of the Sum Assured and
will apply from the date that the increase in Sum Assured
became effective.
If a Policyholder provides evidence of similar previous cover
along with a cancellation letter from the previous product
provider stating that cancellation occurred within 31 days
from issue date of this Funeral Benefit, we will assess any
claim as if cover was continuous and any new waiting period
will only apply to cover in excess of the replaced policy or
Life Assureds not previously covered. Any previous waiting
periods applied to previous cover would therefore be
recognised by us.
In the case of reinstated cover, any previous waiting periods
will be recognised, and any new waiting period will only apply
to cover in excess of the original cover or Life Assureds not
previously covered.
Is the amount paid out limited by legislation?
Payment in respect of a child as a life assured under this
benefit is limited in terms of the Long-Term Insurance Act,
1998 to the following:
•

Where the child is under 6 years of age at date of death,
the amount payable is limited to R20 000 (or any such
other amount as may be determined by the Long-term
Insurance Act, 1998 or other legislation/regulation that
may amend or replace it in future)

•

Where the child is between 6 and 14 at the date of
death, the amount payable is limited to R50 000 (or any
such other amount as may be determined by the Longterm Insurance Act, 1998 or other legislation/regulation
that may amend or replace it in future).

•

Where the child is older than 14 years at the date of
death, the amount payable is limited to the same as the
main member - R125 000.

For the purposes of this benefit only, the definition of Child
includes a stillborn baby of 28 weeks and older.

EDUCATOR
Benefit Description

EduCator will cover the costs associated with a child/children’s education if the life assured should die or
become disabled.

Benefit Type

This is a non-accelerated benefit. At least one core risk benefit must be selected together with
EduCator.

Benefit Events

EduCator can be selected to apply to either:
• the First Life Assured; or
• the First Life Assured and Second Life Assured.
EduCator can be selected to be payable on:
• Death; or
• Death or disability.

Child Beneficiary

A maximum of 8 children will be allowed.

Cover Period

The benefit payment period per child beneficiary will end on the earliest of:
• the end of the child beneficiary’s education; or
• the death of the child beneficiary.
The cover will cease for each child on their 24th birthday. If a claim occurs before their 24th birthday,
the remaining education costs will be paid in line with the terms of the benefit which may result in claim
payments beyond their 24th birthday.
Where disability has also been selected, the disability benefit will cease at the policy anniversary prior to
the First (or Second) Life Assured’s 65th birthday.

Benefit Limits

EduCator pays the cost of the child beneficiary’s education, an allowance towards associated education
costs and an allowance for achievement, subject to maximum benefit limits. These limits are increased
each year in line with education inflation as determined by Liberty.

Entry Ages

Next Gen Cover

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

When the Child Beneficiary completes their education, Liberty will accept an application on their life, for
Life Cover (limited to R1 200 000), critical illness, impairment and/or lump sum disability benefits, without
any health underwriting. However, Liberty reserves the right to call for HIV and smoker status. The offer is
subject to the benefit limits and rules at the time of exercising the option. Normal financial, occupational
and territorial underwriting will still apply.
No claim for any pre-existing diagnosed medical condition, health impairment or a change in health
discovered prior to the inception of this new policy will be considered or payable and will also include
conditions where pre-existing symptoms and/or signs of a health change were present, but a diagnosis
was not made, whether medically investigated, or treated, or not.
To qualify for these benefits, the Child Life Assured will have to apply for Life Cover within three months
of the completion of their education. The Child Beneficiary would have to be removed from the EduCator
benefit once the new policy is taken out.
Premiums for these benefits will be calculated based on Liberty’s business practices at the time

What benefit does EduCator provide?
EduCator pays the costs associated with a child’s education
if the life assured should die, become occupationally disabled
or severely ill. For the purposes of this benefit “child” means
a child who is financially dependent on the life assured,
including a biological or legally adopted child, or a nominated
child, who is living with or depends on the life assured. For
example, this provides a child’s parents, grandparents, aunts,
uncles and godparents with the opportunity to take out the
benefit on behalf of that child.

EduCator will cover the actual tuition fees payable to the
educational institution subject to the maximum benefit limits.
The maximum benefit limits are adjusted every year at the
rate of education inflation, as determined by Liberty. This rate
is based each year on the average increase in education costs
in the past year.

EduCator can be selected to cover either:

Where a child is attending home-schooling the tuition
allowance will cover the verifiable costs of their homeschooling such as invoices, subject to the overall maximum
limit applicable for the institution type that was selected (i.e.
Public or Independent). This will be limited to:
• the actual registration and tuition fees charged by the
platform provider;

•

the First Life Assured, or

•

•

1st of the First Life Assured and Second Life Assured
to claim.

the actual costs of the textbooks that the parent and
child require to enable home-schooling; and the costs
of writing external examinations, where these are not
included in the tuition charged by the platform provider.

•

the costs of writing external examinations, where these
are not included in the tuition charged by the platform
provider.

What type of cover does EduCator provide?

When you select the “1st of First Life Assured and Second
Life Assured” option, you and the Second Life Assured will
be lives assured under this benefit. However, the benefit is
payable on the first of either you or the second life dying (or
becoming disabled), whereafter EduCator ceases.
You can select that EduCator pays on either:
•

Death only, or

•

Death or Disability.

Disability can be either in respect of own occupation (OOD)
or own or similar occupation (OD). The benefit is payable on
the first event (i.e. Death or Disability) only. The choice of OOD
or OD must be the same as that selected on other disability
benefits with occupation-based definitions on the policy.

This excludes any costs that might be associated with
appointing any other person such as tutors or au pairs that
might be facilitating the home-schooling support or teaching
functions.
Tuition fees
The tuition fees that are covered are as follows:
•

2 years pre-primary tuition fees

•

7 years primary school tuition fees

•

5 years secondary school tuition fees

Under the Disability benefit Liberty will also cover the child’s
education costs if you become severely ill. The following
severe illness conditions are covered:

•

cover for first undergraduate tertiary qualification
obtained

•

residential fees at tertiary level.

•

Cancer and Leukaemia

Tuition fees are payable directly to the educational institution
concerned as a lump sum at the beginning of each academic
year, subject to Liberty’s business rules at the time. This may
differ if the child attends home-schooling, since there may
be multiple vendors that require payment, or the timing of
payments differ to traditional schooling institutions. Payment
will be made on receipt of invoices. If a claim is made partway
through an academic year, a pro rata payment in respect of
the remaining academic year is payable.

•

Conditions affecting the Cardiovascular System

•

Stroke

•

Paralysis

Please see the policy document for the full definitions under
which EduCator is payable.
What will EduCator pay?
Tuition allowance

If the child changes institutions during the academic year
after tuition fees have already been paid, no further benefit

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

will be paid to the new institution for that particular year.
You can select whether EduCator should cover the costs of
the child’s education based on the category chosen for the
benefit. The current benefit allows for 3 categories being a
Public, Independent, or Prime categorized educational
institution. The premiums charged for EduCator will be
different depending on which category is selected.
When in claim, the child will be able to take two years off
between the end of their secondary education and starting
tertiary education, as gap years, without reducing the total
benefit payable in claim.
The tuition allowance will only pay for the first
undergraduate degree for the lesser period of the standard
time to get the degree and 4 years. This means that if an
undergraduate degree takes 3 years, then the benefit
will pay for 3 years. The exception is a medical or dental
undergraduate degree, which will be paid for 6 years and 5
years respectively.
The tuition allowance for tertiary institutions will include
any National Qualifications Framework (NQF) recognised
tertiary level certificate. This will include both full-time and
part-time courses.
Only the first certificate or undergraduate degree/diploma
will be covered. If two or more certificates are done
simultaneously the most expensive certificate will be
covered. Payments will be made irrespective of the child
passing or not. However, the maximum number of years
covered per education level will apply. If a claim is accepted
partway through the schooling career the maximum
number of years will reduce to the remaining years needed
for the child to complete the schooling level if each year is
successfully completed.
Tuition fees for undergraduate degrees will also cover the
residence fees associated with the institution.
Types of covered institutions
EduCator covers tuition fees for a child attending either a:
•

Public Institution

This institution is defined as an educational institution owned
and subsidized by the government.
•

Independent Institution

An institution that is a privately owned educational
institution, not receiving any subsidies from the government,
unless specifically applied for.
•

Prime Institution

Like an independent institution, this is also a privately owned
educational institution, not receiving any subsidies from the
South African government, unless specifically applied for.
The difference between this and independent institutions
is the higher benefit maximums applicable at pre-primary,
primary and secondary levels, owing to the additional tuition
fees that are charged by the educational institution that the
child is attending.
Education for special needs
If the child has to attend an institution (approved by Liberty)
for the mentally or physically disabled, Liberty will cover the
tuition fees for that institution subject to a maximum
Rand limit of 150% of the normal maximum Rand limits
determined by Liberty from time to time.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

International study
If a Child beneficiary emigrates whilst in-claim, Liberty will
pay for the tuition fees at recognised pre-primary, primary,
secondary and international tertiary institutions that are not
on the approved list of international institutions. Benefit
payments will be subject to the maximum Rand limits of
Public, Independent and Prime categories per education
level (as determined by Liberty each year). Only international
tertiary institutions that are recognised by the relevant
regulatory body in the country (based on the laws and
regulations that control the establishment, recognition and
status of academic institutions in that country) will be
recognised by Liberty and eligible for continued claim
payments.
International study - Approved list
Liberty will pay the tuition fees should the child attend an
approved international tertiary institution. Liberty reserves
the right to change the approved list of international
institutions from time to time, at its sole discretion.
Please see the policy document for the approved list of
international institutions. Should the child attend one of
the international tertiary institutions on the approved list at
the time of claim, the benefit payments will be subject to a
maximum Dollar limit. This maximum Dollar limit is updated
annually, as determined by Liberty each year.
EduGlobal
This option covers additional international tertiary
institutions that do not form part of the approved
international university list for clients who want a higher level
of international tertiary institution cover whilst in claim. The
international tertiary institutions covered under EduGlobal,
form part of the ENIC-NARIC network, which expands the
choice of international universities that a child beneficiary
can attend. This feature is not automatically included,
however can be selected to expand the coverage, and
pays out up to 3 times the South African tertiary
institutions maximum Rand limits ( as determined by Liberty
each year).
Supplementary allowances
The supplementary allowance will be paid annually for the
entire duration of the child beneficiary’s education starting
from primary education and ending with tertiary education.
The allowance will be paid to the child directly. The amount
of the allowance will depend on the level of education the
child is attending and is a percentage of the Tuition allowance
subject to the maximum and minimum amounts set by
Liberty from time to time.
This may differ if the child attends home-schooling due to
the possible timing differences in tuition and examination
payments. The resultant supplementary allowance will be
made with each tuition allowance related payment. Payment
will be made on receipt of invoices.
While in claim, Liberty will also pay an amount to cover
other expenses associated with the child’s education. These
expenses may include:
•

Extra-mural activities

•

Extra lessons

•

Uniforms

•

Stationery

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

•

Textbooks

•

Transportation costs

Primary school supplementary allowance limits are:
Supplementary allowance will pay an amount of 15% of
Tuition fees, up to the applicable maximum annual limit. The
Supplementary allowance will always pay a minimum of
R8 000, where the calculation of 15% of Tuition fees falls
below R8 000. The current maximum annual limit for
primary school supplementary allowance is R 15 410.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

If my spouse and I have more children in future, will we
be able to cover them too, if at least one of my children
is already covered under EduCator?
If you add the benefit after the policy has been issued, it will
be subject to underwriting. However, if at least one other
child is already covered under EduCator:
•

If you currently have an EduCator benefit on a Lifestyle
Protector policy, you can add an additional EduCator
benefit for a newborn child as a beneficiary without
underwriting, provided the child is born within 5
years of the date you were last underwritten. Liberty
reserves the right to call for an HIV test on you (and
your Second Life Assured), which must have a negative
result. The newborn child will need to be added to the
benefit within 4 months of being born for you to not be
underwritten with the servicing change.

•

If you or your Second Life Assured die (or become
disabled) while you are expecting another child, your
unborn child will become eligible for EduCator. The
benefit will be restricted to children born within a
9-month period from the date of death (or disability).

Secondary School supplementary allowance limits
are:
Supplementary allowance will pay an amount of 17.5% of
Tuition fees, up to the applicable maximum annual limit.
The Supplementary allowance will always pay a minimum
of R8 000, where the calculation of 17.5% of Tuition fees
falls below R8 000. The current maximum annual limit for
secondary school supplementary allowance is R 23 140.
Tertiary education supplementary allowance:
The full supplementary annual limit of R 12 350 is paid for
tertiary education.
Early childhood development allowance
This benefit will be payable prior to when a child starts
formal schooling. This mean that:
•

It will be paid if the Child Beneficiary is younger than 7
years old (age next) who does not yet qualify for the
tuition allowance.

•

The payout covers the cost of early childhood
development subject to maximum limits determined by
Liberty every year.

•

This allowance can cover the cost of nursery schools,
day care, and similar institutions.

•

This allowance will cease once the child starts formal
pre-primary tuition

Achievement allowance
This benefit is intended to reward a child that excels in a
sport, cultural or other activity and represents a South
African province (i.e. provincial level) or South Africa (i.e.
national level). The allowance is payable as a lump sum
directly to the child and is payable until the end of secondary
education. The allowance is structured as follows:
• It will only be paid in respect of the year in which the
provincial or national colours are awarded, and it is only
paid once a year.
•

If the child beneficiary receives provincial and national
colours in the same academic year, then the total
allowance payable will be limited to the national colours
awarded, irrespective of how many times colours are
awarded in that year.

•

The Achievement allowance is payable for South African
colours only. Proof that the colours were awarded, in the
form of a certificate from the relevant regulatory body,
must be submitted.

If all your other children are covered for the costs of
education at an independent institution, your unborn
child will also be covered for the costs of education at an
independent institution. If any of your other children are
covered for the costs of education at a public institution,
your unborn child will also be covered for the costs of
education at a public institution.
Please note: If you add the EduCator after the policy has
been issued, it will be subject to underwriting.
Does all cover cease when EduCator ceases?
Next Gen Cover
When the Child Beneficiary completes their education,
Liberty will accept an application on their life, for life cover,
critical illness, impairment and/or lump sum disability
benefits, with limited underwriting. The available cover
amounts for each benefit will be limited to R1 200 000
for lump sum benefits within the Life Protection, Income
Protection and Lifestyle Protection pillars. Liberty, still,
however, reserves the right to call for HIV and smoker status.
The offer, including the size of accessible cover, is subject
to the benefit limits and business rules at the time of the
exercising the option. Normal financial, occupational, and
territorial underwriting will still apply.
A claim for any pre-existing diagnosed medical condition,
health impairment or a change in health discovered prior
to the inception of this new policy will not be considered or
payable and will also include conditions where pre-existing
symptoms and/or signs of a health change were present, but
a diagnosis was not made, whether medically investigated,
or treated, or not.
To qualify for these benefits, the Child Beneficiary will have
to apply for cover within three months of the EduCator
benefit ending or within three months of completion of their
education. The Child Beneficiary would have to be removed
from the EduCator benefit once the new policy is taken out.
Premiums for these benefits will be calculated based on
Liberty’s business practices at the time.

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

EXAMPLE

Mrs Johnson purchases a Lifestyle Protector policy with
Living Lifestyle Plus in January. In addition Mrs Johnson
selects EduCator on the death or disability (OD) of the 1st of
the First Life Assured and Second Life Assured for their two
children, Jackie (10) and Jonah (14). Mrs Johnson selects
cover at an independent institution for Jackie and at a public
institution for Jonah. EduCator will therefore cover the
actual education costs of the two children if either Mrs or Mr
Johnson dies or becomes disabled.
Two and a half years later in June, while expecting their
third child, Mr Johnson passes away. Since both Mrs and
Mr Johnson are lives assured under EduCator, the benefit
will now cover the premiums towards Jackie and Jonah’s
education. Three months later the third child, Janine is
born. Mrs Johnson informs Liberty of the new addition to
the family and Janine’s future educational costs will also be
covered by Liberty.
Jackie finishes primary school in this year. The next 5 years
she attends a private school in Johannesburg. Directly after
finishing school she enrolls for a 3-year undergraduate
degree in Marketing. She also enrolls as a resident at the
university hostel for this period.
During this time Liberty will cover the following costs
towards Jackie’s education:
Primary education:
• The pro rata fees applicable for the remainder of the
academic year
• Supplementary allowance for the remaining 6 months of
the year.
Secondary education:
• The actual tuition fees of the private secondary school
for the full 5 years of secondary education
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
Tertiary education:
• The tuition fees for the 3-year undergraduate degree
• Residence fees for 3 years
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
The above costs will be covered, subject to the maximum
benefit limits applicable each year.
After the 3 years of tertiary education, EduCator will
cease, and no further benefits will be payable. At this time
Jackie will be able take out her own Lifestyle Protector
policy and purchase any combination of benefits across
life cover, critical illness, impairment and/or lump sum
disability benefits. This application for cover will be free of
underwriting (except for an HIV and smoker status test).
Jonah finishes his secondary education, but has to do
Grade 12 twice. In Grade 9 and 10 Jonah receives Provincial
colours in athletics. While in Grade 11, Jonah receives
Provincial as well as National colours in athletics. After
finishing school, Jonah decides to take 2 years off to explore
Europe, whereafter he decides to start work immediately
and not to continue with his studies.

During this time Liberty will cover the following costs
towards Jonah’s education:
Secondary education:
• The pro rata tuition fees for the remaining Grade 10 year
i.e. last 6 months of the year
• Tuition fees for Grade 11 and 12; the second attempt at
Grade 12 is not covered
• Supplementary Allowance – 6 months for Grade 10 and
full allowance for Grade 11 and 12.
Achievement allowance:
• Payment towards the Provincial colours received in Grade 10
• Payment towards the Provincial colours received in Grade 11
• Payment towards the National colours received in
Grade 11 - the amount will be the difference between the
amount payable for National colours and the amount
already paid towards the Provincial colours.
The above costs will be covered, subject to the maximum
benefit limits applicable each year.
While Jonah is touring Europe his benefit will remain intact
for two years. Since Jonah doesn’t proceed with tertiary
education after the 2 years, EduCator ceases. At this time,
within 3 months of his EduCator ceasing, Jonah will be able
take out his own Lifestyle Protector policy and purchase
any combination of benefits across life cover, critical illness,
impairment and/or lump sum disability benefits. This
application for cover will be free of underwriting (except for
an HIV and smoker status test).
For Janine, the unborn child at the time of Mr Johnson’s
death, Liberty will pay the following for Janine’s education:
Early Childhood Development Allowance:
• The allowance will pay towards the tuition costs for
nursery school, each year, before formal education begins,
up to the applicable benefit limit.
Pre-primary education:
• The tuition fees for two academic years at a public
institution.
Primary education:
• The actual school fees of the public primary school for the
full 7 years of primary education
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
Secondary education:
• The actual school fees of the private secondary school for
the full 5 years of secondary education
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
Tertiary education:
• The tuition fees for her first undergraduate degree (up
to a maximum of 4 years (not applicable to degrees in
medicine and dentistry)
• Residence fees for the period of the undergraduate
degree
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
The above costs will be covered, subject to the maximum
benefit limits applicable each year.

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

EDUCATOR XTRA
Benefit Description

EduCator Xtra will cover the costs associated with a child’s education if the life assured should die or
become disabled. The benefit pays a cashback lump sum following the child’s 18th birthday.

Benefit Type

This is a standalone benefit.

Benefit Events

EduCator Xtra benefit is applicable to either the First or Second Life Assured.
EduCator Xtra can be selected to be payable on:
• Death; or
• Death or disability.

Child Beneficiary

Maximum of 8 children will be allowed.

Cover Period

The benefit payment period for the child beneficiary will end on the earliest of:
• the end of the child beneficiary’s education; or
• the death of the child beneficiary.
The cover will cease for each child on their 24th birthday.
If a claim occurs before their 24th birthday, the remaining education costs will be paid in line with the
terms of the benefit which may result in claim payments beyond their 24th birthday.
Where disability has also been selected, the disability benefit will cease at the policy anniversary prior
to the Life Assured’s 65th birthday.

Cashback feature

A Cashback feature is automatically included within the EduCator Xtra benefit.
This feature pays a once-off lump sum cash amount equal to 100% of the nominal value of all
Premiums received on the policy, from inception of the policy to the anniversary date immediately
following the Child Beneficiary’s 18th birthday.
The Cashback benefit will then be paid on the policy anniversary date immediately following the Child
Beneficiary’s 18th birthday.

Benefit Limits

EduCator Xtra pays the cost of the child beneficiary’s education, an allowance towards associated
education costs and an allowance for achievement, subject to maximum benefit limits.
These limits are increased each year in line with education inflation as determined by Liberty.

Entry Ages

First/Second Life Assured or child beneficiary:
Life Assured

Child Beneficiary

Minimum entry age

Newborn

Maximum next
birthday

Similar limits for the child age are applicable to servicing changes.
Rating Factors

•
•
•
•
•
•
•
•
•

Premium Payment Period

The premiums are payable until the policy anniversary prior the child beneficiary’s 25th birthday.
On disability, the premiums are payable until the earlier of the policy anniversary prior to the Life
Assured’s 65th birthday and the child beneficiary’s 25th birthday.

Benefit and Premium
Increases

The premiums and maximum benefit limits will increase each year in line with education inflation as
determined by Liberty.

Waiting Period

No waiting period applies. In the event of disability, the benefit will be payable once permanence of
the condition has been established.

Payment Pattern

The premium for EduCator Xtra will always be level (i.e. does not increase with age). EduCator Xtra
premiums increase each year in line with education inflation.

Cessions

No absolute cessions are allowed on EduCator Xtra.

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

Next Gen Cover

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

When the Child Beneficiary completes their education, Liberty will accept an application on their life,
for Life Cover (limited to R1 200 000), critical illness, impairment and/or lump sum disability benefits,
without any health underwriting. However, Liberty reserves the right to call for HIV and smoker
status. The offer is subject to the benefit limits and rules at the time of exercising the option. Normal
financial, occupational and territorial underwriting will still apply.
No claim for any pre-existing diagnosed medical condition, health impairment or a change in health
discovered prior to the inception of this new policy will be considered or payable and will also
include conditions where pre-existing symptoms and/or signs of a health change were present, but a
diagnosis was not made, whether medically investigated, or treated, or not.
To qualify for these benefits, the Child Life Assured will have to apply for Life Cover within three
months of the completion of their education. The Child Beneficiary would have to be removed from
the EduCator benefit once the new policy is taken out.
Premiums for these benefits will be calculated based on Liberty’s business practices at the time.

What is EduCator Xtra?
It is a stand-alone EduCator benefit, therefore there is no
requirement to take out a core benefit to enjoy the benefits
that the EduCator benefit offers. The benefit pays out a
cashback lump sum following the child beneficiary’s 18th
birthday.
How does the Cashback feature work?
Cashback is automatically included with the EduCator
Xtra benefit and where the Child Beneficiary meets the
qualification criteria. This benefit pays a once-off lump sum
cash amount equal to the nominal value of premiums paid
per child beneficiary, whereby the nominal value of premiums
does not include any adjustment for inflation or the addition
of interest over the benefit term. The lump sum cash amount
is calculated at a benefit level and does not include premiums
received for any other Lifestyle Protector benefit (if any).
Any changes to the benefit after the Child Beneficiary’s 11th
birthday may result in a reduction of the percentage of the
nominal value of premiums accrued following the change.
When will the Cashback be paid out?
The Cashback benefit will be paid on either the policy
anniversary following the Child Beneficiary’s 18th birthday
or the 5th policy anniversary, whichever is the later date.
The Cashback benefit will not be paid earlier than on the
policy anniversary date following the Child Beneficiary’s 18th
birthday.
What types of events will impact the Cashback?
In the table below, you will find how the different types of
events will affect the Cashback feature:

Event

Cashback payout impact

Death or Disability
of the Life Assured
before the Child
Beneficiary’s 18th
birthday

The Cashback accrued at the time of
Death or Disability of the Life Assured
will be paid.

Death or Disability of
the Life Assured after
the Child Beneficiary’s
18th birthday

No impact as the Cashback was already
paid on the policy anniversary following
the Child Beneficiary’s 18th birthday.

Death of the Child
Beneficiary prior to
their 18th birthday

The Cashback accrued at the time of
Death of the Child Beneficiary will be
paid to the policyholder and benefit will
be cancelled.

Death of the Child
Beneficiary after their
18th birthday

No impact as the Cashback was already
paid on the policy anniversary following
the Child Beneficiary’s 18th birthday.

It is found that
inaccurate or false
information has been
provided

Liberty reserves the right to terminate
the entire benefit. This is at the sole
discretion of Liberty and is dependent
on the nature of the non-disclosure.

The EduCator Xtra
benefit lapses or is
removed prior to the
Child Beneficiary’s
18th birthday

The Cashback benefit amount will be
forfeited. If the EduCator Xtra benefit
is reinstated, the Cashback benefit
amount will be paid on the Cashback
benefit payment date, provided that all
arrear premiums are paid in full.

What happens to the Cashback if the child is a
beneficiary on more than one benefit?
Where there are multiple EduCator Xtra benefits for a Child
Beneficiary, aggregation rules will apply. Aggregation rules are
applied when the Child Beneficiary may be entitled to more
than one claim of the same benefit for a single claim event.
An example of this is in the case where a Child Beneficiary is
listed as the beneficiary under more than one EduCator or
EduCator Xtra benefit at claim stage.

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

Liberty will handle the claim as follows:
•
Only the oldest benefit will be paid.
•
For the benefit that will not pay out, Liberty will refund
the premiums paid for that benefit, net of expenses,
and cancel the benefit.
•
If there is a claim paid, the Cashback will be the
accumulated value of premiums to date. If the claim is
adjusted due to aggregation rules, as described above,
there will be a refund of premiums, net of expenses.
For more detail on the exact treatment, refer to each
client’s policy annexures.
Can I add the Cashback feature to an existing EduCator
rider benefit?
No, the cashback feature is only available on the EduCator
Xtra benefit. A new EduCator Xtra benefit will have to be
taken out to enjoy the benefits of the feature. A client cannot
have an EduCator and EduCator Xtra benefit simultaneously
on the same child beneficiary.
How will I receive the Cashback?
The Cashback benefit will be paid into the Policyholder’s
bank account on record subject to the benefit being active,
and there being no outstanding contractual premiums
due at time of payment. Liberty may offer alternative
payment options to the Policyholder from time to time and
in the event of a claim where this option to pay into the
Policyholder’s bank account is not available.
When will the Cashback be forfeited?
The term of the qualifying Cashback benefit cannot be
reduced to receive the lump sum cash amount earlier than
the policy anniversary following the Child Beneficiary’s 18th
birthday. You cannot cancel only the Cashback benefit,
however, there is the option to cancel the entire EduCator
Xtra benefit/policy. If the policy/benefit is cancelled or lapses
prior to the Cashback benefit payment date, the accrued
Cashback value will be forfeited.
What client need does EduCator Xtra benefit provide for?
EduCator Xtra pays the costs associated with a child’s

education if the life assured should die or become
permanently occupationally disabled or severely ill. For the
purposes of this benefit “child” means a child who is financially
dependent on the life assured, including a biological or legally
adopted child, or a nominated child, who is living with or
depends on the life assured. For example, this provides a
child’s parents, grandparents, aunts, uncles and godparents
with the opportunity to take out the benefit on behalf of that
child.
What type of cover does EduCator Xtra provide?

EduCator Xtra can be selected to cover either:
•
the First Life Assured, or
•
1st of the First Life Assured and Second Life Assured to
claim.
When you select the “1st of First Life Assured and Second
Life Assured” option, you and the Second Life Assured will

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

be lives assured under this benefit. However, the benefit is
payable on the first of either you or the second life dying (or
becoming disabled), whereafter EduCator ceases.
You can select that EduCator Xtra pays on either:
Death only, or
•
•
Death or Disability.
Disability can be either in respect of own occupation (OOD)
or own or similar occupation (OD). The benefit is payable
on the first event (i.e. Death or Disability) only. The choice
of OOD or OD must be the same as that selected on other
disability benefits with occupation-based definitions on the
policy. Under the Disability benefit Liberty will also cover the
child’s education costs if you become severely ill.
The following critical illness conditions are covered:
•
Cancer and Leukaemia
•
Conditions affecting the Cardiovascular System
•
Stroke
•
Paralysis
Following diagnosis, the client will need to meet the mimium
criteria to qualify for the benefit payout. Please see the policy
document for the full definitions under which EduCator is
payable for the above conditions.
What will EduCator Xtra pay?
Tuition allowance
EduCator Xtra will cover the actual tuition fees payable to
the educational institution subject to the maximum benefit
limits. The maximum benefit limits are adjusted every year
at the rate of education inflation, as determined by Liberty.
This rate is based each year on the average increase in
education costs in the past year. Where a child is attending
home-schooling the tuition allowance will cover the verifiable
costs of their homeschooling such as invoices, subject to the
overall maximum limit applicable for the institution type that
was selected (i.e. Public or Independent).
This will be limited to:
•
the actual registration and tuition fees charged by the
platform provider;
•
the actual costs of the textbooks that the parent and
child require to enable home-schooling; and the costs
of writing external examinations, where these are
not included in the tuition charged by the platform
provider.
•
the costs of writing external examinations, where these
are not included in the tuition charged by the platform
provider.
This excludes any costs that might be associated with
appointing any other person such as tutors or au pairs that
might be facilitating the home-schooling support or teaching
functions.
Tuition fees
The tuition fees that are covered are as follows:
•
2 years pre-primary tuition fees
•
7 years primary school tuition fees
•
5 years secondary school tuition fees
•
cover for first undergraduate tertiary qualification
obtained

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

•

residential fees at tertiary level.

Tuition fees are payable directly to the educational institution
concerned as a lump sum at the beginning of each academic
year, subject to Liberty’s business rules at the time. This may
differ if the child attends home-schooling, since there may
be multiple vendors that require payment, or the timing of
payments differ to traditional schooling institutions. Payment
will be made on receipt of invoices. If a claim is made partway
through an academic year, a pro rata payment in respect of
the remaining academic year is payable.
If the child changes institutions during the academic year after
tuition fees have already been paid, no further benefit will be
paid to the new institution for that particular year.
You can select whether EduCator Xtra should cover the costs
of the child’s education in a public, independent, or prime
educational institution. The premiums charged for EduCator
Xtra will be different depending on which option is selected
will be different depending on which option is selected.
When in claim, the child will be able to take two years off
between the end of their secondary education and starting
tertiary education.
The tuition allowance will only pay for the first undergraduate
degree for the lesser period of the standard time to get the
degree and 4 years. This means that if an undergraduate
degree takes 3 years, then the benefit will pay for 3 years.
The exception is a medical or dental undergraduate degree,
which will be paid for 6 years and 5 years respectively.
The tuition allowance for tertiary institutions will include any
National Qualifications Framework (NQF) recognised tertiary
level certificate. This will include both full-time and part-time
courses.
Only the first certificate or undergraduate degree/diploma
will be covered. If two or more certificates are done
simultaneously the most expensive certificate will be
covered. Payments will be made irrespective of the child
passing or not. However, the maximum number of years
covered per education level will apply. If a claim is accepted
partway through the schooling career the maximum
number of years will reduce to the remaining years needed
for the child to complete the schooling level if each year is
successfully completed.
Tuition fees for undergraduate degrees will also cover the
residence fees associated with the institution.
Types of institutions are covered
EduCator covers tuition fees for a child attending either a:
• Public Institution
This institution is defined as an educational institution
owned and subsidized by the government.
• Independent Institution
An institution that is a privately owned educational
institution, not receiving any subsidies from the
government, unless specifically applied for.
• Prime Institution
Like an independent institution, this is also a privately
owned educational institution, not receiving any
subsidies from the South African government, unless
specifically applied for. The difference between this and
independent institutions is the higher benefit maximums
applicable at primary and secondary levels, owing

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

to the additional tuition fees that are charged by the
educational institution that the child is attending.
Education for special needs
If the child has to attend an institution (approved by Liberty)
for the mentally or physically disabled, Liberty will cover
the tuition fees for that institution subject to a maximum
Rand limit of 150% of the normal maximum Rand limits
determined by Liberty from time to time.
International study
If a Child beneficiary emigrates whilst in-claim, Liberty will
pay for the tuition fees at recognised pre-primary, primary,
secondary and international tertiary institutions that are
not on the approved list of international institutions. Benefit
payments will be subject to the maximum Rand limits of
public, independent and prime categories of education
and South African tertiary institutions (as determined by
Liberty each year). Specifically for tertiary institutions, only
international tertiary institutions that are recognised by the
relevant regulatory body in the country (based on the laws
and regulations that control the establishment, recognition
and status of academic institutions in that country) will
be recognised by Liberty and eligible for continued claim
payments.
List of Approved International Tertiary institutions
Liberty will pay the tuition fees should the child attend an
approved international tertiary institution. Should the child
attend one of the international tertiary institutions on the
approved list at the time of claim, the benefit payments will
be subject to a maximum Dollar limit. This maximum Dollar
limit is updated annually, as determined by Liberty each
year. Liberty reserves the right to change the approved
list of international institutions from time to time, at its
sole discretion. Please see the policy document for the full
approved list of international institutions.
EduGlobal
This feature covers additional international tertiary institutions
that do not form part of the approved international tertiary
institution list. The additional international tertiary institutions
covered under EduGlobal, form part of the ENIC-NARIC
network, which effectively expands the choice of international
universities that a child beneficiary can attend. This feature
is not automatically included, however can be selected to
expand the coverage for the client, and pays out 3 times that
South African benefit limit.
Supplementary allowances
The supplementary allowance will be paid annually for the
entire duration of the child beneficiary’s education starting
from primary education and ending with tertiary education.
The allowance will be paid to the child directly. The amount
of the allowance will depend on the level of education the
child is attending and is a percentage of the Tuition allowance
subject to the maximum and minimum amounts set by
Liberty from time to time.
This may differ if the child attends home-schooling due to
the possible timing differences in tuition and examination

BENEFITS | LIFE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

payments. The resultant supplementary allowance will be
made with each tuition allowance related payment. Payment
will be made on receipt of invoices.
While in claim, Liberty will also pay an amount to help cover
other expenses associated with the child’s education. These
expenses may include:
• Extra-mural activities
• Extra lessons
• Uniforms
• Stationery
• Textbooks
• Transportation costs

Primary school supplementary allowance limits are:
Supplementary allowance will pay an amount of 15% of
Tuition fees, up to the applicable maximum annual limit.
The Supplementary allowance will always pay a minimum
of R8 000, where the calculation of 15% of Tuition fees
falls below R8 000. The current maximum annual limit for
primary school supplementary allowance is R 15 410.
Secondary School supplementary allowance limits are:
Supplementary allowance will pay an amount of 17.5% of
Tuition fees, up to the applicable maximum annual limit.
The Supplementary allowance will always pay a minimum
of R8 000, where the calculation of 17.5% of Tuition fees
falls below R8 000. The current maximum annual limit for
secondary school supplementary allowance is R 23 140.
Tertiary education supplementary allowance:
The full supplementary annual limit of R 12 350 is paid for
tertiary education.
Early childhood development allowance:
This benefit will be payable prior to when a child starts
formal schooling. This mean that:
• It will be paid if the Child Beneficiary is younger than 7
years old (age next) who does not yet qualify for the
tuition allowance.
• The payout covers the cost of early childhood
development subject to maximum limits determined by
Liberty every year.
• This allowance can cover the cost of nursery schools,
day care, and similar institutions.
• This allowance will cease once the child starts formal
pre-primary tuition.
Achievement allowance
This benefit is intended to reward a child that excels in a
sport, cultural or other activity and represents a South
African province (i.e. provincial level) or South Africa (i.e.
national level). The allowance is payable as a lump sum
directly to the child and is payable until the end of secondary
education. The allowance is structured as follows:
•
It will only be paid in respect of the year in which the
provincial or national colours are awarded, and it is only
paid once a year.
• If the child beneficiary receives provincial and national
colours in the same academic year, then the total
allowance payable will be limited to the national colours
awarded, irrespective of how many times colours are

awarded in that year.
The Achievement allowance is payable for South African
colours only. Proof that the colours were awarded, in the
form of a certificate from the relevant regulatory body,
must be submitted.

•

If you add the benefit after the policy has been issued, it
will be subject to underwriting. However, if at least one
other child is already covered under EduCator Xtra:
You can add EduCator Xtra for a newborn child as
a beneficiary without underwriting, provided the
child is born within 5 years of the date you were last
underwritten. Liberty reserves the right to call for an HIV
test on you (and your Second Life Assured), which must
have a negative result. The newborn child will need to
be added to the benefit within 4 months of being born.
If you or your Second Life Assured die (or become
disabled) while you are expecting another child, your
unborn child will become eligible for EduCator Xtra.
The benefit will be restricted to children born within a
9-month period from the date of death (or disability).

If all your other children are covered for the costs of
education at an independent institution, your unborn
child will also be covered for the costs of education at an
independent institution. If any of your other children are
covered for the costs of education at a public institution,
your unborn child will also be covered for the costs of
education at a public institution.
Please note: If you add the EduCator Xtra after the policy
has been issued, it will be subject to underwriting.

to the benefit limits and business rules at the time of the
exercising the option. Normal financial, occupational, and
territorial underwriting will still apply.
A claim for any pre-existing diagnosed medical condition,

To qualify for these benefits, the Child Beneficiary will have

benefit ending or within three months of completion of their

Premiums for these benefits will be calculated based on

benefit will now cover the premiums towards Jackie and
Jonah’s education. Three months later the third child, Janine
is born. Mrs Johnson informs Liberty of the new addition to
the family and Janine’s future educational costs will also be
covered by Liberty.
Jackie finishes primary school in this year. The next 5 years
she attends a private school in Johannesburg. Directly after
finishing school, she enrolls for a 3-year undergraduate
degree in Marketing. She also enrolls as a resident at the
university hostel for this period.
During this time Liberty will cover the following costs
towards Jackie’s education:
Cashback payout
The Cashback on all premiums paid up until date of claim
would be paid from the date of claim.
Primary education:
• The pro rata fees applicable for the remainder of the
academic year
• Supplementary allowance for the remaining 6 months of
the year.
Secondary education:
• The actual tuition fees of the private secondary school for
the full 5 years of secondary education
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
Tertiary education:
• The tuition fees for the 3-year undergraduate degree
• Residence fees for 3 years
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
The above costs will be covered, subject to the maximum
benefit limits applicable each year.
After the 3 years of tertiary education EduCator Xtra will
cease and no further benefits will be payable. At this time
Jackie will be able to purchase Life Cover, critical illness,
impairment and/or lump sum disability benefits, free of
underwriting (except for an HIV and smoker status test).
Jonah finishes his secondary education but has to do Grade
12 twice. In Grade 9 and 10 Jonah receives Provincial colours
in athletics. While in Grade 11, Jonah receives Provincial as
well as National colours in athletics. After finishing school,
Jonah decides to take 2 years off to explore Europe,
whereafter he decides to start work immediately and not to
continue with his studies.

During this time Liberty will cover the following costs
towards Jonah’s education:
Cashback payout
The Cashback on all premiums paid up until date of claim
would be paid from the date of claim.
Secondary education:
• The pro rata tuition fees for the remaining Grade 10 year
i.e. last 6 months of the year
• Tuition fees for Grade 11 and 12; the second attempt at
Grade 12 is not covered
• Supplementary Allowance – 6 months for Grade 10 and
full allowance for Grade 11 and 12.
Achievement allowance:
• Payment towards the Provincial colours received in
Grade 10
• Payment towards the Provincial colours received in
Grade 11
• Payment towards the National colours received in Grade
11 - the amount will be the difference between the
amount payable for National colours and the amount
already paid towards the Provincial colours.
The above costs will be covered, subject to the maximum
benefit limits applicable each year.
While Jonah is touring Europe his benefit will remain intact
for two years. Since Jonah doesn’t proceed with tertiary
education after the 2 years, EduCator Xtra ceases. At this
time Jonah will be able to purchase Life Cover, critical illness,
impairment and/or lump sum disability benefits, free of
underwriting (except for an HIV and smoker status test).
For Janine, the unborn child at the time of Mr Johnson’s
death, Liberty will pay the following for Janine’s education:
Early Childhood Development Allowance:
The allowance will pay for nursery school before formal
education begins.
Pre-primary education:
The tuition fees for two academic years at a public
institution.
Primary education:
• The actual school fees of the public primary school for
the full 7 years of primary education
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
Secondary education:
• The actual school fees of the private secondary school
for the full 5 years of secondary education
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
Tertiary education:
• The tuition fees for her first undergraduate degree (up
to a maximum of 4 years (not applicable to degrees in
medicine and dentistry)
• Residence fees for the period of the undergraduate
degree
• Annual Supplementary Allowance.
The above costs will be covered, subject to the maximum
benefit limits applicable each year.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

4.2 LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR
4.2.1 LUMP SUM RANGE
CAPITAL DISABILITY
Benefit Description

Capital Disability pays you a lump sum if you are occupationally disabled. There are two definitions
of occupational disability:
• Own occupation disability (OOD)
• Own or any reasonable occupation disability (OD).
If you have selected either a Lifestyle Protector (Term) policy or a Lifestyle Protector (Term with
PayOut) policy, only Capital Disability (OOD) can be selected.
If you have selected the Whole of Life cover period, once you are retired the nature of your cover will
change. After retirement, if you become permanently impaired as defined in the policy document,
you will receive a monthly benefit which increases each year with inflation (as measured by CPI
capped at 10.5%) for the remainder of your life. The amount of your monthly benefit is a proportion of
the sum assured and depends on the severity of your impairment.
No post-retirement claims will be accepted on Term benefits.

Benefit Type

This is an accelerated benefit that can be added to either or both of the Life Cover and the
Renewable Life Cover(1) benefits on the policy.
If you have selected a term benefit, this benefit reduces the total of your Life Cover (Term) sum
assured by the amount of any claim. Your other accelerated benefits may also be affected by claiming
since the maximum accelerated benefit cover allowed is restricted to the amount of your Life Cover
(Term) sum assured.
If you have selected the To end of the month of the Life Assured’s: 65th or 70th birthday cover
period, or if you have selected the Whole of Life cover period and claim before retirement, this benefit
reduces the total of your Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1) by the amount of any claim. Your
other accelerated benefits may also be affected by claiming since the maximum accelerated benefit
cover allowed is restricted to the amount of your Life Cover plus your Renewable Life Cover(1).
If you have selected the Whole of Life cover period, any claims for this benefit after retirement will not
reduce your Life Cover or Renewable Life Cover(1) even though it is an accelerated benefit.

Cover Period

• Whole of Life
• To: end of the month of the Life Assured’s: 65th or 70th birthday
• Term: Minimum - 5 years.
Maximum - Lesser of 70 minus age next birthday and 25 years.

Benefit Limits

Minimum: R100 000
Maximum: 	Term cover, To end of the month of the Life Assured’s: 65th and 70th birthday: R60
million (aggregated over all Loss of Income Protection benefits)
	Whole of Life cover period: R9 million (aggregated over all Loss of Income Protection
benefits)
	If the Whole of Life cover period is selected on both the Income Protector benefit and
Capital Disability benefit, the sum of 1.5% of the Capital Disability sum assured and
50% of the Income Protector sum assured cannot exceed 55% of after-tax insurable
monthly income.
