hand at the MP joint in an accident and submits a claim.
This does not render him permanently occupationally
disabled, rather he is permanently impaired. His claim
therefore qualifies for a 50% benefit ( R700 000) payout under The Limbs impairment benefit category. At
the first policy anniversary the sum assured increases
by 7% to R1 070 000.
Mr Smith is diagnosed with terminal cancer 18 months
after the inception of the policy and is assessed as
permanently occupationally disabled. The claim is valid
for a 100% pay-out, however 50% of the original sum
assured has already been paid. Now, the increased
sum assured is used to calculate the value of the claim
pay-out. Therefore, the remaining portion of the sum
assured (R1 070 000 x 50% = R535 000) is paid and
the benefit ceases.

After I suffer an impairment and claim, is there a waiting
period before I can receive my benefit?
No. Your claim will be paid as soon as Liberty has established
the permanence of the condition you are claiming for.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

What impact does my occupation have on my benefit?
Your occupation has a direct bearing on your ability to claim
and hence your eventual benefit.
EXAMPLE

Mr Jones, who is a bank manager, has an accident and
loses his dominant hand. While the injury would impair
his ability to play golf, this injury might not affect Mr
Jones’s ability to permanently perform his occupational
duties. If Mr Jones could still perform his occupational
duties, he would not be able to claim for occupational
disability. However, Mr Jones would be able to claim
for his permanent impairment, and 50% of the sum
assured would be paid under the impairment benefit
category, The Limbs. If Mr Jones were a surgeon, this
would seriously affect his ability to perform surgery in
the future and is a permanent condition. In this case,
he suffers both occupational disability and impairment.
Liberty would pay the maximum benefit (100%) on Mr
Jones’s claim in this instance. After which, the benefit
will cease.
The Absolute Protector Plus benefit meets different
needs before and after retirement. Before retirement the
benefit protects you against the risk that you are unable to
earn an income due to a disability or impairment. Usually
people will have some form of debt before retirement, for
example a house or car loan. A lump sum payment can help
settle that debt immediately should you suffer a disability or
impairment which prevents you from being able to earn an
income. After retirement, you no longer face the risk of not
being able to work but you do face the risk that you suffer
a serious impairment which leads to additional, unforeseen
expenses such as the cost of a nurse to care for you, or
a driver to transport you. These types of expenses will
effectively reduce your retirement income. Since you are not
likely to have any debt after retirement and that your new
expenses will be incurred predominantly on a monthly basis
a monthly benefit amount more closely meets your need for
protection.
Can I select the Whole of Life cover period on both
the Absolute Protector Plus benefit and the Income
Protector benefit?
Yes, you may select the Whole of Life cover period on both
the Income Protector benefit and the Absolute Protector
Plus benefit, provided that:
• 1.5% of the Absolute Protector Plus sum assured; plus
• 50% of the Income Protector sum assured
do not exceed 55% of your after-tax insurable monthly
income.

Whole of Life cover period: When will I be
considered retired for this benefit?
When assessing claims for this benefit you will be
considered retired:
•
•

At the policy anniversary immediately preceding your
70th birthday; or
If you are over the age of 55 and you have stopped
active or full-time engagement in your occupation (but

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

not due to the impairment that you are claiming for).
Your claim amount will be equal to 0.5%, 1% or 1.5% of your
benefit amount depending on the type of impairment you
suffer and the severity of the impairment. Your benefit
amount will increase at each claim anniversary by inflation
(as measured by CPI and capped at 10.5% per annum).
More information on the levels of payout, can be found in
the Impairment Benefit Categories section. However, you
should always consult your policy document for the claim
percentages applicable to your policy.
EXAMPLE

Mr Mashego has the Absolute Protector Plus
benefit with a Whole of Life cover period and a sum
assured of R1 000 000. At age 75 Mr Mashego suffers
a brain injury and starts to suffer from permanent
receptive aphasia and is partially unable to comprehend
language symbols. This condition qualifies Mr Mashego
for the 0.5% monthly benefit level. This means that
Mr Mashego will be paid R5 000 per month
(i.e., R1 000 000 x 0.5%) increasing by inflation every
year for the remainder of his life.
If Mr Mashego’s aphasia condition had been more
serious and he was totally unable to comprehend
language symbols he would have qualified for the 1.5%
monthly benefit level. In this case his initial monthly
claim amount would be R15 000 rather than the
R5 000 described above.
Are there restrictions as to which Loss of Income
Protection benefits I can select on my policy?
You may select only one of the lump sum Loss of Income
Protection benefits on your policy.
On a Lifestyle Protector policy you can choose either:
• Capital Disability, or
• Capital Disability Plus, or
• Impairment, or
• Impairment Plus, or
• Absolute Protector, or
• Absolute Protector Plus, or
• Absolute Impairment, or
• Absolute Impairment Plus, or
• Vital Living, or
• Vital Living Plus.
On a Lifestyle Protector (Term) or Lifestyle Protector (Term
with PayOut) policy you can choose either:
• Capital Disability, or
• Capital Disability Plus, or
• Absolute Protector, or
• Absolute Protector Plus, or
• Absolute Impairment, or
• Absolute Impairment Plus.
Your Second Life Assured may choose a different lump sum
Loss of Income Protection benefit to you. Income Range
benefits can be selected even if you already have a lump
sum Loss of Income Protection benefit on your Lifestyle
Protector policy.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

What is the Absolute Impairment benefit?
Absolute Impairment provides you with a lump sum to
cover the loss of your ability to earn an income, should you
suffer from a permanent impairment that is covered under
this benefit. Impairment refers to the loss of function in any
part of the body, whether it is physical, functional or mental.
This is an accelerated benefit that can be added to either
or both of Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1). This
benefit reduces the total of your Life Cover and Renewable
Life Cover(1) benefit by the amount of any claim. Your other
accelerated benefits may also be affected by claiming since
the maximum accelerated benefit cover allowed is restricted
to the amount of your Life Cover plus your Renewable Life
Cover(1) benefits.

EXAMPLE

Mr Jones has Life Cover with a sum assured of R3
million and Absolute Impairment with a sum assured
of R1 000 000. He loses both of his legs in a motor
vehicle accident 6 months after the inception of the
policy.
His Absolute Impairment benefit will pay the full sum
assured of R1 000 000. His Absolute Impairment
benefit will cease and his Life Cover sum assured will
be reduced to R2 000 000.

What are the Benefit Categories?

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What type of cover does the Absolute Impairment
benefit provide?
Absolute Impairment is intended to protect you against
potential loss of income. The lump sum payment is paid if
you suffer from permanent Impairment rather than whether
you are occupationally disabled. Absolute Impairment
provides a comprehensive benefit which pays your full sum
assured if you meet the specified impairment criteria. The
fact that the benefit is not tiered makes it ideal for business
assurance purposes.
What is the Activities of Daily Living (ADL) Catch-All
Benefit Category?
The Activities of Daily Living (ADL) Catch-All Benefit
Category is one of the Impairment Benefit Categories. In
this Benefit Category claims are assessed by considering
your ability to perform activities of daily life (known as ADLs)
rather than medical criteria. ADLs are made up of “Basic
ADLs” such as washing and dressing and “Instrumental ADLs”
such as telephone use and housekeeping. The aim of this
Benefit Category is to provide you with additional security
that should your impairment be serious enough to cause
you to be unable to perform a required number of Basic
and Instrumental ADLs, but is not listed in any of the other
benefit categories, your claim will still be assessed. More
information on the Activities of Daily Living (ADL) CatchAll Benefit Category is included in the Impairment Benefit
Categories section. However, you should always consult
your policy document for the specific claim percentages
applicable to your policy document.
Additional claims payment security:
Activities of Daily Living

Cardiovascular System

•

•

Impairment benefit section. However you should always

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

EXAMPLE

Mr Smith contracts a very rare and unusual disease
at age 35. The disease is not included explicitly in
the list of impairments covered by his Absolute
Impairment benefit. It is, however, severely, and
permanently impacting Mr Smith’s ability to lead a
normal life. Mr Smith’s Impairment will be assessed
under the Activities of Daily Living (ADL) Catch-All
Benefit Category. His claim will be assessed based on
the number of Basic and Instrumental ADLs he cannot
perform. If Mr Smith is unable to permanently perform
3 Basic ADLs or 4 Instrumental ADLs, he would be paid
his full sum assured.

After I suffer from an impairment and claim, is there a
waiting period before I can receive my benefit?
No. Your claim will be paid as soon as Liberty has established
the permanence of the condition you are claiming for.
What is the difference between the Absolute
Impairment benefit and the Impairment benefit?
The Absolute Impairment benefit and the Impairment
benefit both provide a benefit when an injury or disease
affects your functionality.
The Impairment benefit is a tiered benefit. This means that
the proportion of your sum assured that you receive as a
payment under the Impairment benefit will depend on
the severity of your Impairment. In contrast, the Absolute
Impairment benefit is a non-tiered benefit. This means that
any payment you receive under the Absolute Impairment
benefit will always be for the full sum assured.
Impairments that are less severe may qualify for a payout
on Impairment but not qualify for a payout on Absolute
Impairment.
EXAMPLE

Suppose Mr Jones loses his thumb through the MP joint
in a motor vehicle accident. The Impairment benefit
will pay out 25% of the sum assured. This impairment
is not severe enough to qualify for a payment under
Absolute Impairment.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

However, Absolute Impairment generally provides a 100%
payout at a lower severity of impairment than the
Impairment benefit.
EXAMPLE

Mr Jones is diagnosed with Schizophrenia, has had to
be institutionalised 3 times in the last 12 months, and
his GAF score is less than 40. Impairment will pay 50%
of the sum assured. Absolute Impairment will pay out
100% of the sum assured.
While both benefits provide comprehensive cover to replace
a loss of earnings, each has its own claims criteria. Depending
on your needs, one benefit might be more suitable than the
other.
What is the difference between the Absolute
Impairment benefit and the Capital Disability benefit?
The Absolute Impairment benefit provides a benefit
when an injury or disease affects your functionality. Capital
Disability provides a benefit when the injury or disease
affects your ability to perform the duties of your occupation.
While both benefits provide comprehensive cover to replace
a loss of earnings, each has its own claims criteria. Depending
on your needs, one benefit might be more suitable than the
other.
EXAMPLE

Suppose that Mr Jones is involved in a motor vehicle
accident and loses both his legs. Absolute Impairment
will provide Mr Jones with 100% of the sum assured.
Capital Disability will provide Mr Jones with a benefit
if the loss of his legs affected his ability to do the duties
of his occupation.

•

amenable to colostomy.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

BASIC ADLS
Washing

The ability to wash oneself without physical assistance or supervision. This includes transferring in
and out of the bath or shower.

Dressing

The ability to independently put on or take off all garments, including the securing and unfastening
thereof. Where appropriate, this includes any braces, prosthesis or other surgical appliance.

Feeding / eating

The ability to eat independently once food has been prepared and made available.

Continence

The ability to manage bowel and bladder functions such that an adequate level of personal hygiene
can be maintained.

Mobility

The ability to move independently between indoor rooms on a level surface with the assistance of a
walking aid, including a wheelchair

Transferring

The ability to independently transfer from the bed to a chair with the assistance of a walking aid.
INSTRUMENTAL ADLS

Telephone use
(communication)

The ability to use a telephone independently. This includes answering the phone on an incoming call,
being able to hold a basic conversation, as well as the ability to dial a well-known or written down
number.

Food preparation

The ability to prepare and serve simple, everyday meals. This includes the ability to perform simple
measurements, preparation activities and having awareness of general kitchen safety.

Housekeeping

The ability to perform light household chores such as making a bed, washing dishes and maintaining a
reasonable level of cleanliness.

Transport

The ability to safely drive a car (including getting into and out of the car) or the ability to use public
transport, including being able to provide a drop-off address, and reaching a public transport pick-up
point.

Handling finances

The ability to perform basic transactions such as purchasing daily consumables, including the ability to
perform a basic calculation of what money is required to pay for purchases and what change is due.

Writing

The ability to take down a simple message or complete a simple form requiring personal details such
as name, address and date of birth.

VITAL LIVING
Benefit Description

Vital Living pays you the full sum assured if you are unable to perform a number of specified daily
activities, are permanently impaired or are diagnosed with Cancer as defined in the policy document.
Vital Living has a built-in conversion option which allows you to convert the benefit, free of
underwriting, into more comprehensive lump sum disability and critical illness cover on certain
specified events as well as at the end of the cover period.

Benefit Type

This is an accelerated benefit that can be added to either or both of the Life Cover and the
Renewable Life Cover(1) benefits. This benefit reduces the total of your Life Cover and Renewable
Life Cover(1) by the amount of any claim. Your other accelerated benefits may be affected by claiming
since the maximum accelerated benefit cover is restricted to the amount of your Life Cover plus your
Renewable Life Cover(1) benefit.

Cover Period

Until the policy anniversary preceding your 40th birthday, or until the benefit is converted if sooner.

Benefit Limits

Minimum: R100 000
Maximum: R1 500 000

Premium Pattern

•
•
•
•

Age Rated Premium Pattern
Fixed Increasing Premium Pattern (Level Premium Pattern applies)

Conversion Option

The Vital Living benefit can be converted into lump sum disability and/or critical illness benefits on
certain specified events. The conversion will be free of underwriting but will be subject to the normal
business rules for the benefits converted into.

The premiums for the benefits converted into will be calculated using the Life Assured’s age at the
time of conversion.
Conversion before the end of the cover period (Voluntary conversion)
The policyholder has the option to convert the Vital Living benefit within:
•
•
•
•

1 month of any policy anniversary before the end of the term of the benefit
3 months of the life assured getting married
3 months of the life assured becoming a parent (natural or adopted)
3 months of the life assured (or their spouse) purchasing a new home.

For every R1 sum assured of Vital Living benefit, the policyholder can convert into up to R1.50 sum
assured of:
Capital Disability; or
Impairment; or
Absolute Impairment; or
Absolute Protector

and / or

Living Lifestyle (with or
without Top-Up and Extended
options)

All cease age options of the benefits above are available to be chosen.
The necessary Life Cover or Renewable Life Cover(1) must be available to support the desired sum
assured.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

VITAL LIVING (CONTINUED)
Conversion Option (cont.)

On voluntary conversion, a pre-existing condition exclusion will apply to the new benefits. If medical
advice was sought within the 12-month period prior to a voluntary conversion for a condition that is
claimed for after conversion, the claim will be assessed according to the Vital Living claims criteria
and not the new benefits claims criteria. The sum assured will be limited to the final Vital Living sum
assured before conversion and not the current sum assured of the benefits converted into.
Standard conversion at the end of the cover period (Automatic conversion)
If the policyholder has not informed Liberty about which benefits they would like to convert into at
the end of the term of the benefit, the following standard conversion will automatically take place.
For every R1 sum assured of Vital Living benefit, the policyholder will be converted into:
• R1 Impairment (To end of the month of the Life Assured’s 65th birthday); and
• R1 Living Lifestyle with Top-Up and Extended options (Whole of Life).

Premium Payment Period

Until the benefit expires.

Waiting Period

No waiting period applies. Your claim will be paid as soon as Liberty has established the permanence
of the condition you are claiming for.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

What is the Vital Living benefit?
Vital Living provides you with a lump sum to cover your loss
of earnings should you, as per the policy document:
•

Become permanently unable to perform a specified
number of Activities of Daily Living (ADLs); or

•

Become permanently impaired; or

•

Are diagnosed with Cancer in an advanced stage.

Activities of Daily Living
Specified Impairment
conditions
Cancer in an advanced stage

The principal claims assessment criteria of the benefit are
your ability to perform the Activities of Daily Living. However,
to provide additional security that claims will be paid for
certain events, conditions such as deafness, blindness and
heart attacks are covered under the specified impairment
conditions and advanced Cancer is covered under the
Cancer claims criteria.
EXAMPLE

Miss Odebe purchases the Vital Living benefit with
Life Cover, each with a sum assured of R1 000 000.
If Miss Odebe is in a car accident and cannot perform 3
basic Activities of Daily Living (ADLs) or 4 instrumental
ADLs, the Vital Living benefit will pay out R1 000 000.
Suppose instead that Miss Odebe suffers severe burns
covering more than 20% of her body surface area, the
Vital Living benefit will pay out R1 000 000.
Suppose instead that Miss Odebe is diagnosed with
Stage 3 Lung Cancer, the Vital Living benefit will pay
out R1 000 000.
In each of the scenarios above, Miss Odebe’s Vital
Living benefit would end after paying out the
R1 000 000 and the payment of the Vital Living
would also reduce her Life Cover by R1 000 000 as it
is an accelerated benefit.
As can be seen from the example above, Vital Living
therefore provides broad coverage against a severe disability,
impairment or cancer.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

The Vital Living benefit also has built-in conversion
options which allow the policyholder to convert into more
comprehensive benefits over time.
This is an accelerated benefit that can be added to either
or both of Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1). This
benefit reduces the total of your Life Cover and Renewable
Life Cover(1) benefit by the amount of any claim.
Who should buy the Vital Living benefit?
The Vital Living benefit is a simple, affordable and flexible
benefit. The benefit is ideal for younger clients who have not
reached life events such as getting married, becoming a
parent or purchasing a home and don’t have the need,
or don’t have the disposable income available, for more
comprehensive benefits at their current life stage. For a very
affordable premium, the Vital Living benefit protects you
against a severe disability, impairment or Cancer event and at
the same time offers you the flexibility to convert into more
comprehensive benefits as the need arises without having to
submit medical evidence.
How does the Vital Living benefit compare with the
other impairment and critical illness benefits available
on Lifestyle Protector?
Vital Living offers broad coverage against severe disabilities,
impairments or Cancers and the impact these may
have on your ability to earn an income in the future.
However, there are other, more specialised benefits
available on the Lifestyle Protector product which
offer more comprehensive cover within each of these
areas. For example, Absolute Protector provides more
comprehensive lump sum income protection cover than
the Vital Living benefit and the Living Lifestyle benefit
provides more comprehensive critical illness cover than the
Vital Living benefit. If you want the most comprehensive
cover possible, a combination of these benefits would be
more suitable than the Vital Living benefit.
Will I always receive my full sum assured on a successful
claim?
Yes, for any successful Vital Living benefit claim you will be
paid your full sum assured.
The Renewable Life Cover option is closed to New Business from July 2022.

(1)

(2)

The Renewable Premium Pattern is closed to New Business from July 2022.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

How long will I be covered?
The Vital Living benefit will end on the policy anniversary
preceding your 40th birthday. However, cover can
effectively be extended through other benefits by taking
advantage of the built-in conversion options on the Vital
Living benefit.
How do the conversion options on Vital Living work?
One of the key features of the Vital Living benefit is its
flexibility. By including built-in conversion options, the Vital
Living benefit has been designed to enable you to easily
increase the level of your insurance protection as you reach
life stages which demand it. During the term of your Vital
Living benefit you can convert your benefit at any of the
following points:
•

Within 1 month of any policy anniversaries

•

Within 3 months of you getting married

•

Within 3 months of you becoming a parent

•

Within 3 months of you or your spouse purchasing a
new home.

When you decide to exercise your conversion option, you
have a large degree of flexibility in which benefits you would
like to convert into and how much you would like to convert
into. For every R1 sum assured of Vital Living benefit you
have, you can choose to convert your benefit into up to
R1.50 sum assured of:
Capital Disability; or
Impairment; or
Absolute Impairment;
or
Absolute Protector

and / or

Living Lifestyle (with
or without Top-Up and
Extended options)

Lump sum disability:

Vital Living
benefit

Capital Disability
Impairment
Absolute Impairment
Absolute Protector

Critical illness:

and/or

Living Lifestyle
Top-Up Option
Extended Option

Conversion:

Marriage, children, buying a house
or a policy anniversary

When you convert your benefits, the premiums for your new
benefits will be calculated using your age at the time of the
conversion.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

EXAMPLE

Miss Odebe is aged 22. She decides to purchase the
Vital Living benefit with a sum assured of R1 000 000
because she feels she needs some broad protection
against the risk that she suffers some form of severe
traumatic event which results in her being unable to lead
a normal life. However, she has no dependants and feels
she does not need more comprehensive (and more
expensive) coverage at this stage of her life.
After 3 years Miss Odebe gets married and soon after
has children. At this point she decides that she needs
more comprehensive cover. Below are some possibilities
that she could convert her Vital Living benefit (without
submitting any medical evidence), into:
Example 1
R1 500 000 Absolute Protector (OOD) (Whole of
Life) and R1 500 000 Living Lifestyle (To end of the
month of the Life Assured’s 65th birthday) with the
Top-Up and Extended options.
Example 2
R1 500 000 Capital Disability (OD) (To end of the month
of the Life Assured’s 70th birthday) and R 875 000 Living
Lifestyle (Whole of Life) with the Extended option.
Example 3
No lump sum disability benefit but R1 500 000 Living
Lifestyle (Whole of Life) with the Top-Up option.
Example 4
R1 100 000 Impairment (To end of the month of
the Life Assured’s 65th birthday) and no critical illness
cover.

It is important to note that if you choose to convert your
benefit, any conditions that you sought medical advice for in
the 12 months before the conversion, and then subsequently
claim for after conversion, will be assessed according to the
Vital Living benefit’s cover periods and conditions and not
the new benefits’ terms and conditions. The sum assured
used for assessing these claims will be the final Vital Living
benefit sum assured and not the sums assured of the new
benefits.
Conversion at last policy anniversary
At the policy anniversary before your 40th birthday the
Vital Living benefit comes to an end. If at this stage, you
have not selected which benefits to select as part of your
conversion option, Liberty will exercise the standard
conversion at this policy anniversary on your behalf. The
standard conversion is that your Vital Living benefit will be
converted into an equivalent sum assured of Impairment
(To end of the month of the Life Assured’s 65th birthday)
cover and an equivalent sum assured of Living
Lifestyle (Whole of Life) with the Top-Up and Extended
options cover. You still do have the full flexibility to choose a
different combination of benefits, or a different combination
of sum assureds, or even choose not to convert at all at the
end of your Vital Living cover period. You simply need to make
Liberty aware of your choice before that policy anniversary.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

EXAMPLE

Miss Jones has the Vital Living benefit with a sum
assured of R1 000 000. She is nearing the policy
anniversary before her 40th birthday. She receives a
letter from Liberty informing her that her R1 000 000
Vital Living benefit will convert into an Impairment

Lifestyle (with the Top-Up and Extended options) with a
Whole of Life cover period.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What is the level of Cancer cover that the Vital Living
benefit provides?
In addition to the Activities of Daily Living-based cover, and the specific impairment conditions covered, the Vital Living
benefit also provides cover against Cancer in an advanced stage as a third level of protection. Broadly speaking the Vital
Living benefit provides protection against Stage 3 and Stage 4 Cancers. More specific information is summarised in the table
below and in the Vital Living Benefit Categories section (including information on how payouts relate to SCIDEP payout
requirements). However, you should always consult your policy document to see which claims criteria apply to your specific
benefit.
CANCER: Any malignant tumour characterised by the uncontrolled growth and spread of malignant cells and invasion of
normal tissue. Unequivocal histological evidence and, where appropriate, clinical evidence of invasive malignancy must be
provided. The classifications are based on the latest edition of the AJCC Cancer Staging Manual.
Specifically excluded

What effectively is included

• All cancers described as benign, low malignant potential or insitu (Tis) or (Ta) and all pre-malignant conditions.
• All cancers / tumours that have not reached stage III according
to the general classification of the American Joint Committee
for Cancer.
• All tumours of the prostate unless they have progressed to at
least clinical TNM classification T4N0M0 (stage IV) or where
lymph node involvement has been confirmed.
• Chronic lymphocytic leukaemia Stage 0, I, and II (Binet A, B, Low
Risk CLL-IPI and intermediate risk CLL-IPI requiring treatment)
• Hodgkins/Non Hodgkins lymphoma Stage I and II on Ann Arbor
classification.
• Multiple melanoma Stage I and II on the Durie-Salmon scale.
• Chronic myeloid leukaemia with no bone marrow
transplantation.
• Hairy cell leukaemia.
• All non-melanoma’s including Stage I and II malignant
melanomas.
• All myelodysplastic syndromes and myeloproliferative
neoplasms including but not limited to, essential
thrombocythemia, primary myelofibrosis, polycythemia vera
• Primary cutaneous lymphoma and dermatofibrosarcoma which
are confined to the skin and which have not spread to the lymph
nodes or distant sites
• All cancers only identified from tumour cells, pieces of DNA, or
any other biomarkers, any of which may be present in the blood,
saliva, urine, or other bodily fluids, including, but not limited to,
tests known as “liquid biopsies”.

All Cancers/tumours that have reached Stage III according to the
general classification of the American Joint Committee for
Cancer.*
• All tumours of the prostrate that have progressed to at least
clinical TNM classification T4NOMO (Stage IV) or where lymph
node involvement has been confirmed.*
• Chronic Lymphocytic Leukaemia Stage III or more on the Rai
classification.*
• Hodgkins/Non Hodgkins Lymphoma Stage III or more on the
Ann Arbor classification.*
• Multiple Myeloma Stage III or more on the Durie-Salmon scale.*
• Chronic Myeloid Leukaemia requiring bone marrow
• transplant.*
• Skin Cancers where malignant melanoma has been histologically
classified as having caused invasion beyond the epidermis.*

* And not being specifically excluded under one of the other specific exclusions

VITAL LIVING PLUS
Benefit Description

Vital Living Plus pays you the full sum assured if you are unable to perform a number of specified
daily activities, are permanently impaired or are diagnosed with Cancer as defined in the policy
document.
Vital Living Plus has built-in conversion options which allow you to convert the benefit, free of
underwriting, into more comprehensive lump sum disability and critical illness cover on certain
specified events as well as at the end of the cover period.

Benefit Type

This is a core risk protection benefit and is a non-accelerated benefit.

Cover Period

Until the policy anniversary preceding your 40th birthday, or until the benefit is converted if sooner.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What is the Vital Living Plus benefit?
Vital Living Plus provides you with a lump sum to cover
your loss of earnings should you, as per the policy document:
•

Become permanently unable to perform a specified
number of Activities of Daily Living (ADLs); or

•

Become permanently impaired; or

•

Are diagnosed with cancer in an advanced stage.

Activities of Daily Living
Specified impairment
conditions
Cancer in an advanced stage

The principal claims assessment criteria of the benefit are
your ability to perform the Activities of Daily Living. However,
to provide additional security that claims will be paid for
certain events, conditions such as deafness, blindness and
heart attacks are covered under the specified impairment
conditions and advanced cancer is covered under the cancer
claims criteria.
EXAMPLE

Miss Odebe purchases the Vital Living Plus benefit
with a sum assured of R1 000 000.
If Miss Odebe is in a car accident and cannot perform 3
basic Activities of Daily Living (ADLs) or 4 instrumental
ADLs, the Vital Living Plus benefit will pay out
R1 000 000.
Suppose instead that Miss Odebe suffers severe burns
covering more than 20% of her body surface area, the
Vital Living Plus benefit will pay out R1 000 000.
Suppose instead that Miss Odebe is diagnosed with
Stage 3 Lung Cancer, the Vital Living Plus benefit will
pay out R1 000 000.
In each of the scenarios above, Miss Odebe’s Vital
Living Plus benefit would end after paying out the
R1 000 000 sum assured.
As can be seen from the example above, Vital Living Plus
therefore provides broad coverage against a severe disability,
impairment or cancer.

The Vital Living Plus benefit also has built-in conversion
options which allow the policyholder to convert into more
comprehensive benefits over time.
Who should buy the Vital Living Plus benefit?
The Vital Living Plus benefit is a simple, affordable and
flexible benefit. The benefit is ideal for younger clients
who have not reached life events such as getting married,
becoming a parent or purchasing a home and don’t have
the need, or don’t have the disposable income available, for
more comprehensive benefits at their current life stage. For
a very affordable premium, the Vital Living Plus benefit
protects you against a severe disability, impairment or
cancer event and at the same time offers you the flexibility
to convert into more comprehensive benefits as the need
arises without having to submit medical evidence.
How does the Vital Living Plus benefit compare with the
other impairment and critical illness benefits available
on Lifestyle Protector?
Vital Living Plus offers broad coverage against severe disabilities,
impairments or cancers and the impact these may have on your
ability to earn an income in the future. However, there are other,
more specialised benefits available on the Lifestyle Protector
product which offer more comprehensive cover within each of
these areas. For example, Absolute Protector Plus provides
more comprehensive lump sum income protection cover
than the Vital Living Plus benefit and the Living Lifestyle
Plus benefit provides more comprehensive critical illness
cover than the Vital Living Plus benefit. If you want the most
comprehensive cover possible, a combination of these benefits.
Will I always receive my full sum assured on a successful
claim?
Yes, for any successful Vital Living Plus benefit claim you
will be paid your full sum assured.
How long will I be covered?
The Vital Living Plus benefit will end on the policy
anniversary preceding your 40th birthday. However, cover
can effectively be extended through other benefits by taking
advantage of the built-in conversion options on the Vital
Living Plus benefit.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

How do the conversion options on Vital Living Plus work?
One of the key features of the Vital Living Plus benefit is its
flexibility. By including built-in conversion options, the Vital
Living Plus benefit has been designed to enable you to
easily increase the level of your insurance protection as you
reach life stages which demand it. During the term of your
Vital Living Plus benefit you can convert your benefit at any
of the following points:
•

Within 1 month of any policy anniversaries

•

Within 3 months of you getting married

•

Within 3 months of you becoming a parent (natural or
adopted)

•

Within 3 months of you or your spouse purchasing a
new home.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

Conversion at last policy anniversary
At the policy anniversary before your 40th birthday the
Vital Living Plus benefit comes to an end. If at this stage,
you have not selected which benefits to select as part of
your conversion option, Liberty will exercise the standard
conversion at this policy anniversary on your behalf. The
standard conversion is that your Vital Living Plus benefit
will be converted into an equivalent sum assured of
Impairment Plus (To end of the month of the Life Assured’s
65th birthday); cover and an equivalent sum assured of
Living Lifestyle Plus (Whole of Life) with the Top-Up and
Extended options cover. You still do have the full flexibility
to choose a different combination of benefits, or a different
combination of sum assureds, or even choose not to convert
at all at the end of your Vital Living Plus cover period. You
simply need to make Liberty aware of your choice before
that policy anniversary.
EXAMPLE

Miss Jones has the Vital Living Plus benefit with a
sum assured of R1 000 000. She is nearing the policy
anniversary before her 40th birthday. She receives a letter
from Liberty informing her that her R1 000 000 Vital
Living Plus benefit will convert into an Impairment
Plus benefit, with a To end of the month of the Life
Assured’s 65th birthday cover period, and Living
Lifestyle Plus (with the Top-Up and Extended options)
with a Whole of Life cover period.
Miss Jones has a meeting with her financial adviser and
decides that the standard conversion option that Liberty
will implement is appropriate given her financial needs.
She does not need to inform Liberty about her decision
as this conversion will automatically occur.
Suppose instead that after the meeting with her
financial adviser, Miss Jones feels that the standard
conversion option that Liberty will implement is not
appropriate. She decides that she would like Capital
Disability Plus with a Whole of Life cover period and
feels she does not need any critical illness cover. She
can inform Liberty of her decision before the policy
anniversary and the combination of benefits that she
chooses will be allowed for in the conversion instead of
the standard conversion.
(1)

The Renewable Life Cover option is closed to New Business from July 2022.

(2)

The Renewable Premium Pattern is closed to New Business from July 2022.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

VITAL LIVING AND VITAL LIVING PLUS BENEFIT CATEGORIES
These benefit categories are the latest Benefit Categories and do not necessarily apply to your
policy. To ensure you know which definitions are applicable to your policy please consult your
policy document.

1. Activities of daily living
Permanent inability to perform 3 basic activities of daily living (ADLs) or 4 instrumental ADLs.
BASIC ADLS
Washing

The ability to wash oneself without physical assistance or supervision. This includes transferring in
and out of the bath or shower.

Dressing

The ability to independently put on or take off all garments, including the securing and unfastening
thereof. Where appropriate, this includes any braces, prosthesis or other surgical appliance.

Feeding / eating

The ability to eat independently once food has been prepared and made available.

Continence

The ability to manage bowel and bladder functions such that an adequate level of personal hygiene
can be maintained.

Mobility

The ability to move independently between indoor rooms on a level surface with the assistance of a
walking aid, including a wheelchair

Transferring

The ability to independently transfer from the bed to a chair with the assistance of a walking aid.

INSTRUMENTAL ADLS
Telephone use
(communication)

The ability to use a telephone independently. This includes answering the phone on an incoming call,
being able to hold a basic conversation, as well as the ability to dial a well-known number or a number
that has been written down.

Food preparation

The ability to prepare and serve simple, everyday meals. This includes the ability to perform simple
measurements, preparation activities, and being aware of general kitchen safety.

Housekeeping

The ability to perform light household chores such as making a bed, washing dishes and maintaining a
reasonable level of cleanliness.

Transport

The ability to safely drive a car (including getting into and out of the car) or the ability to use public transport,
including being able to provide a drop-off address, and reaching a public transport pick up point.

Medication

The ability to take responsibility for use of medication as prescribed.

Handling finances

The ability to perform basic transactions such as purchasing daily consumables, including the ability
to perform a basic calculation of how much money is required to pay for purchases and how much
change is due.

The ability to perform an activity will be assessed as being able to perform it independently with assistive devices and/or
equipment where indicated and without supervision.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: LUMP SUM | OCTOBER 2025

2. Cancer in an advanced stage
Liberty will pay a claim for any malignant tumour
characterised by the uncontrolled growth and spread of
malignant cells and invasion of normal tissue. Unequivocal
histological evidence and, where appropriate, clinical
evidence of invasive malignancy must be provided. The
classifications are based on the latest edition of the AJCC
Cancer Staging Manual.
The following conditions are excluded:
•

All cancers described as benign, low malignant potential
or in-situ (Tis) or (Ta) and all pre-malignant conditions.

•

All cancers / tumours that have not reached stage III
according to the general classification of the American
Joint Committee for Cancer.

•

All tumours of the prostate unless they have progressed
to at least clinical TNM classification T4N0M0 (stage IV)
or where lymph node involvement has been confirmed.

•

Chronic lymphocytic leukaemia Stage 0, I, and II (Binet
A, B, Low Risk CLL-IPI and intermediate risk CLL-IPI
requiring treatment)

•

Hodgkins/Non Hodgkins lymphoma Stage I and II on
Ann Arbor classification.

•

Multiple Melanoma Stage I and II on the Durie-Salmon
scale.

•

Chronic myeloid leukaemia with no bone marrow
transplantation.

•

Hairy cell leukaemia.

•

All non-melanoma’s including Stage I and II malignant
melanomas.

•

All myelodysplastic syndromes and myeloproliferative
neoplasms including but not limited to, essential
thrombocythemia, primary myelofibrosis, polycythemia
vera

•

Primary cutaneous lymphoma and
dermatofibrosarcoma which are confined to the skin
and which have not spread to the lymph nodes or
distant sites

•

All cancers only identified from tumour cells, pieces of
DNA, or any other biomarkers, any of which may be
present in the blood, saliva, urine, or other bodily fluids,
including, but not limited to, tests known as “liquid
biopsies”.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Loss of Speech
Total permanent and irreversible loss of the ability to speak
as a result of physical injury or disease, or the complete
inability to understand and interpret any language symbols
(spoken or written).
Facial Disfigurement
Severe destruction of the bones and tissue of the face or
head as a result of a traumatic event, which requires multiple
medically necessary reconstructive surgeries to correct
the disfigurement. Cosmetic surgery and related medical
treatment undergone to improve appearance rather than for
medically required reasons is specifically excluded. Surgery
of any kind to the ears and nose only, is specifically excluded.

The actual undergoing of a transplant as a recipient of one
of a complete human lung. The undergoing of an organ
transplant as a result of direct or indirect alcohol or drug
abuse is excluded.

Coronary Artery Bypass Graft (CABG)
The undergoing of surgery to correct the narrowing of, or
blockage to, two or more coronary arteries by means of a
by-pass graft.
Aorta Graft Surgery
The undergoing of surgery to the thoracic or abdominal
aorta involving excision of a portion of the aorta and
replacement with a graft. Surgery to the branches of the
thoracic and abdominal aortas is excluded.
Valve Replacement
The undergoing of heart valve surgery, performed to replace
or repair one or more heart valves.
No benefit is payable where any of the above mentioned
procedures were performed through key hole, laser,
catheter-based or intra-arterial techniques or procedures.
Furthermore, angioplasty and the insertion of stents is not

Vital Living and Vital Living Plus and standardised critical illness definitions project
The Vital Living and Vital Living Plus benefits are not
pure critical illness benefits. However, the cover that these
benefits provide does overlap with some of the cover
that typical critical illness benefits provide. In these cases
the benefits comply with the critical illness definitions
(SCIDEP) recommended by the Association for Savings and
Investment SA (ASISA).

This ensures that the Vital Living benefit criteria are
objective and consistent with the minimum industry
standards adopted.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

4.2 LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR
4.2.2 INCOME RANGE

Income Range overview
Income Range is a flexible package of comprehensive benefits which aim to protect you against
a wide range of risks which threaten your income. The extent of your coverage and which risks
your income is protected against are dependent on the benefits that you have selected within
the Income Range package.

What is pre-tax monthly income?
•

Salaried individuals
Pre-tax monthly income is defined as the cost to
company, earned for the last 12 months. This is the total
cost to the Life Assured’s employer and includes all
benefits associated with employment except for the
following:
− Annual bonuses (including 13th cheques)
−

Ad hoc bonuses

−

Gratuities

−

Leave pay

−

Merit award

−

Share incentive awards

−

Bonus/incentive amount paid to the Life Assured to
retain his/her service for a specified period

−

Pension

−

Superannuation allowance

−

Retirement fund lump sums

−

Retiring allowance

−

Dividends

−

Stipends

Pre-tax monthly Income also excludes any passive
income that is not related to the income being generated
for the occupation being insured for example dividends,
interest and rental income generated from a property
that the Life Assured owns.
•

Self-employed individual or a partnership
Pre-tax monthly income is defined as the share of
the average monthly fees (and sales) earned, less
the share of cost of sales, less the share of overhead
expenses (where fees earned and costs incurred are
shared on a pro-rata basis), earned and incurred over
the last 12 months. It excludes any passive income that
is not related to the income being generated for the
occupation being insured for example dividends and
rental income generated from a property that the Life
Assured owns.

•	Self-employed professional that charges a fee for
services
Pre-tax Monthly Income is defined as the average
monthly sum of the professional fee and other income
from trading activities, less business overheads
expenses, earned and incurred for the 12 months. It
excludes any passive income that is not related to the
income being generated for the occupation being
insured for example dividends and rental income
generated from a property that the Life Assured owns.
•	Self-employed individuals, partnerships and
professionals and proof of income
For self-employed individuals, partnerships and
professionals that provide proof of their income at the
inception or reinstatement of the Income Protector
benefit and whenever the sum assured is increased,
Liberty will allow an increase to the pre-tax monthly
income by an amount of up to 20% of the Life Assured’s
share of monthly business expenses that will continue
to be incurred in the Life Assured’s personal capacity
after a disability or permanent impairment event occurs.
Expenses that are insured under any other insurance
benefit that aims to cover business expenses on the
Life Assured’s disability may not be included. Personal
expenses that are not related to business activities
may not be included. Expenses that will not continue
in the Life Assured’s personal capacity after a disability
or permanent impairment event occurs may not be
included.
What is after-tax monthly income?
This is the pre-tax monthly income as defined above less
the tax payable on the pre-tax monthly income earned on
account of the Life Assured’s employment, or any services
rendered by the Life Assured.
What is the estimated after-tax income?
This is the estimated after-tax monthly income as
determined by Liberty after applying the tax tables
published by the South African Revenue Services (SARS)
that apply at that time, to the Life Assured’s pre-tax monthly
income. Allowance is also made for employer and employee

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

retirement contributions towards pension or provident
funds which attracts tax relief. This estimated after-tax
monthly income is determined when pre-tax monthly
income is provided.
What is the insurable monthly income?
This is an amount used in the calculation of the maximum
amounts that are payable under this benefit and is equal to
the higher of the estimated after-tax monthly income and
the actual after-tax monthly income as defined above.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What happens if I do not know my after-tax income?
If you do not have your after-tax income easily available,
Liberty apply the tax tables published by the South African
Revenue Services (SARS) that apply at that time, to estimate
your after-tax income based on your pre-tax monthly
income. Lifestyle Protector provides flexibility to tailor your
cover to your individual needs. You have the flexibility to
choose whether you would like to use the estimated aftertax income or your actual after-tax income when taking out
this benefit.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Income Protector

Bonus Cover

The Income Protector pays a monthly claim payment, after
the expiry of the waiting period, if:
• You are temporarily or permanently occupationally
disabled;

Allows you to insure your annual cash bonus on primary
occupation only. The benefit is payable on total and
permanent occupational disability after a 24-month waiting
period.

•

You are permanently impaired - the percentage of
the benefit that is paid will be 50%, 75% or 100%
depending on the severity of the impairment;

•

You suffer one of the conditions covered by the
Guaranteed Payment Periods (7-day backdated and
1-month waiting period only); or

The Overhead Expenses Protector provides up to 23
monthly claim payments of up to 75% of business overheads
if you are temporarily or permanently unable, due to illness
or injury, to perform the duties of your own occupation.

•

You are partially occupationally disabled. A
proportionate monthly claim payment will be
determined with respect to the partial loss of income
and the disability.

Guaranteed Dependant Protector

You have flexibility in choosing the amount of cover you
want to be paid (up to 100% of your after-tax monthly
income) within the first 24 months after a claim event, and
thereafter. The Income Protector sum assured is therefore
made up of two cover amounts as follows:
• cover amount payable monthly within the first 24
months - This is the selected sum assured that will be
payable in the first 24 months from a claim event, after
the expiry of the waiting period.
•

cover amount payable monthly after 24 months
- This is the selected sum assured that will be payable
after the 24 months since the claim event, and up until
the selected cover period.

You can cover your income from dual occupations if
applicable. This means that up to two qualifying occupations
can be covered under a single Income Protector benefit.
You must however receive at least 20% of your income from
the secondary occupation and must have been performing
it for at least 1 year. The sum assured specified must equal
the combined cover amount for both your primary and
secondary occupation. You must then specify how the sum
assured will be split between your primary and secondary
occupation. This is defined as the dual occupations cover
amount split.
Please note: If one occupation qualifies for a claim, it is not
guaranteed that the other occupation will also qualify.
The following two benefits will be included free of charge:
• The Child Illness Protector benefit provides monthly
claim payments (capped at R30 000 per month) for up
to 12 months, if your child is diagnosed with a terminal
illness which results in the child’s life expectancy being
12 months or less.
•

The Claim Booster benefit pays up to an additional
33.33% of the Income Protector cover amount payable
monthly after 24 months. The Income Protector claim
amount that would be payable including your Claim
Booster benefit, is subject to a maximum of 100% of
your after-tax insurable monthly income.

Overhead Expenses Protector

The Guaranteed Dependant Protector pays up to 12 times
the Income Protector sum assured as a lump sum to your
beneficiaries if you die within 12 months from the first claim
payment of an Income Protector claim that is being paid at
the time of your death. The Income Protector sum assured
used will refer to the cover amount payable monthly within
the first 24 months, or if this cover is not in place, the cover
amount payable monthly after 24 months.
The multiple of the Income Protector sum assured that is
payable will be between 1 and 12 depending on the number
of monthly claim payments that have already been paid
for the Income Protector claim in payment, at the time of
death.
Retrenchment Protector
The Retrenchment Protector benefit pays the monthly
sum assured (capped at R30 000 per month), after the
expiry of a 1-month waiting period, for up to 6 months* if you
are retrenched from full-time employment.
Living Lifestyle Protector
The Living Lifestyle Protector pays up to 24 times the
Income Protector sum assured as a monthly payment or
lump sum if you suffer a critical illness covered by the benefit.
The Income Protector sum assured used will refer to the
cover amount payable monthly within the first 24 months,
or if this cover is not in place, the cover amount payable
monthly after 24 months. The multiple of the sum assured
that is paid out will be either 3 or 24 based on the severity of
the condition.
Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector
The Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector pays out 6
times the Income Protector sum assured as a monthly
payment or lump sum if your spouse or your child suffers a
critical illness covered by the benefit. The Income Protector
sum assured used will refer to the cover amount payable
monthly within the first 24 months, or if this cover is not in
place, the cover amount payable monthly after 24 months.

*A 12-month payment period is available on the Loan Protection package only. Please refer to the Loan Protection package Annexure for more details.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

INCOME PROTECTOR
Benefit Description

The Income Protector pays a monthly claim payment, after the expiry of the waiting period, if:
• You are temporarily or permanently occupationally disabled;
• You are permanently impaired - the percentage of the benefit that is paid will be 50%, 75% or 100%
depending on the severity of the impairment;
• You suffer one of the conditions covered by the Guaranteed Payment Periods (7-day backdated and
1-month waiting period only); or
• You are partially occupationally disabled. A proportionate monthly claim payment will be determined
with respect to the partial loss of income and the disability.
You have flexibility in choosing the amount of cover you want to be paid (up to 100% of your after-tax
monthly income) within the first 24 months after a claim event, and thereafter. The Income Protector
sum assured is therefore made up of two cover amounts as follows:
• cover amount payable monthly within the first 24 months - This is the selected sum assured that
will be payable in the first 24 months from a claim event, after the expiry of the waiting period.
• cover amount payable monthly after 24 months - This is the selected sum assured that will be
payable after the 24 months since the claim event, and up until the selected cover period.
For pre-retirement claims:
• Payments within the first 24 months will be limited to 100% of your after-tax insurable monthly income.
• Payments thereafter will be limited to 100% of your after-tax monthly income for total and permanent
occupational disability claims and 75% of your after-tax monthly income for partial and temporary
occupational disability or permanent impairment claims. The benefit will be paid up until the end of
the selected payment period (if valid claim persists), or the end of the month of the Life Assured’s 70th
birthday in the case of the Whole of Life cover period.
If you have selected the Whole of Life cover period you will also be eligible to be paid a monthly claim
amount if you suffer a permanent impairment after retirement. The percentage of the benefit that is paid
will be 16.67%, 33.33% or 50%, depending on the severity of the post-retirement permanent impairment.
Once in claim, your claim amount will increase by CPI (capped at 10.5%) on every claim anniversary, and the
benefit will be paid for the rest of your life.
There will be no waiting period for all post-retirement claims and the claim payment will be based on the
cover amount payable monthly after 24 months.
A Guaranteed Payment Period will apply for certain injuries, certain procedures, severe cancer, severe
strokes and hospitalisation events where either 7 days backdated or 1 month waiting period has been
selected.
You can cover your income from dual occupations if applicable. This means that up to two qualifying
occupations can be covered under a single Income Protector benefit. You must however receive at least
20% of your income from the secondary occupation and must have been performing it for at least 1 year.
The sum assured specified must equal the combined cover amount for both your primary and secondary
occupation. You must then specify how the sum assured will be split between your primary and secondary
occupation. This is defined as the dual occupations cover amount split.
Please note: If one occupation qualifies for a claim, it is not guaranteed that the other occupation will also
qualify.
The following two benefits will be included free of charge:
• The Child Illness Protector benefit provides monthly claim payments (capped at R30 000 per month)
for up to 12 months, if your child is diagnosed with a terminal illness which results in the child’s life
expectancy being 12 months or less.
• The Claim Booster benefit is a free benefit which is automatically included when the Income
Protector benefit is taken with a payment period of more than 2 years. The Claim Booster benefit
pays up to an additional 33.33% of the Income Protector cover amount payable monthly after 24
months since the claim of the event up to the selected payment period, or the end of the month of the
life assured’s 70th birthday where the Whole of Life benefit term is selected. This means that the life
assured is covered up to 100% for very severe claims.
The Income Protector claim amount that would be payable including your Claim Booster benefit, is
subject to a maximum of 100% of your after-tax insurable monthly income.

Continuation Option (at
Retirement Age)

• Where you have selected a cover amount payable monthly after 24 months and a cover period to the
end of the month of your 60th, 65th or 70th birthday, your Income Protector benefit will be replaced
with an Impairment Income benefit with a Whole of Life benefit term without medical underwriting.
The Impairment Income Sum Assured will be equal to the Income Protector cover amount payable
monthly after 24 months.
You will be notified prior to your Income Protector benefit end date, giving you the option to opt out.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

Premium Waiver

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

The premium for the Income Protector will be waived while a claim is being paid.

Benefit Type

This benefit is a non-accelerated benefit. The benefit is a core risk protection benefit.

Cover Period

• To the end of the month of the Life Assured’s 60th, 65th, 70th birthday
• Whole of Life (not available on Income Protector (first 24 months) option)
*The choice of cover period is subject to rating factors like education, income and occupation.

Guaranteed Payment
Periods

New Guaranteed Payment Periods have been introduced for the 7-day and 1 month waiting period.
Another 35 claim events have been added to the current 15 claim events. Fractures, hospital stays, certain
procedures, severe cancer and strokes are now included.

Payment period

• Up to 2 years
• Same as cover period

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

INCOME PROTECTOR (CONTINUED)
Benefit Limits

Minimum: R2 000 per month
Maximum: The lesser of:
• R200 000 per month (R300 000 per month for professionals) and
• 100% of after-tax monthly income for the first 2 years and thereafter

Cessation of Claims

For Whole of Life and a lump sum disability benefit:
• 1.5% of the lump sum disability benefit and 50% of Income Protector cover amount
payable monthly after 24 months cannot exceed 55% of after-tax monthly income.

Pre-retirement claims for an occupational disability, permanent impairment, Claim Booster and Child
Illness Protector that are admitted will end on the earliest of:
• The benefit end date as specified in the policy summary; or
• The date on which the maximum number of payments have been made in respect of:
− A period of continuous occupational disability or permanent impairment, or
− A claim admitted for a guaranteed payment period (7 day backdated and 1 month waiting period
only), or
• The Life Assured attaining the age of 70 and therefore being considered retired (Whole of Life cover
period); or
• The death of the Life Assured; or
• The death of the Child in the case of a Child Illness Protector claim; or
• The date, as decided by Liberty, on which:
− The Life Assured is again capable of performing a substantial portion of the duties of their nominated
occupation in the case of claims admitted under the disability and Claim Booster claim definitions;
− The Life Assured no longer suffers a reduction in income that is solely attributable to the injury, illness,
disease or surgical operation that gave rise to the claim in the case of claims admitted under the
occupational disability and Claim Booster claim definitions;
− The Life Assured refuses to submit to any physical examinations or tests required by Liberty to assess
the continuation of an admitted claim;
− The Policyholder fails to provide satisfactory evidence of the Life Assured’s continued incapacity
when requested to do so by Liberty from time to time;
− There has been a material change in the Life Assured’s health, income or occupation that affects the
continuation or validity of the claim; or
− The Life Assured declines to undergo any medical treatment recommended by their own medical
practitioner or Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer, which they could reasonably be expected to undergo,
and which could substantially improve or remove the disability or impairment.
Post-retirement admitted claims will end on the earlier of:
• Death of the Life Assured;
• The date, as decided by Liberty, on which:
− The Life Assured refuses to submit to any physical examinations or tests required by Liberty to assess
the continuation of an admitted claim;
− The Policyholder fails to provide satisfactory evidence of the Life Assured’s continued incapacity
when requested to do so by Liberty from time to time;
− There has been a material change in the Life Assured’s health that affects the continuation or validity
of the claim; or
− The Life Assured declines to undergo any medical treatment recommended by their own medical
practitioner or Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer, which they could reasonably be expected to undergo,
and which could substantially improve or remove the disability or impairment (Whole of Life cover
period).

Premium Payment Period

The premium payment period will correspond with the cover period.

Waiting Period

You must be disabled or impaired for the full duration of the waiting period before a claim can be admitted:

Age
Gender
Financial information such as income
Education
Occupation category (1 to 5)
Professional occupation status
In-claim escalation

Six waiting periods are available:
•
•
•
•
•
•

7-day backdated – only available to self-employed or fee-based professionals
1 month
3 months
6 months
12 months
24 months

If the 7-day backdated waiting period has been selected, once you meet the claim definition for 7 days and
a claim is admitted, a backdated payment will also be made in respect of the 7 days of the waiting period.
The exceptions to this are:
• Conditions which don’t qualify for backdating, listed in the specific exclusions
• Permanent impairment claims – claims will not be backdated further than the date that the permanence
of the impairment was established.
• The waiting period will be extended from the selected 7-day backdated waiting period to 1 month
if the Life Assured’s occupational disability or functional impairment is caused by the following: Any
condition, disorder, treatment or complication due to an outbreak, epidemic or pandemic of a disease,
virus or condition. This includes any derivative or mutation of such disease, virus or condition as defined
or notified by the World Health Organisation, or relevant South African authority (e.g. National Institute
for Communicable Diseases, Health Department, Labour Department), unless the Life Assured was
hospitalised for at least 72 hours in which case the 7-day backdated waiting period will apply.
The waiting period will not apply to Child Illness Protector claims.
The Claim Booster has a 24-month waiting period.
No waiting period will apply for post-retirement claims.
In-claim Escalation

In-claim Escalation
Once in claim, benefits will increase on the claim anniversary, based on the in-claim escalation which is
selected. The following options are available:
• 0% - this can only be selected where the ABI is 0%.
• 5% per annum - this can only be selected where the ABI is 2.5% p.a. or more, R/$ p.a. or CPI p.a.
• CPI (maximum capped at 10.5%, minimum capped at 0%) per annum - this can only be selected where
the ABI is 5% p.a. (or higher), R/$ p.a. or CPI p.a.
Super In-claim Escalation
Professionals who are 35 or younger at a claim anniversary will receive Super In-claim Escalation of 150%
of the selected in-claim escalation rate that would otherwise have applied at that claim anniversary.
In-claim escalation for post-retirement permanent Impairment claims (Whole of Life)
Post-retirement permanent impairment claims will always increase by CPI (capped at 10.5%) on the claim
anniversary, irrespective of what in-claim escalation option was selected to apply to pre-retirement claims.

Future Income
Assessment

The Future Income Assessment facility gives you the opportunity to review your Income Protector sum
assured to ensure that the benefit is appropriate relative to your income at the time, without having to
provide medical evidence of insurability or a statement of good health.
This facility is only available to lives:
•
•
•
•

That do not have any health loadings
That have not reached the policy anniversary preceding their 55th birthday
With policies that have an automatic benefit increase present
That have not submitted a claim on any Liberty policy within the last 12 months.

This option can only be exercised once between any two consecutive policy anniversaries. A minimum of
six months must elapse between two Future Income Assessment options being exercised.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

INCOME PROTECTOR (CONTINUED)
Future Income
Assessment (contd)

If you do not increase your sum assured after a given policy anniversary, the opportunity to increase your
sum assured for that year will not accumulate to future policy anniversaries.
The sum assured can be increased by a maximum of 20% less the automatic benefit increase that applied
at the previous policy anniversary.
The increase in sum assured is limited to your actual percentage increase in salary.
This option can be used as long as the sum assured does not breach the normal benefit maximums.

Specific Exclusions

No claim will be assessed or become payable where the disability, permanent impairment, loss of income
due to incapacity or other claim event arose directly or indirectly as a result of cosmetic surgery, other
than procedures necessitated by injury or illness which occurred during the existence of this benefit.
No backdating of claims will apply in respect of occupational disabilities or permanent impairments
caused by the below. This means that for any occupational disabilities or permanent impairments
resulting from any of the below, no claim payments are payable in respect of the duration of the initial 7
day waiting period.
• Pregnancy, abortion, childbirth or complications thereof; or
• Mental or psychiatric disorders (including adjustment disorders, cognitive impairments, communication
disorders, dementia disorders, dissociative disorders, mood disorders, personality disorders, schizoaffective disorders and somatoform disorders, chronic fatigue syndrome and any other stress-related
disorders), unless such disorder requires hospitalisation for 24 hours or more, or is diagnosed as a
chronic condition. Liberty defines mental or psychiatric disorders in accordance with the latest version
of the Diagnostic and Statistical Manual of Mental Disorders Criteria (DSM criteria) and a confirmed
diagnosis by an appropriate specialist is required; or
• Active participation in any form of sport as a professional player; or
• Influenza unless hospitalised for 24 hours or more or bronchitis unless diagnosed as chronic bronchitis
requiring hospitalisation by a qualified registered medical practitioner; or
• Laryngitis, sinusitis or pharyngitis unless hospitalised for 24 hours or more; or
• Headaches/migraines and cluster headaches unless diagnosed as chronic headaches/migraines
requiring hospitalisation for 24 hours or more; or
• Gastric influenza, gastritis, constipation or diarrhoea unless chronic and requiring hospitalisation for 24
hours or more; or
• Elective surgical procedures for infertility conditions.
In addition, the waiting period will be extended from the selected 7-day backdated waiting period to 1
month if the Life Assured’s occupational disability or functional impairment is caused by the following:
Any condition, disorder, treatment or complication due to an outbreak, epidemic or pandemic of a
disease, virus or condition. This includes any derivative or mutation of such disease, virus or condition as
defined or notified by the World Health Organisation, or relevant South African authority (e.g. National
Institute for Communicable Diseases, Health Department, Labour Department), unless the Life Assured
was hospitalised for at least 72 hours in which case the 7-day backdated waiting period will apply.
No claim will be assessed or become payable if you decline to undergo any medical treatment
recommended by your own medical practitioner or Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer which you could
reasonably be expected to undergo and which could substantially improve or remove the condition being
claimed for.
No claims will be payable for sickness or post-sickness consequences where there are no objective
medical investigation results to prove the ongoing cause of the inability to work or the functional
impairment except for periods of hospitalisation.
No Child Illness Protector claims will be paid if:
• The condition claimed for existed before the inception or the reinstatement of the benefit;
• The condition claimed for is as a result of a wilful or negligent act on the part of the parent or caregiver; or
• Liberty is denied full access to the child’s medical records.
Regardless of the waiting period chosen, a waiting period of 6 weeks will apply in the case of claims
arising as a result of a caesarean section. Payment on this benefit will not be backdated even if the 7-day
backdated waiting period option is selected.
These exclusions apply in addition to the general exclusions of the policy.

Eligibility

The benefit is available to all lives. Depending on the risk classification of the client, Liberty will determine
if clients are eligible for Income Protector. This will be determined at quote stage and depends on rating
factors such as the education, income and occupation(s) of the client.

even if the max number of payments have been made.

Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector

Only one Child Illness Protector claim will be admitted per child across all Liberty policies. A maximum of

(1)

What is the Income Protector benefit?
The Income Protector benefit pays a monthly benefit
equal to:
• The sum assured if the life assured is temporarily
or permanently unable to perform their nominated
primary/secondary occupation due to a disability; or
• A percentage of the sum assured if the life assured is
partially occupationally disabled
• A percentage of the sum assured if the life assured is
permanently impaired. The percentage payable will
depend on the severity of the permanent impairment.
• The sum assured if the life assured suffers one of the
conditions covered under the Guaranteed Payment
Periods (7 day backdated and 1 month waiting periods
only).
Claims will be admitted after the expiry of the waiting period
and will be paid up until the earlier of the claimant’s recovery,
the end of the selected payment period, or the death of the
claimant. Disability claims will only be paid until the end of
the month of the Life Assured’s 70th birthday where the
Whole of Life benefit term is selected, thereafter the claim
will be reassessed against the impairment definitions.
If a claim qualifies for a claim under more than one of the
claims definitions of the benefit, the claim definition which
results in the highest claim will be used.
The life assured can choose:
• The cover period (how long they want to be insured for).
• The payment period (how long the claim should be
payable for after the expiry of the waiting period).
• Waiting period (7-day, 1 month, 3 months, 6 months,
12 months, 24 months)
• Cover amount (monthly sum assured). The life assured
has the flexibility to choose different cover amounts
before and after 24 months.

Whole of Life
•

If the Whole of Life cover period is selected, you will also
have cover for permanent impairments suffered after
retirement. After retirement, the Income Protector will
pay a monthly benefit equal to a percentage of the sum
assured when you are permanently impaired according
to the post-retirement impairment criteria. The
percentage payable will depend on the severity of the
post-retirement permanent impairment. The payments
will commence immediately once the permanence
of the impairment is established. This benefit will be
paid for the rest of the claimant’s life and will increase
with CPI (capped at 10.5%) irrespective of the in-claim
escalation chosen for pre-retirement claims.

A Guaranteed Payment Period will apply for fractures,
hospital stays, certain procedures, severe cancer and strokes
where either the 7-day backdated or 1 month waiting period
has been selected.
The Child Illness Protector is a free benefit, automatically
included and pays the monthly Income Protector sum
assured (up to a maximum of R30 000 per month) for up
to 12 months if the life assured’s child is diagnosed with a
terminal illness which is likely to lead to the child’s death
within 12 months of diagnosis. The Income Protector sum
assured used will refer to the cover amount payable monthly
within the first 24 months, or if this cover is not in place, the
cover amount payable monthly after 24 months.
The Claim Booster benefit is a free benefit which is
automatically included when the Income Protector benefit
is taken with a payment period of more than 2 years. If
the life assured is both totally occupationally disabled and
permanently impaired at the highest severity level, the Claim
Booster benefit will increase the monthly claim payment by
up to 33.33%. The increase is limited such that the overall
claim payment is less than or equal to 100% of the aftertax insurable monthly income. This benefit applies to claim
amounts payable after 24 months since the claim event, up
to the selected payment period, or until end of the month of
the Life Assured’s 70th birthday where the Whole of Life cover
period is selected. This means that a life assured is covered
up to 100% for very severe claims.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

The Income Protector is a non-accelerated, core risk benefit.
Can I cover more than one occupation?
You can insure your income from dual occupations if
applicable. The sum assured specified must equal the
combined cover for both your primary and secondary
occupation. You must then specify how the sum assured will
be split between your primary and secondary occupation.
This is defined as the dual occupations cover amount split.

Sam is financial manager (primary occupation) and at night
he is a lecturer at a university (secondary occupation).
He receives R30 000 from the finance company and

Sam purchased the Income Protector with:
• Cover period to end of the month of his 70th

• Payment period of up to 2 years
• Waiting period of 1 month
• Cover amount payable monthly within the first
24 months of R50 000 (Combined for two

Three years after he purchased his Income Protector
