LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

How much do we cover on Income Protector?
You can cover up to the 100% of your after-tax insurable
monthly income. The after-tax insurable monthly income is
the pre-tax monthly income less the tax payable as a result
of this income. The pre-tax monthly income depends on the
type of employment.
If the after-tax income is not easily available, Liberty will
apply the tax tables published by the South African Revenue
Services (SARS) that apply at that time to estimate your
after-tax insurable monthly income based on your pre-tax
monthly income.
Disability claims criteria
The Income Protector will pay the monthly sum assured if
you are temporarily or permanently unable to perform the
duties of their nominated primary/secondary occupation
due to an injury, illness, disease or surgical operation and
suffers a loss of income as a result.

The full sum assured of R50 000 will be paid to Sam for
up to two years after the 1 month waiting period. He will
receive 23 payments.

The benefit will be paid from the expiry of the waiting period
and will be paid until the earliest of recovery, death, or the
end of the cover period or the end of the month of the Life
Assured’s 70th birthday where the Whole of Life cover
period is selected.

EXAMPLE 2

When you are only partially disabled, a proportion of the sum
assured may be paid at Liberty’s discretion.

Mandy is an office manager (primary occupation) and
in the evenings she trains young artists at an art school
(secondary occupation). She receives R80 000 from her
primary occupation and R20 000 from the art school.

These claims criteria fall away at retirement as you cannot
be assessed for occupational disability post-retirement.
Post-retirement, you will be assessed under the impairment
claims criteria.

Mandy purchased the Income Protector with a:
•

Cover period to end of the month of her 70th
birthday

•

Payment period of up to 2 years

•

Waiting period of 1 month

•

Cover amount payable monthly within the first
24 months of R80 000 (Combined for two
occupations)

•

Dual occupation cover amount split elected to be
50% for primary occupation and 50% secondary
occupation

The sum assured split would imply Mandy wants to
R40 000 cover for her primary occupation and
R40 000 cover for her secondary occupation. As she
only earns R20 000 from her secondary occupation,
she would not be allowed to proceed with this selection.
Her dual occupation cover amount split would need to
change so that the percentage allocated to her secondary
occupation is not more than 25% = 20 000 / 80 000
(i.e. her income earns in the secondary occupation divided
by the combined sum assured).

EXAMPLE 1

Henry (age 34) is a surgeon and purchases the Income
Protector benefit with:
• A cover period to end of the month of his 70th

• A payment period of up to 2 years

24 months of R80 000
He is involved in a car accident and injures his hand.
As Henry is a surgeon, he is unable to perform the duties

disabled.
The monthly amount payable, after the expiry of the
1 month waiting period, is 100% of R80 000 per month.
Because Henry selected the 1 month waiting period and
a payment period of 2 years, he will receive R80 000 for
23 months for his disability.
If he dies or returns to work before the end of the
23 months after the first payment date, his payments
will cease before the 23 monthly payments are made.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Impairment Post-retirement (Whole of Life cover period
only)
This benefit will pay a percentage of the monthly sum
assured if you suffer a permanent impairment after
retirement. The percentage payable will be 16.67%, 33.33%
or 50% of the monthly sum assured depending on the
severity of the impairment suffered.
Where a pre-retirement disability or permanent impairment
claim has been admitted and is paid to the end of the month
of the Life Assured’s 70th birthday, you will not automatically
qualify for a post-retirement permanent impairment
claim. Your condition will be re-assessed according to the
Impairment Benefit Categories that apply to post-retirement
impairments to assess whether you qualify for a postretirement permanent impairment claim.
Note:
Should a post-retirement claim be accepted, the claim will
be based on the cover amount payable monthly after
24 months of the benefit at post-retirement and severity
of impairment, and not the claim amount that was being
paid for the pre-retirement claim.

At the end of the month of his 70th birthday and
provided that his claim is still being paid at the time, Sello’s
condition will be re-assessed to determine whether he
is permanently impaired in terms of the post retirement
permanent impairment criteria.

Once the claim is being paid and the severity of the
impairment increases in such a way that the life assured
qualifies for a higher percentage of the sum assured, the
claim amount will be increased. For example, if a claim
is being paid at the 16.67% level and the severity of the
impairment increases and the life assured now qualifies at
the 50% level, the claim amount which is being paid at the
time will be increased to the new severity level of 50%.

If Sello’s condition does not meet the post-retirement
permanent impairment criteria, no further claim payments
will be paid.
If Sello’s condition does meet the post-retirement
permanent impairment criteria, he will be paid a percentage
according to the severity of his impairment, which increases
by CPI (capped at 10.5%) on every claim anniversary, and
the benefit will be paid for the rest of his life.

The Activities of Daily Living (ADL) Catch-All Benefit
Category is one of the Impairment Benefit Categories. In this
Benefit Category claims are assessed by considering your
ability to perform activities of daily life rather than medical
criteria. ADLs are made up of “Basic ADLs” such as washing
and dressing and “Instrumental ADLs” such as telephone use
and housekeeping.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

EXAMPLE

Julie (age 40) is an Administration Manager and has purchased the Income Protector (Whole of Life) with:
•
•
•
•
•
•

A cover period of Whole of Life
A payment period of Whole of Life
A waiting period of 24 months
In-claim escalation of CPI
A cover amount payable monthly after 24 months of R40 000 per month (which corresponds to 75% of her after tax
insurable monthly income).
ABI = 7%

At age 58, Julie starts to suffer from anaemia. She has not retired as yet. A disability claim is not admitted as Julie is still able
to perform the duties of an Administration Manager.
The claim will be assessed according to the pre-retirement permanent impairment criteria.
After 3 months, it is established that Julie’s anaemia is permanent and meets the claims definition at the 50% severity
level. This qualifies her to be paid 50% of the Income Protector sum assured.
As a 24-month waiting period applies to this benefit and it took 3 months to establish permanence of the anaemia,
payments will only commence after the expiry of the 24-month waiting period.
The Income Protector sum assured payable to Julie would be R154 787 per month which is the original sum assured of
R40 000 per month increased by 7% per annum (over a period of 20 years which includes the 24-months waiting period).
The amount payable to Julie every month is 50% of R154 787 = R77 394 per month.
At each claim anniversary, the claim amount will be increased by CPI (capped at 10.5%). If CPI was 10%, Julie’s claim will
increase to R85 133 at the first claim anniversary, R93 646 at the second claim anniversary and so on, until the end of the
month of Julie’s 70th birthday or until her death, whichever is sooner.
On the end of the month of Julie’s 70th birthday, she is re-assessed on the post-retirement impairment definitions. Julie still
suffers from the same anaemia disorder and the severity is such that she qualifies for the 16.67% severity level on the postretirement impairment category claims criteria for the anaemia benefit sub-category.
The Income Protector monthly sum assured is now R304 490 (original sum assured of R40 000 per month increased by
the ABI of 7% per annum over 30 years).
The amount payable to Julie every month is 16.67% of R304 490 = R50 759 per month.
Note that this claim amount is not related to the pre-retirement claim amount which was being received up to the end
of the month of she turns 70. After each post-retirement claim anniversary, Julie’s claim amount will be increased by the
minimum of CPI and 10.5%. If CPI was 10% at each claim anniversary, the claim amount would be increased to R55 834 at
the first post-retirement claim anniversary, R61 412 at the second post-retirement claim anniversary and by the in-claim
escalation rate that applied at each claim anniversary thereafter.
The post-retirement claims payments will be paid to Julie for the rest of her life.

Claim Booster Pre-retirement
The Claim Booster benefit is a free benefit which is
automatically included when the Income Protector benefit
is taken with a payment period of more than 2 years. If you
are both totally occupationally disabled and permanently
impaired at the highest severity level, the Claim Booster
benefit will increase your monthly claim payment by up to
33.33%. The increase is limited such that the overall claims
payment is less than or equal to 100% of the after-tax
insurable monthly income. This benefit applies to the cover
amount payable monthly after 24 months since the claim
event, up to the selected payment period, or until the end of
the month the Life assured turns 70 where the Whole of Life
cover period is selected. This means that you are covered for
up to 100% for very severe claims.

If you select a sum assured equal to 100% of after tax
insurable monthly income and you qualify for a claim for
the full amount, the Claim Booster will no longer apply.
Where the cover amount payable monthly after 24 months
is between 75% and 100% of after-tax insurable monthly
income, the claim will be boosted to a maximum of 100% of
after tax insurable monthly income.
The increased claim amount provided by the Claim Booster
will be payable up to the selected retirement age, or the
end of the month the Life assured turns age 70 where the
Whole of Life has been selected.
The Claim Booster benefit does not apply to postretirement permanent impairment claims.

EXAMPLE 1

Marcus purchased the Income Protector with:
•
•
•
•

Whole of Life cover period.
Whole of Life payment period.
Waiting period of 24 months .
Cover amount payable monthly after 24 months of R75 000 (which corresponds to 75% of Marcus’ after-tax insurable
monthly income). Marcus did not select an ABI or an in-claim escalation.

At age 36, Marcus is involved in a serious car accident and is completely paralysed. As Marcus is not retired the claim will be
assessed on the pre-retirement claims definition.
Marcus is considered disabled as he cannot perform the duties of his nominated primary occupation as a physiotherapist.
A disability claim will be admitted. Because Marcus’s condition also qualifies for a 100% severity level pre-retirement
permanent impairment, a Claim Booster will also be admitted and the total claim amount payable will be increased by
33.33%.
The total claim amount payable to Marcus, after the 24-month waiting period, is R100 000 = R75 000 X 133.35%
(100% of sum assured and an extra 33.33% for the Claim Booster). He therefore receives his full after-tax monthly
income.
The claim will be paid until the end of the month of Marcus’ 70th birthday.
At the end of the month of Marcus’ 70th birthday, he will be re-assessed on the post-retirement impairment definitions.
Marcus is still fully paralysed, and a post-retirement impairment claim will be admitted. The post-retirement percentage
payable for paralysis is 50% of the sum assured.
The amount payable every month is 50% of R75 000 per month which equals R37 500 per month.
Note that this claim amount payable is in no way related to the pre-retirement amount which was being received up until
the end of the month of he turned 70.
The Claim Booster benefit does not apply to the post-retirement impairment claim as Marcus cannot be assessed
occupationally after retirement.
After each post-retirement claim anniversary, the claim amount will be increased by CPI (capped at 10.5%). If CPI is 10% at
each claim anniversary, the claim amount will be increased to R41 250 at the first claim anniversary, R45 375 at the second
claim anniversary and so on.
The post-retirement claim payments will continue for the rest of Marcus’ life.

EXAMPLE 2

Arthur (age 50) has purchased the Income Protector
benefit with a:
•
•
•
•

•
•

Cover period of Whole of Life
Payment period of Whole of Life (equal to the cover
period)
Waiting period of 24 months
Cover amount payable monthly after 24 months
of R40 000 per month (which is equal to 75% of
Arthur’s after-tax insurable monthly income.)
Benefit increase of 0%
In-claim escalation of 0%

At age 74, Arthur suffers a heart attack and is diagnosed
with coronary artery disease. Arthur is considered retired
and the claim will be assessed according to the postretirement benefit claims definitions.
After 3 months, it is established that the impairment
is permanent, and the severity of the coronary artery
disease is such that Arthur qualifies for 16.67% severity
level payout under the impairment benefit category.
The amount payable is 16.67% of the Income Protector
sum assured. No waiting period applies for postretirement impairment claims and claim payments will
commence once the permanence of the impairment has
been established.
The amount payable every month is 16.67% of R40 000
which equals R6 668 per month.
As this is a post-retirement claim, a CPI in-claim escalation
(capped at 10.5%) will apply despite the 0% in-claim
escalation which Arthur chose at the inception of this
policy.
After each claim anniversary, the amount will be
increased by CPI (capped at 10.5%). If CPI was 10% at
each claim anniversary, the claim amount will increase
to R7 335 at the first claim anniversary, R8 068 at the
second claim anniversary and by the in-claim escalation
that applies at each claim anniversary thereafter.
Soon after the second claim anniversary, Arthur is
diagnosed with severe aphasia and meets the criteria for
the 50% severity level payout. Arthur’s monthly claim
payment of R8 068 will be increased by the ratio of 50%
and 16.67% (i.e. multiplied by 3). Arthur’s claim payment
will be increased to R24 205 per month.

What are the Guaranteed Payment Period claim events?
A guaranteed minimum payment period will be allowed will
be allowed for fractures, hospital stays, certain procedures,
severe cancer and strokes.
The minimum payment period is only available on the 7-day
backdated and 1 month waiting periods.
There is no requirement for the life assured to be disabled
(i.e. unable to perform their nominated primary/secondary
occupation) or permanently impaired to qualify for this
benefit.

If a claim qualifies under more than one of the conditions

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

EXAMPLE

Alice (age 29) purchased the Income Protector benefit
with a waiting period of 1 month. She fell at the gym and
fractured her knee cap. Alice will be paid the full Income
Protector sum assured (after the expiry of the 1 month
waiting period) for one month, irrespective of whether
she is able to return to work during this period.
If Alice had chosen the 7-day backdated waiting period,
she would have been guaranteed to receive the sum
assured for two months.
If Alice is still unable to work or is permanently impaired
after the expiry of her guaranteed payment period, she
will still be eligible for a claim under the occupational
disability or permanent impairment definitions.
If Alice fractured her knee cap and her thigh, only one
payout would take place, i.e. the longest guaranteed
payment period. In this case Alice would be paid out for
the thigh fracture as it is a 3-month payment period on a
7-day backdated waiting period.
What is the Child Illness Protector?
The Child Illness Protector is automatically included, free of
charge, in the Income Protector benefit.
The Child Illness Protector will pay the Income Protector
monthly sum assured (capped at R30 000) in the event that
your child is diagnosed with a terminal illness where the child’s
life expectancy is 12 months or less as confirmed by a medical
professional. The Income Protector sum assured will be
based on the cover amount payable monthly within the first 24
months, or if this cover is not in place, the cover amount payable
monthly after 24 months.
Your income will be protected, in the event, that you need to
stay at home to look after your child because of the illness. You
will receive this benefit irrespective of whether or not you have
to physically stay at home to care for the child. For example, you
may prefer to hire a care giver to look after the child while you
continue to work.
The benefit will pay for a maximum of 12 months. If the child
dies before the end of the 12 months after the first payment
date, the payments will cease before 12 monthly payments are
made.
100% of the sum assured will be paid subject to a maximum of
R30 000 per month.
What are some of the Child Illness Protector rules?
•

Payment will be based on the diagnosis of a severe
illness only and the sum assured will be paid
irrespective of whether the policyholder physically
needs to stay home to care for the child or not, i.e.
there will be no requirement to prove lost income in
respect of this benefit.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

•

•
•
•

•

•
•

•
•
•
•

The severity of the illness covered is that the child is
considered terminally ill, i.e. expected survival is less
than 12 months as confirmed by a medical assessment
from a specialist medical professional.
The waiting period for the Income Protector benefit
is not applicable to this benefit.
The benefit amount paid out will be 100% of sum
assured subject to a maximum of R30 000 per month.
Only one claim can be submitted per child. For
example, if both parents of a child have the benefit
(on the same or different policies), only one parent
can claim for a child who is diagnosed with a terminal
illness.
If the same parent has two different policies with the
same benefit, the Child Illness Protector can only be
claimed under one of the policies.
A maximum of two claims may be submitted per life
assured across all Liberty policies.
Only one claim can be submitted per policy. If either
life assured on a policy has claimed on the Child Illness
Protector previously, the benefit cannot be claimed
on the policy again, even if it is a different life assured
who claims.
Biological and legally adopted children will qualify.
Children between the ages of 0 and 18 and who are
unmarried at the date of diagnosis will be covered.
Conditions which existed before the inception of the
benefit will not be covered.
No Child Illness Protector will be paid if an Income
Protector benefit is being paid at the time.

Will my benefit keep up with my needs?
Future Income Assessment
The Future Income Assessment facility gives policyholders
the opportunity to review their Income Protector sum
assured to ensure that the benefit is appropriate relative to
their income at the time, without having to provide medical
evidence of insurability or a statement of good health.
This facility is only available to lives:
• That do not have any health loadings.
• Age next 55 or younger.
Super In-claim Escalation for Young Professionals

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

•
•

On policies that have an ABI present.
That have not submitted a claim on any Liberty policy
within the last 12 months.

This option can only be exercised once between any two
policy anniversaries.
A minimum of six months must elapse between two Future
Income Assessment options being exercised.
If a policyholder does not increase their sum assured after
a given policy anniversary, the opportunity to increase their
sum assured for that year will not accumulate to future policy
anniversaries.
The sum assured can be increased by a maximum of 20%
less the ABI that applied at the previous policy anniversary.
For example, if an ABI of 7% applied at the previous policy
anniversary, the maximum sum assured increase that will
apply at this policy anniversary is 13% (i.e. 20% minus 7%).
The increase in sum assured is limited to the actual
percentage increase in salary.
This option can be used as long as the sum assured does not
breach benefit maximums.
In-claim Escalation
Pre-retirement:
There are three options available:
•
•

0% - this can only be selected where the ABI is 0%.
5% per annum - this can only be selected where the ABI
is 2.5% p.a. or more, R/$ p.a. or CPI p.a.

•

CPI (maximum capped at 10.5%, minimum capped at
0%) per annum - this can only be selected where the
ABI is 5% p.a. (or higher), R/$ p.a. or CPI p.a.

Once in claim, the claim amount will increase at this claim
escalation rate on every claim anniversary.
Claimants who are professionals and are 35 or younger at a
claim anniversary will qualify for the Super In-claim escalation.
They will receive 150% of the normal escalation rate.

For example, if a 5% escalation rate is chosen, the young
professional will receive 7.5% (i.e. 150% of 5%) on every
claim anniversary until they reach 35 age next. Thereafter,
the in-claim escalation will revert back to the normal in-claim
escalation level that they had selected.
The sum assured is not affected by the in-claim escalation.
The sum assured will continue to increase at the ABI
selected. For any claim, the claim amount will be based on
the sum assured at the time of the claim and will increase
with in-claim escalation for the duration of the claim only.
At the expiry of the claim, the sum assured reverts back to
the sum assured with ABIs increases assuming no claim has
been made.
Post-retirement:
When a post-retirement claim is admitted and is being paid,
irrespective of what in-claim escalation percentage has been
selected for pre-retirement claims, the post-retirement claim
amount will increase by the change in CPI (capped at 10.5%)
on the claim anniversary.
Can I select the Whole of Life cover period on both a
lump sum disability benefit and the Income Protector
benefit?
Yes, you may select the Whole of Life cover period on both
the Income Protector benefit and a lump sum disability
benefit, provided that:
•
•

1.5% of the lump sum disability benefit sum assured; plus
50% of the Income Protector cover amount payable
monthly after 24 months do not exceed 55% of your
after-tax insurable monthly income.

When do I submit proof of income?
Even though proof of income will still be encouraged at
underwriting stage, it will not be a requirement for the
policy to be issued. You must, regardless of the level of
income, provide proof of income before the next policy
anniversary after your policy has been issued. This also
applies to servicing changes that warrant a proof of income
requirement.

What penalties exist if I do not submit proof of income
timeously?
If proof of income is received after the post-issue period
(the period before the next policy anniversary following
inception or reinstatement of a new Income Protector
benefit or amendments to an existing Income Protector
that would warrant a request for proof of income) and
you are deemed to be over-insured, any premiums paid
for the over-insured portion of your sum assured will only
be refunded back to your last policy anniversary prior to
receipt of proof of income. Any expenses incurred will also
be deducted from the premiums to be refunded.
When do I need to submit proof of loss of income?
At claim stage, proof of loss of income will only be a
requirement after 12 months or 24 months in the case of
professionals, provided medical evidence is sufficient.
Where a claim is admitted under the permanent impairment,
Guaranteed Payment Period or Child Illness Protector
claim definitions you will not be required to prove a loss of
income.
After I suffer a claim event, how long do I need to wait
before I start receiving benefits?
For claims admitted under the disability claims definition
or the Guaranteed Payment Periods claims definition your
payments will commence after the expiry of the waiting
period which you select for your benefit.
For claims admitted under the permanent impairment claims
definition, your payments will commence on the later of
the expiry of the waiting period and the permanence of the
impairment being established.
Where the Whole of Life cover period is selected, postretirement permanent Impairment claims will commence as
soon as the permanence of the impairment is established.
Payment commences immediately because the Income
Protector benefit (and other temporary Loss of Income
Protection benefits you may have) will have ceased by
retirement.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

No waiting period applies to Child Illness Protector claims.
Will I be covered for partial disability?
A proportionate claim payment will be payable when you
are partially disabled. Partially disabled means that you are
totally unable to perform some, but not all, of the duties of
your nominated occupation due to the disablement and, as
a result, suffers a partial loss of income. The proportionate
claim payment amount will equal the Income Protector Sum
Assured multiplied by the Proportion of Income Lost. The
Proportion of Income Lost as a result of the disability must be
at least 20% for a partial claim to be considered.
Where the Proportion of Income Lost cannot be determined,
the proportionate claim payment amount will equal the
Income Protector Sum Assured multiplied by the Proportion
of Disablement. The Proportion of Disablement as a result
of the disability must be at least 25% of the specified
occupational duties for a partial claim to be considered.
The proportion of income lost is the difference between
your pre-disability post-tax monthly income and any post-tax
monthly income that you earn during the period of disability
divided by your pre-disability pre-tax monthly income.
The proportion of disablement is the proportion of specified
duties of the nominated occupation that can no longer be
performed due to disability. This Proportion of Disablement
must be supported by sufficient medical evidence, in
Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer’s opinion, to prove that you are
unable to perform the duties of your nominated occupations.
How many payments will I receive once I start claiming?
Your payments will continue until you no longer meet the
claim definition, you reach the maximum number of payments
allowed, you reach the end of your payment period, or you die.
The maximum number of monthly claim payments is the lesser
of:
• Payment Period less the duration of the waiting period;
and
• Payment Period less the duration required to establish
permanence of the impairment
Where the 7-day backdated waiting period is selected, the
duration of the waiting period will be assumed to be zero.
Where you have selected the Whole of Life cover period,
your pre-retirement claim payments can continue until your
selected cover period, or the end of the month you turn
70 in the case of the Whole of Life cover period. At the end
of the month you turn age 70, or for any post-retirement
claims, the claim will be assessed on permanent impairment
definitions and will be payable for the rest of your life.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

•
•

At the end of the month of your 70th birthday; or
If you are over the age of 55 and you have stopped
active or full-time engagement in your occupation (but
not due to the disability or impairment that you are
claiming for).

Can I make multiple claims?
Multiple Income Protector claims are allowed provided that:
• The maximum number of payments has not already
been made in respect of a disability, permanent
impairment or Guaranteed Payment Period claim; or
•

The claim is totally unrelated to a previous disability,
permanent impairment or Guaranteed Payment
Period claim for which the maximum number of claim
payments has already been made.

For Child Illness Protector claims, only one Child Illness
Protector claim will be admitted per child across all Liberty
policies. A maximum of two Child Illness Protector claims
will be admitted per Life Assured across all Liberty policies.
No more than one claim can be paid at any particular point
in time across all claim definitions (including Child Illness
Protector).
If my impairment worsens will I receive a higher claim
amount?
Yes, if your impairment severity increases and you qualify for a
higher claim severity level according to the Impairment Benefit
Categories, your claim amount will be increased.
Will my premiums be waived while I am claiming?
The Income Protector monthly premium will be paid by
Liberty in addition to the monthly claims payment while an
Income Protector claim is being paid. Therefore, the premium is
effectively waived while a claim is being paid.
What are the tax implications of this benefit?
This benefit is not taxable in the hands of the recipient.
Are there special features which apply to professionals?
Yes, there are special features which apply to professionals, as
classified by Liberty:
•

Professionals qualify for preferential premium rates.

•

Young professionals qualify for Super In-claim
Escalation.

•

The maximum number of claim payments for a Child Illness
Protector claim is for 12 months.

Self-employed professionals have a specialised income
definition.

•

Whole of Life cover period: When will I be considered
retired

Professionals have higher benefit maximum limits for
both Income Protector and Bonus Cover.

•

Proof of loss of income will only be called for after 24
months for professionals at claims stage.

When assessing claims for this benefit you will be
considered retired:

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

IMPAIRMENT INCOME
Benefit Description

The Impairment Income pays a monthly claim payment, if:
You are permanently impaired - the percentage of the benefit that is paid will be 50%, 75% or 100%
depending on the severity of the impairment.
Once 100% of the Sum Assured is in-claim, no further claims will be admitted.

Premium Waiver

The premium for the Impairment Income will be waived while a claim is being paid.

Benefit Type

This benefit is a non-accelerated benefit. The benefit is a core risk protection benefit.

Cover Period

• To the end of the month of the Life Assured’s: 60th, 65th or 70th birthday OR
• Whole of Life

Payment Period

Same as cover period

Benefit Limits

Minimum: R2 000 per month
Maximum: The lesser of:
• R150 000 per month
• 100% of the after-tax monthly income

Cessation of Claims

Permanent impairment claims that are admitted will end on the earliest of:
• The benefit end date as specified in the policy summary; or
• The death of the Life Assured;

Rating Factors

Premium Payment Period

The premium payment period will correspond with the cover period.

Waiting Period

No waiting period applies. Your claim will be paid as soon as Liberty has established the permanence
of the impairment you are claiming for.

In-claim Escalation

Once in claim, your claim amount and Sum Assured will increase by CPI (capped at 10.5%) on every
claim anniversary, and the benefit will be paid for the rest of your life.

Specific Exclusions

No claim will be assessed or become payable if you decline to undergo any medical treatment
recommended by your own medical practitioner or Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer, at your own
cost, which you could reasonably be expected to undergo, and which could substantially improve or
remove the impairment being claimed for.

These exclusions apply in addition to the general exclusions set out in the general terms and
conditions.
Eligibility

The benefit is available to all lives. Depending on the risk classification of the client, Liberty will
determine if clients are eligible for Impairment Income. This will be determined at quote stage and
depends on rating factors such as the education, income and occupation(s) of the client.

Multiple Claims

If a claim arising from a single event is submitted in respect of more than one impairment benefit
category, each paying the same proportion of the Sum Assured, only one of the categories, as
selected by Liberty, will pay a claim.
If a claim, or a number of claims arising from a single event, is submitted in respect of more than one
impairment benefit category, the claim will be paid in respect of the impairment benefit category
paying the highest proportion of Sum Assured.

What is the Impairment Income benefit?

Claims Criteria

The Income Protector benefit pays a monthly benefit a
percentage of the sum assured if the life assured suffers
from a permanent impairment that is covered under this
benefit. The percentage payable will depend on the severity
of the permanent impairment. Impairment refers to the loss
of function in any part of the body, whether it is physical,
functional or mental.

The benefit will pay a percentage of the monthly sum
assured if you are permanently impaired according to the
Impairment Benefit Categories.

If a claim qualifies for a claim under more than one of the
claims definitions of the benefit, the claim definition which
results in the highest claim will be used.

If a claim qualifies for a payment in more than one benefit
category, the claim paid will be for the benefit category that
results in the highest claim payment.

How much do we cover on Impairment Income?

If the severity of the impairment increases, once a claim is
being paid, and you qualify for a higher percentage of the
sum assured, the claim amount will be increased.

You can cover up to the 100% of your after-tax monthly
income. The after-tax monthly income is the pre-tax monthly
income less the tax payable as a result of this income.
If the after-tax income is not easily available, Liberty will
apply the tax tables published by the South African Revenue
Services (SARS) that apply at that time to estimate your
after-tax insurable monthly income based on your pre-tax
monthly income.

The percentages payable will be 50%, 75% or 100% of the
monthly sum assured depending on the severity of the
impairment suffered.

The claim will be paid once permanence of the impairment
being established. The maximum number of payments
that will be made will be the lesser of the selected payment
period less the duration of time required to establish the
permanence of the impairment.
The benefit will be paid up until the end of the selected
payment period or death.

What type of cover does the Impairment Income benefit
provide?

If my impairment worsens will I receive a higher claim
amount?

Impairment Income is intended to protect you against
potential loss of income. Payment is dependent on the
level of impairment suffered rather than whether you are
occupationally disabled. Impairment Income provides a
comprehensive benefit. The benefit payment is proportional
to the severity of your condition.

Yes, if your impairment severity increases and you qualify
for a higher claim severity level according to the Impairment
Benefit Categories, your claim amount will be increased.

How do benefit increases affect the tiered claim
payments?
The amount of any claim is based on the sum assured at the
date of claim, the percentage of the sum assured payable as
a result of the impairment, and any impairment claims that
have already been paid.
After I suffer from an impairment and claim, is there a
waiting period before I can receive my benefit?
No. Your claim will be paid as soon as Liberty has established
the permanence of the condition you are claiming for.
What is the Activities of Daily Living (ADL) Catch- All
Benefit Category?
The Activities of Daily Living (ADL) Catch-All Benefit Category
is one of the Impairment Benefit Categories. In this Benefit
Category claims are assessed by considering your ability to
perform activities of daily life rather than medical criteria.
ADLs are made up of “Basic ADLs” such as washing and
dressing and “Instrumental ADLs” such as telephone use and
housekeeping.

Will my premiums be waived while I am claiming?
The Impairment Income monthly premium will be paid by
Liberty in addition to the monthly claims payment while
an Impairment Income claim is being paid. Therefore, the
premium is effectively waived while a claim is being paid.
What are the tax implications of this benefit?
This benefit is not taxable in the hands of the recipient.

BONUS COVER
Benefit Description

The Bonus Cover benefit allows you to insure your annual cash performance bonuses. This benefit
needs to be selected on the Income Protector benefit with a payment period of more than 2 years.
A premium is charged for the benefit.
The benefit will be payable for up to 7 years for total and permanent occupational disability claims
only. Claim payments will be made annually on the claim anniversary (provided the life assured is still
in claim at the time of each claim anniversary) after the expiry of the 24-month waiting period. The
first payment will be made immediately following the 24-month waiting period.
Where dual occupations are covered under your Income Protector benefit, the Bonus Cover
benefit will only apply to the primary nominated occupation.
The selections made for the Income Protector benefit (i.e.in-claim escalation, cover period and
payment period) will also apply to the Bonus Cover benefit. Where the cover period of the Income
Protector benefit is Whole of life, the Bonus Cover benefit cover period will be limited to age 70.

Premium Waiver

The premium for the Bonus Cover benefit will be waived while a claim is being paid.

Benefit Type

This benefit is a non-accelerated benefit. The benefit is not a core risk protection benefit.

Cover Period

• To the end of the month of the Life Assured’s: 60th, 65th or 70th birthday
Where the cover period of the Income Protector benefit is Whole of life, the Bonus Cover benefit
cover period will be limited To end of the month of the Life Assured’s 70th birthday.

Benefit Limits

The sum assured for this feature will be limited to the lesser of:
• 100% of the average bonuses earned over the last three years
• 3 times their monthly after-tax income
• An absolute rand amount of R450 000 for non-professionals and R600 000 for professionals.

Maximum Number of
Payments and Claims

The maximum number of payments for total and permanent occupational disability will be 7.

Cessation of Claims

An admitted claim will end on the earliest of:
• The benefit end date as specified in the policy summary; or
• The date on which the maximum number of seven annual claim payments have been made; or
• The death of the Life Assured; or
• The date, as decided by Liberty, on which:
− The Life Assured refuses to submit to any physical examinations or tests required by Liberty to
assess the continuation of an admitted claim; or
− The Policyholder fails to provide satisfactory evidence of the Life Assured’s continued incapacity
when requested to do so by Liberty from time to time; or
− There has been a material change in the Life Assured’s health, income or occupation that affects
the continuation or validity of the claim; or
− The Life Assured declines to undergo any medical treatment recommended by their own
medical practitioner or Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer, which they could reasonably be expected
to undergo, and which could substantially improve or remove the occupational disability.

The benefit is only available to salaried employees and on primary occupations. It will only be

(1)

What is the Bonus Cover?
The Bonus Cover benefit will be payable for total and
permanent occupational disability only, after the expiry
of the 24-month waiting period. The sum assured will be
specified and will be limited to 100% of the average cash
performance bonuses earned by the customer in the
previous 3 years. Claim payments will be made annually
on the claim anniversary for up to a maximum of 7 years,
provided the life assured is still in claim at the time of each
claim anniversary.
The Bonus Cover benefit is only available if you have the
Income Protector benefit with a Payment period of more
than 2 years. Further, the Bonus Cover benefit will only be
available to salaried employees and on primary occupations
only. It will only be applicable to your primary occupation if
you have selected dual occupations.

EXAMPLE

John purchased the Income Protector and selected the
Bonus Cover benefit. He wants to insure his cash bonus
as he is dependent on this to pay the school fees of his
children.
John’s bonuses for the past three years have been:
•
•
•

2017 = R40 000
2018 = R45 000
2019 = R50 000

The average amount of his bonus over the 3 years is
R45 000. John is able to select a Bonus Cover benefit
of up to R45 000. However, he chooses to only insure
R30 000 of his cash bonus.
John is injured in an accident and is unable to perform
any of the duties of his nominated primary occupation
and is considered totally and permanently occupationally
disabled.
John’s claim will be paid after the 24-month waiting
period where he will receive R30 000 annually on the
claim anniversary, and each year after that for up to
7 years.

What is the maximum sum assured I can select?
The sum assured for this benefit will be limited to the
lesser of:
•

100% of the average bonuses earned over the last three
years
3 times their monthly after-tax income
An absolute rand amount of R450 000 for nonprofessionals and R600 000 for professionals.

EXAMPLE

Vuyo works as an auditor and takes out an Income
Protector benefit and adds the Bonus Cover benefit.
She earns a monthly after-tax salary of R32 500 and has
received an average annual performance bonus of
R100 000 for the past three years.
The maximum Bonus Cover sum assured Vuyo can take
is the lesser of:
•
•
•

R100 000 (100% of the average bonuses earned
over the last three years)
R97 500 (3 times her monthly after-tax income)
R600 000 (An absolute rand amount of R600 000
for professionals)

Vuyo’s maximum Bonus Cover sum assured amount is
R97 500.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

When do I submit my proof of cash bonus?
Proof of cash bonus is an underwriting requirement when
taking out the Bonus Cover benefit. It is preferred that
proof of cash bonus is provided at underwriting stage but
can also be provided post-issue (the period before the next
policy anniversary following inception or reinstatement of
a new Bonus Cover benefit or amendments to an existing
Bonus Cover that would warrant a request for proof of cash
bonus). This also applies to proof of bonus requirements for
a servicing change where applicable.
What penalties exist if I do not submit proof of bonus
timeously?
If proof of cash bonus is received after the post-issue period
(the period before the next policy anniversary following
inception or reinstatement of a new Bonus Cover benefit
or amendments to an existing Bonus Cover that would
warrant a request for proof of cash bonus) and you are
deemed to be over-insured, any premiums paid for the overinsured portion of your sum assured will only be refunded
back to your last policy anniversary prior to receipt of proof
of cash bonus. Any expenses incurred will also be deducted
from the premiums to be refunded.
Can I make multiple claims?
No. The Bonus Cover benefit is only payable on total and
permanent occupational disability only and claim payments
will be made annually on the claim anniversary for up to a
maximum of 7 years, provided the policyholder is still in claim
at the time of each claim anniversary.
Will my premiums be waived?
The Bonus Cover monthly premium will be paid by Liberty a
claim is being paid.
What are the tax implications of this benefit?
This benefit is not taxable in the hands of the recipient.
Are there any other benefits which I must take with this
benefit?
Yes, this benefit is only available if you have also selected the
Income Protector benefit with a payment period of more
than 2 years.

OVERHEAD EXPENSES PROTECTOR
Benefit Description

The Overhead Expenses Protector provides up to 23 monthly claim payments of up to 75% of
business overheads if you are temporarily or permanently unable, due to illness or injury, to perform
the duties of your own occupation.

Benefit Type

This benefit is a non-accelerated benefit. The benefit is not a core risk protection benefit. In the event
of a claim, this benefit will not affect any of your other benefits.

Cover Period

• To the end of the month of the Life Assured’s 65th birthday

Benefit Limits

Minimum benefit:
Maximum benefit:

R5 000 p.m.
Lesser of R150 000 p.m. and 75% of actual business overheads

Aggregation across other insurance policies will be applied in determining the maximum benefit
amount.
Maximum Number of
Payments and Claims

The maximum number of payments for a disability will be 23.
Multiple claims are possible under the benefit provided that the maximum number of payments has not
already been paid in respect of the disability being claimed for, or where the disability is totally unrelated to a
previous disability for which the maximum number of payments has already been made.

Cessation of Claims

An admitted claim will end on the earliest of:
• The date on which the maximum number of payments have been made in respect of a period of
disability;
• The end of the cover period;
• Your death;
• The date, as decided by Liberty, on which:
− You are again capable of performing a substantial portion of the duties of your occupation;
− You no longer suffer an inability to meet the overhead expenses that is solely attributable to the
injury, illness, disease or surgical operation that gave rise to the claim;
− You refuse to submit to any physical examinations or tests required by Liberty to assess the
continuation of an admitted claim;
− You fail to provide satisfactory evidence of your continued incapacity when requested to do so
by Liberty from time to time;
− There has been a material change in your health, income or occupation that affects the
continuation or validity of the claim; or
− You decline to undergo any medical treatment recommended by your own medical practitioner
or Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer, which you could reasonably be expected to undergo and which
could substantially improve or remove the disability.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

OVERHEAD EXPENSES PROTECTOR (CONTINUED)
In-claim Escalation

No in-claim escalation will apply to this benefit.

Specific Exclusions

No claim will be assessed or become payable where the disability or other claim event arose directly
or indirectly as a result of cosmetic surgery, other than procedures necessitated by injury or illness
which occurred during the existence of this benefit.
No claim will be assessed or become payable if you decline to undergo any medical treatment
recommended by your own medical practitioner or Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer, which you could
reasonably be expected to undergo and which could substantially improve or remove the condition
being claimed for.
These exclusions apply in addition to the general exclusions of the policy.

Eligibility

The benefit is available to all lives. Depending on the risk classification of the client, Liberty will
determine if clients are eligible for Overhead Expenses Protector. This will be determined at quote
stage and depends on rating factors such as the education, income and occupation(s) of the client.
Only occupation groups A and B are eligible for the benefit. Occupation category C is not eligible
although Liberty’s underwriters may consider special merit cases. Please refer to the section below
for an explanation of the occupation groups and for examples of which occupations would be
included in the various occupation groups.

(1)The Renewable Premium Pattern is closed to New Business from July 2022.

What is the Overhead Expenses Protector benefit?

Can I make multiple claims?

The Overhead Expenses Protector benefit is a temporary
business overheads expense protection benefit. The benefit
allows you to protect up to 75% of your actual business
overheads if you are temporarily or permanently unable to
perform your occupation as a result of a disability.

Multiple Overhead Expenses Protector claims are allowed
provided that the maximum number of payments has not
already been made in respect of the disability or where the
claim is totally unrelated to a previous disability for which the
maximum number of claim payments has already been made.

The benefit will help to ensure that your business will not
be abruptly halted as a result of temporary disability. In
other cases, where a disability is of a longer-term nature, the
benefit can be used to help ensure that the business owner
can retain the value of the business while winding it up.

Will my premiums be waived while I am claiming?

Benefits only become payable after the expiry of the
1-month waiting period of the benefit.
The maximum number of payments you can receive for a
disability will be 23 monthly payments.
EXAMPLE

Hendrik, a business owner, buys the Overhead Expenses
Protector benefit. He suffers an injury which results in him
being unable to perform his occupation. The Overhead
Expenses Protector benefit will pay Hendrik the
sum assured up to 75% of his actual business overhead
expenses for up to 23 months, after the expiry of the
1-month waiting period.

No, premiums will not be waived for the Overhead
Expenses Protector benefit. Insurance premiums are
included in the definition of overhead expenses and,
therefore, the premiums for this benefit could potentially be
included in a claim amount if the insurance premiums are
actually a part of the business’ expenses.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What is the definition of overhead expenses?

Who is eligible for this benefit?

Overhead expenses include only your proportion of business
overhead expenses, as determined by your percentage
interest in the business.

Liberty has developed a classification to determine which
business and professional people are eligible to take out this
benefit based on need.

Overhead expenses include:
• Salaries and/or wages of employees subject to the
exclusions below;
• Rent on business premises (that the commissioner of
inland revenue allows as a deduction);
• Electricity, water, telephone;
• Regular maintenance services;
• Property taxes and mortgage interest payments on
business premises;
• Leasing costs of equipment that is an essential feature
of the business;
• Insurance premiums;
• Accounting fees; and
• Any other fixed expenses, which are normal and
necessary in conducting the business and have been
declared to and accepted by Liberty.
Overhead expenses do not include:
• Depreciation;
•

Salaries to members of your immediate family, or
profession or trade, or business associates, or any
person performing your duties in your absence;

•

Fees, drawings;

•

Any remuneration for yourself;

•

Additions to the inventory;

•

The cost of any types of goods or merchandise;

•

The cost of furniture or equipment or other expenditure
on assets;

•

Capital repayments on outstanding debt; or

•

Expenses recoverable under any disability insurance.

EXAMPLE

Consider a lawyer who works for a large institution and
a lawyer who runs her own small practice. Both lawyers
are considered professionals. However, only the second
lawyer may have a need for the Overhead Expenses
Protector benefit. In the case of the second lawyer, if
she were to become unable to perform her occupation,
the business will have difficulty in paying its overhead
expenses.
The classification system groups occupations into one of
three groups – A, B or C. Only Group A and Group B are
eligible to take out this benefit. Occupations belonging to
Group C are not eligible to take out this benefit, although
Liberty’s underwriters may consider special merit cases.
What are the tax implications of this benefit?
This benefit is not taxable in the hands of the recipient.
Are there any other benefits which I must take with this
benefit?
Yes, this benefit is only available if you have also selected the
Income Protector benefit.

RETRENCHMENT PROTECTOR - RE-OPENED TO NEW BUSINESS SEPTEMBER 2024
Benefit Description

The Retrenchment Protector benefit pays the monthly sum assured (capped at R30 000 per
month), after the expiry of a 1-month waiting period, for up to 6 months* if you are retrenched from
full-time employment.

Premium Waiver

The Retrenchment Protector monthly premium will be paid by Liberty in addition to the monthly
claim payment while a Retrenchment Protector claim is being paid. Therefore, the premium is
effectively waived while a claim is being paid.

Benefit Type

This benefit is a non-accelerated benefit. The benefit is not a core risk protection benefit.

Cover Period

• To the end of the month of the Life Assured’s 60th birthday.

Benefit Limits

Minimum benefit:

R2 000 p.m.

Maximum monthly benefit will be calculated as the maximum of:
• 2.5% of the combined Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1) sum assured (if applicable)
• 75% of Income Protector sum assured (if applicable).
Subject to an overall maximum of the lesser of 75% of after-tax insurable monthly income or R30 000
per month (aggregated across all policies).
The figures above represent the maximum allowable sum assured. A lower Retrenchment Protector
sum assured may be selected.

Premium Guarantees

Retrenchment Protector premiums are not guaranteed and Liberty reserves the right to change
premiums at least once a year. We will advise you of any changes at least 31 days before it is
implemented.

Maximum Number of
Payments and Claims

A maximum of 6 payments* will be made per retrenchment.

Cessation of Claims

An admitted claim will end on the earliest of:

A maximum of 2 retrenchment claims will be admitted (irrespective of whether the maximum
number of payments was actually made for each period of retrenchment). Once the first claim has
been admitted and payment has ceased, 24 months must elapse before any further claims will be
considered.

• The date that you find new employment;
• You stop providing proof of unemployment on a monthly basis (through registration as an
unemployed individual with the unemployment insurance fund);
• The Retrenchment Protector benefit end date;
• The date when the sixth payment* is made in respect of a period of retrenchment; and
• Your death.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

RETRENCHMENT PROTECTOR (CONTINUED)

Specific Exclusions

A claim will only be admitted if the benefit has been in force for at least six months at the Date of
Retrenchment, and where there is at least three months – from benefit inception date - before
section 189 and 189A of the Labour Relations Act 66 of 1995 is lodged by the company.
To claim you must have been in permanent employment for an uninterrupted period of at least 24
months prior to the Date of Retrenchment with the last 12 months of this period being with current
employer. Over a 24-month period, one break of less than a month between employers will still be
considered as uninterrupted employment.
Liberty will not accept claims if you:
• Resign or retire;
• Accept voluntary Retrenchment prior to or outside of a formal retrenchment consultation process
as defined in section 189 and section 189A of the Labour Relations Act (1995);
• Come to the expected finish date of a fixed-term contract;
• Finish the job you were specifically employed to do;
• Lose your job due to:
− strikes in which you took part or lock-outs by your employer;
− misconduct, fraud or dishonesty;
− sickness, disability or a medical condition, whether directly or indirectly related;
− participation in a criminal act; or
− nationalisation or other government action;
• Are employed at a branch or office outside of South Africa; or
• Were already aware of your Retrenchment before the commencement of the benefit or the
benefit being reinstated.
The following lives do not qualify for cover and are specifically excluded from claiming under the
Retrenchment Protector benefit:
• Contract workers, seasonal workers, part-time workers, temporary workers, casual workers or
workers whose primary source of income is commission related;
• Self-employed individuals;
• Employees not contributing to UIF;
• Directors of companies; and
• Family members of the owners of the employer; and
• Family members of the directors of the employer.
These exclusions apply in addition to the general exclusions of the policy.

Eligibility

The following lives do not qualify for cover and are specifically excluded from claiming under the
benefit:
• Contract workers, seasonal workers, part-time workers, temporary workers, casual workers or
workers whose primary source of income is commission related;
• Self-employed individuals;
• Employees not contributing to UIF;
• Directors of companies; and
• Family members of the owners of the employer; and
• Family members of the directors of the employer.
The benefit is available to all lives. Depending on the risk classification of the client, Liberty will
determine if clients are eligible for the Retrenchment Protector. This will be determined at quote
stage and depends on rating factors such as the education, income and occupation(s) of the client.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

What is the Retrenchment Protector benefit?
The Retrenchment Protector benefit is an Income
Protection benefit which protects you against the risk of
being retrenched and losing income as a result. The benefit
allows you to cover up to 75% of your after-tax insurable
monthly income (capped at R30 000 per month) for up to
six months* per period of retrenchment.
How is retrenchment defined in terms of the benefit?
Retrenchment is defined as the termination of employment
in terms of sections 189 and 189A of the Labour Relations
Act (1995), as amended.
There is a specific list of circumstances which do not
constitute retrenchment in terms of the benefit as detailed in
the specific exclusions section.
How long do I need to wait after retrenchment before I
start receiving benefits?
A 1-month waiting period applies to this benefit. Therefore,
following your retrenchment, you will need to be
unemployed for a full month before you can start receiving a
claim payment.
How will I prove that I am retrenched?
You will need to provide Liberty with written notice that you
are retrenched within one month of your retrenchment. You
will also need to provide proof that you are unemployed
on a monthly basis through registration as an unemployed
individual with the Unemployment Insurance Fund in order
to claim and continue claiming under the benefit.
How many payments will I receive once I start claiming?
For each period of retrenchment, you may receive a
maximum of 6 monthly payments* provided you remain
unemployed over the period.
Are there other important restrictions on claiming?
Yes. A claim will only be admitted if the benefit has been in
force for at least six months at the Date of Retrenchment,
and where there is at least three months – from benefit
inception date - before section 189 and 189A of the Labour
Relations Act 66 of 1995 is lodged by the company. To claim
you must have been in permanent employment for an
uninterrupted period of at least 24 months prior to the Date
of Retrenchment with the last 12 months of this period being
with current employer.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

The 6 month no claim period will reapply on any part of the
benefit which is increased (other than through automatic
benefit increases). Therefore where a claim is admitted for
a retrenchment within 6 months of an increase in the sum
assured, other than through an automatic benefit increase,
the claim amount to be paid will be based on the sum
assured which would have applied had the increase not
occurred.
These rules apply to avoid people, who know or strongly
suspect that they are going to be retrenched, taking out
the benefit and then immediately claiming. Allowing this
to happen would result in Liberty having to charge a much
higher premium which would make the cost of the benefit
unaffordable for all other clients.
It is also important that you take note of what does and does
not constitute a retrenchment in terms of the benefit.
Can I make multiple claims?
Yes, up to two periods of retrenchment will be covered.
Once the first claim has been admitted and ceases, any
further claims will only be considered once 24 months
have elapsed and if you have been in full-time and active
employment for 12 months with the same employer before
the date of another retrenchment.
Once a second claim which has been admitted ceases, the
benefit will cease.
Will my premiums be waived while I am claiming?
The Retrenchment Protector monthly premium will be
paid by Liberty in addition to the monthly claim payment
while a Retrenchment Protector claim is being paid.
Therefore, the premium is effectively waived while a claim is
being paid.
Will I need to prove my income when I claim?
The intention of this benefit is that no more than 75% of
after-tax insurable monthly income is covered.
Liberty’s preference is that proof of income is provided when
the benefit is purchased and whenever the sum assured is
increased (excluding automatic benefit increases). Where
proof of income is provided at these points then, should a
claim occur, it will not be a requirement to prove income and
your sum assured will not be limited by your actual pre-claim
income.
However, if you choose not to provide proof of income at
these points then, should a claim occur, proof of income
will be required before a claim is admitted. The actual claim
amount will be limited to 75% of the pre-claim after-tax
insurable monthly income which is actually proven.

* A 12-month payment period is available on the Loan Protection package only. Please refer to the Loan Protection package Annexure for more details.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Do all lives qualify for this benefit?
No. The following lives do not qualify for cover and
are specifically excluded from claiming under the
Retrenchment Protector benefit:
•

•
•
•
•
•

Contract workers, seasonal workers, part-time workers,
temporary workers, casual workers or workers whose
primary source of income is commission related;
Self-employed individuals;
Employees not contributing to UIF;
Directors of companies; and
Family members of the owners of the employer; and
Family members of the directors of the employer.

It is important that, if you initially qualify for the benefit but
then your employment status changes, you review your
Retrenchment Protector benefit as your new employment
status may not qualify for benefits.
What are the tax implications of this benefit?
This benefit is not taxable in the hands of the recipient.
Are there any other benefits which I must take with this
benefit?
To select Retrenchment Protector at least one of the
following benefits must be selected on the policy:
•
•

Life Cover
Renewable Life Cover(1)

•

Income Protector

The sum assured of the Retrenchment Protector will also
be restricted relative to the sum assured you have selected
for these benefits.
Does the Future Income Assessment facility extend to
Retrenchment Protector?
The Future Income Assessment facility is a sum assured
review facility which allows you to increase your Income
Protector benefit in-line with changes in your income,
without having to provide medical evidence of insurability or
a statement of good health (please see Income Protector
section for full details). When using this facility to increase
your Income Protector sum assured, you will also be able to
increase your Retrenchment Protector sum assured at the
same time. Your Retrenchment Protector increases will be
limited by the increases selected on the Income Protector
benefit.
(1)

The Renewable Life Cover option is closed to New Business from July 2022.

(2)

section 189 and 189A of the Labour Relations Act 66 of 1995 is lodged by the company.
To claim you must have been in permanent employment for an uninterrupted period of at least 24
months prior to the Date of Retrenchment with the last 12 months of this period being with current
employer. Over a 24-month period, one break of less than a month between employers will still be
considered as uninterrupted employment.
Liberty will not accept claims if you:
• Resign or retire;
• Accept voluntary Retrenchment prior to or outside of a formal retrenchment consultation process
as defined in section 189 and section 189A of the Labour Relations Act (1995);
• Come to the expected finish date of a fixed-term contract;
• Finish the job you were specifically employed to do;
• Lose your job due to: strikes in which you took part or lock-outs by your employer;
− misconduct, fraud or dishonesty;
− sickness, disability or a medical condition, whether directly or indirectly related;
− participation in a criminal act; or
− nationalisation or other government action;
• Are employed at a branch or office outside of South Africa; or
• Were already aware of your Retrenchment before the commencement of the benefit or the
benefit being reinstated.
The following lives do not qualify for cover and are specifically excluded from claiming under the
Retrenchment Protector benefit:
• Contract workers, seasonal workers, part-time workers, temporary workers, casual workers or
workers whose primary source of income is commission related;
• Self-employed individuals;
• Employees not contributing to UIF;
• Directors of companies; and
• Family members of the owners of the employer; and
• Family members of the directors of the employer.
These exclusions apply in addition to the general exclusions of the policy.

Eligibility

The following lives do not qualify for cover and are specifically excluded from claiming under the
benefit:
• Contract workers, seasonal workers, part-time workers, temporary workers, casual workers or
workers whose primary source of income is commission related;
• Self-employed individuals;
• Employees not contributing to UIF;
• Directors of companies; and
• Family members of the owners of the employer; and
• Family members of the directors of the employer.
The benefit is available to all lives. Depending on the risk classification of the client, Liberty will
determine if clients are eligible for the Retrenchment Protector. This will be determined at quote
stage and depends on rating factors such as the education, income and occupation(s) of the client.

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

What is the Retrenchment Protector - Voluntary Home
Loan Cover benefit?
The Retrenchment Protector benefit is an Income
Protection benefit which protects you against the risk of
being retrenched and losing income as a result. The benefit
allows you to cover up to 1.25% of your home loan amount
(limited to 75% of your after-tax insurable monthly income
and capped at R30 000 per month) for up to six months* per
period of retrenchment.
What is the qualification criterion for the benefit?
This benefit can only be taken where proof of a home loan,
that has been approved within 6 months of the application,
is provided.
How is retrenchment defined in terms of the benefit?
Retrenchment is defined as the termination of employment
in terms of sections 189 and 189A of the Labour Relations Act
(1995), as amended.
There is a specific list of circumstances which do not
constitute retrenchment in terms of the benefit as detailed in
the specific exclusions section.
How long do I need to wait after retrenchment before I
start receiving benefits?
A 1-month waiting period applies to this benefit. Therefore,
following your retrenchment, you will need to be unemployed
for a full month before you can start receiving a claim
payment.
The 6 month no claim period will reapply on any part of the
benefit which is increased (other than through automatic
benefit increases). Therefore where a claim is admitted for
a retrenchment within 6 months of an increase in the sum
assured, other than through an automatic benefit increase,
the claim amount to be paid will be based on the sum assured
which would have applied had the increase not occurred.
These rules apply to avoid people, who know or strongly
suspect that they are going to be retrenched, taking out
the benefit and then immediately claiming. Allowing this
to happen would result in Liberty having to charge a much
higher premium which would make the cost of the benefit
unaffordable for all other clients.
It is also important that you take note of what does and does
not constitute a retrenchment in terms of the benefit.
Can I make multiple claims?
Yes, up to two periods of retrenchment will be covered. Once
the first claim has been admitted and ceases, any further
claims will only be considered once 24 months have elapsed
and if you have been in full-time and active employment
for 12 months with the same employer before the date of
another retrenchment.
Once a second claim which has been admitted ceases,
the benefit will cease, unemployed on a monthly basis

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

through registration as an unemployed individual with
the Unemployment Insurance Fund in order to claim and
continue claiming under the benefit..
How will I prove that I am retrenched?
You will need to provide Liberty with written notice that you
are retrenched within one month of your retrenchment. You
will also need to provide proof that you are unemployed
on a monthly basis through registration as an unemployed
individual with the Unemployment Insurance Fund in order to
claim and continue claiming under the benefit.
How many payments will I receive once I start claiming?
For each period of retrenchment, you may receive a
maximum of 6 monthly payments* provided you remain
unemployed over the period.
Are there other important restrictions on claiming?
Yes. A claim will only be admitted if the benefit has been in
force for at least six months at the Date of Retrenchment,
and where there is at least three months – from benefit
inception date - before section 189 and 189A of the Labour
Relations Act 66 of 1995 is lodged by the company. To claim
you must have been in permanent employment for an
uninterrupted period of at least 24 months prior to the Date
of Retrenchment with the last 12 months of this period being
with current employer.
Will my premiums be waived while I am claiming?
The Retrenchment Protector monthly premium will be paid
by Liberty in addition to the monthly claim payment while a
Retrenchment Protector claim is being paid. Therefore, the
premium is effectively waived while a claim is being paid.
Will I need to prove my income when I claim?
The intention of the benefit is that no more than 1.25% of the
home loan amount (and no more than 75% of your after-tax
insurable monthly income) is covered. In addition to proof of
income, for the purposes of this benefit, we will also require
proof of the home loan that was approved within 6 months
of application for this policy or proof of replacement of
existing cover for a home loan (please note that this is not for
compulsory credit life purposes).
Liberty’s preference is that proof of income is provided when
the benefit is purchased and whenever the sum assured is
increased (excluding automatic benefit increases). Where
proof of income is provided at these points then, should a
claim occur, it will not be a requirement to prove income and
your sum assured will not be limited by your actual pre-claim
income.
However, if you choose not to provide proof of income at
these points then, should a claim occur, proof of income
will be required before a claim is admitted. The actual claim
amount will be limited to 75% of the pre-claim after-tax
insurable monthly income which is actually proven.

GUARANTEED DEPENDANT PROTECTOR
Benefit Description

The Guaranteed Dependant Protector pays up to 12 times your Income Protector sum
assured as a lump sum to your beneficiaries where you die within 12 months from the first
claim payment of an Income Protector claim that is being paid at the time of your death.
The Income Protector sum assured used will refer to the cover amount payable monthly
within the first 24 months, or if this cover is not in place, the cover amount payable monthly
after 24 months.
The multiple of the Income Protector sum assured that is payable, will be between 1 and
12 depending on the number of monthly claim payments that have already been paid for
the Income Protector claim in payment at the time of death.

Benefit Type

This benefit is a non-accelerated benefit. The benefit is not a core risk protection benefit.

Cover Period

The cover period will automatically correspond to the Income Protector cover period
selected.

Dependant Protector benefit cover period will be limited To end of the month of the Life
Assured’s 70th birthday.

The benefit amount corresponds to the Income Protector sum assured and, therefore, the
benefit limits of the Income Protector benefit will automatically apply.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What is the Guaranteed Dependant Protector benefit?
The Guaranteed Dependant Protector benefit pays your beneficiaries a lump sum when you die within a year of having an
Income Protector claim admitted and that is being paid at the time of your death.
The lump sum amount payable will be a multiple between 1 and 12 of your Income Protector sum assured, depending on how
many Income Protector payments have already been made for the claim in payment at the time of your death.
The aim of the benefit is to ensure that once you are disabled and receiving a payment for your Income Protector benefit,
your dependants’ short-term financial security is guaranteed irrespective of the outcome of that disability or permanent
impairment. Once you start receiving payments for a disability or permanent impairment there are three main possible
outcomes for you and your dependants’ financial security during the first 12 months:
•
•
•

You could remain disabled or impaired and continue to receive Income Protector payments. You and your dependants
will have financial security over the balance of the period.
You could recover and return to work and start earning an income again. You and your dependants will have financial
security over the balance of the period.
You could pass away. Your dependants’ financial security is uncertain over the balance of the period.

The Guaranteed Dependant Protector aims to provide financial security for your dependants in the third scenario, which is
not covered by traditional temporary income protection benefits.

Twelve months’ financial security of a traditional
temporary income protection benefit

You recover

are financially secure over the balance
of the period.

You die after being disabled
Gap in traditional Income Protection
cover. Your dependants are not
financially secure over the balance of
the period.

Income Protector. Your dependants
are financially secure over the balance
of the period.

How is the multiple of sum assured calculated?
The multiple will be calculated as 12 less the number of full monthly payments which have already been made under the
Income Protector claim in payment at the time of your death. This ensures that the value of payments received under the
Income Protector benefit and the Guaranteed Dependant Protector combine to ensure that at least the equivalent of 1
year’s Income Protector benefit is received in total, even if you die, once a claim payment commences.
If you die after having already received 12 or more full monthly claim payments for the Income Protector benefit for the
claim in question, the Guaranteed Dependant Protector multiple will effectively be zero and no Guaranteed Dependant
Protector claim payment will be made.

Yes, the intention of the Guaranteed Dependant Protector
benefit is to guarantee financial security to your dependants
once an Income Protector claim has been admitted.
Therefore, an Income Protector claim must have been
admitted and being paid at the time of death to qualify for a
Guaranteed Dependant Protector claim.

passes away after having received only three full monthly
payments on the Income Protector benefit.
As Ruby received three full monthly payments, the multiple
of the Guaranteed Dependant Protector will be payable
as follows:
•

Multiple of sum assured payable is calculated as

This ensures that Ruby’s dependants are not left financially
destitute after already having gone through the traumatic
event of Ruby’s disability and death.

Will the full benefit amount still be paid even if my

of the sum assured at the time of death?
Yes, even if your Income Protector claim was being paid at

impairment claim), the calculation of a Guaranteed
Dependant Protector claim will still be based on the full

BENEFITS | LOSS OF INCOME PROTECTION PILLAR: INCOME RANGE | OCTOBER 2025

Income Protector sum assured.
Does the Guaranteed Dependant Protector replace the
need for life cover?
No, this benefit does not replace or meet the same need as
life cover. This benefit is specifically designed to complement
the Income Protector benefit by adding security to the
financial circumstances of your dependants once you have
suffered a disability or permanent Impairment and have
started claiming on your Income Protector benefit. In some
circumstances this benefit and any life cover benefit which
you have would both pay out. In these circumstances any
payment under the Guaranteed Dependant Protector
will complement the life cover benefit payment because it
can, for example, be used to fund the living expenses of your
dependants for a temporary period of time while a more
difficult and longer-term decision is being made about how
to use or invest the life cover benefit amount received.

The sum assured for this benefit is directly related to the
Income Protector sum assured. Therefore, in order to
select the Guaranteed Dependant Protector benefit the
Income Protector benefit must be present on the policy.

The Living Lifestyle Protector complies with the
standardised critical illness definitions adopted by the

definitions. This guarantees that the Living Lifestyle
Protector benefit criteria are objective and consistent
with the minimum industry standards. The following table
