What is Medical Advancements Protection and what
does it cover?
The Living Lifestyle Protector benefit covers specific
medical conditions using specific claim definitions. These
definitions help to determine the level of severity of the
conditions, as to whether a payout should be made and
how much will be paid out. To ensure that the definitions
are objective and transparent, they are often based on a
specified medical classification system, medical diagnostic
test, medical surgery or procedure.
While these are appropriate at the time the policy is bought,
there is a risk that medical advancements might result in the
criteria becoming outdated over time. To protect you from
this, we include the Medical Advancements Protection
(MAP) feature. MAP allows future claims to be considered for
payment where a medical classification system, diagnostic test
or investigation or medical surgery or procedure relating to a
claim definition becomes outdated and is no longer a standard
of clinical care due to medical advancements.

REQUIREMENT
(Must be met in the opinion of Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer)

The specific claim definition is eligible for MAP coverage (as shown in the Living Lifestyle Series Benefit Payout Summary section)

replaced by the new classification system, diagnostic test or investigation, surgery or procedure

The diagnosis, severity level and clinical manifestation is the same as would have been required
The new diagnostic or investigative parameters must reach a level that implies a severity level that

The new surgery/procedure is not experimental and is medically necessary and medically

A minimum lifestyle impact still occurs – at least 1 of the following must hold:
• The new surgery or procedure must have the same or a higher level of invasiveness
• An average person would be booked off work for at least 3 weeks to undergo and recover from
the new surgery or procedure
• Similar residual impact on physical or mental abilities as the original surgery or procedure would
have caused

What is not covered through MAP?
In addition to claims which do not meet the stated

How does the Whole of Life cover period operate once
the Income Protector term ends?

•

If you have selected the Whole of Life cover period, your
Living Lifestyle Protector benefit will continue after your
Income Protector benefit reaches the end of the cover
period you selected. Your sum assured will continue to
increase with automatic benefit increases that apply on
your policy and that would have applied on the Income
Protector benefit, had it remained in force.

•

•

New medical or surgical procedures, which are
considered, in the opinion of Liberty’s Chief Medical
Officer, preventative or prophylactic.
An underlying condition which has been identified
as a result of screening tests (for example isolated
genetic screening tests) but where there is no clinical
manifestation of the illness or condition.
A medical procedure or surgery which is already
covered, or an equivalent procedure or surgery is
already covered, at a lower severity level.

Will claims under the Living Lifestyle Protector
accelerate payments under the Income Protector
benefit?
No, payments under the Living Lifestyle Protector will not
affect your ability to claim under the Income Protector benefit.
EXAMPLE

James buys the Income Protector benefit and the
Living Lifestyle Protector benefit. His sum assured
for the Income Protector sum assured is R30 000 per
month and, therefore, his Living Lifestyle Protector
sum assured will also automatically be R30 000.
James suffers a renal disorder which qualifies him for a
payout on the 3-month severity level. He can choose to
receive R30 000 for 3 months or a lump sum of
R90 000 (i.e. 3 times R30 000). He chose to receive a
lump sum amount.
James’ condition worsens over time and he eventually
meets the requirements for a 24-month severity level claim.
Given that James has already been paid a 3-month claim,
only the difference of 21 times the sum assured
(i.e. 24 less 3) will be paid. James will be paid R630 000
(i.e. 21 times R30 000) as he chose to receive a lump sum
amount.

Are there any other benefits which I must take with this
benefit?
The sum assured for this benefit is directly related to the
Income Protector sum assured. Therefore, in order to
select the Living Lifestyle Protector benefit the Income
Protector benefit must be present on the policy.

LIVING LIFESTYLE DEPENDANT PROTECTOR
Benefit Description

The Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector pays out 6 times the Income Protector sum assured
as a monthly payment or lump sum if your spouse or your child suffers a critical illness covered by the
benefit. The Income Protector sum assured used will refer to the cover amount payable monthly
within the first 24 months, or if this cover is not in place, the cover amount payable monthly after 24
months.

Medical Advancements
Protection

The Medical Advancements Protection (MAP) feature is automatically included and provides
enhanced protection against the risk that specified claim definitions linked to medical classification
systems, medical diagnostic tests and investigations or medical procedures or surgeries become
outdated due to medical advancements.

Benefit Type

This benefit is a non-accelerated benefit. The benefit is not a core risk protection benefit.

Cover Period

• To the end of the month of the Life Assured’s 60th birthday
Children will only be covered if they are 18 or younger (and unmarried) at the date of diagnosis. There
is no restriction on the age of spouses.
The cover period relates to your age and not your spouse or your child’s age.

Benefit Limits

The benefit amount corresponds to the Income Protector sum assured and, therefore, the benefit
limits of the Income Protector benefit will automatically apply.
Only one Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector benefit is allowed per life assured across all Liberty
policies.

Maximum Number of
Payments and Claims

A maximum of one spouse claim will be admitted under the benefit.
A maximum of one claim per child will be accepted under the benefit.
An overall maximum of three claims will be admitted under the benefit.

Premium Payment Period

Survival Period

A 14-day survival period applies to this benefit. This means that your spouse or child needs to survive
for at least 14 days after diagnosis of a condition before they qualify for benefits.

Specific Exclusions

Specific exclusions are listed under the various Benefit Categories.
No claim will be admitted for claims where the condition arose directly or indirectly from a condition
or circumstance that occurred before the inception of the benefit.

Eligibility

(1)

The Income Protector eligibility criteria will automatically apply. Therefore, the benefit is available to
all lives. Depending on the risk classification of the client, Liberty will determine if clients are eligible for
the Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector. This will be determined at quote stage and depends on
rating factors such as the education, income and occupation(s) of the client.

The Renewable Premium Pattern is closed to New Business from July 2022.

What is the Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector

The Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector pays out 6
times the Income Protector sum assured as a monthly
income or lump sum if your spouse or your child suffers a
critical illness covered by the benefit. The Income Protector
sum assured used will refer to the cover amount payable
monthly within the first 24 months, or if this cover is not in

place, the cover amount payable monthly after 24 months.
What is the meaning of Spouse and Child in terms of this

For this benefit, a spouse includes the person you are legally
married to either by civil or customary law, by civil union,
by Asiatic relationship or the person whom you are in a
same-sex or heterosexual cohabiting relationship with. Your

Does Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector meet SCIDEP
definitions?
The Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector complies
with the standardised critical illness definitions adopted by
the Association for Savings and Investment SA (ASISA) for
spouse claims. These definitions are commonly referred to
as the SCIDEP definitions. This guarantees that the Living
Lifestyle Dependant Protector benefit criteria are objective
and consistent with the minimum industry standards. The
following table shows the multiple of sum assured that the
Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector pays out for each of
the four severity levels for each of the SCIDEP events.

What are the requirements to claim through MAP?
For a claim to be considered through MAP it must meet a few requirements:
These definitions help to determine the level of severity of the conditions, as to whether a payout should be made and
how much will be paid out. To ensure that the definitions are objective and transparent, they are often based on a specified
medical classification system, medical diagnostic test, medical surgery or procedure. While these are appropriate at the time
the policy is bought, there is a risk that medical advancements might result in the criteria becoming outdated over time.
To protect you from this, we include the Medical Advancements Protection (MAP) feature.

MEDICAL
SURGERIESAND
PROCEDURES

REQUIREMENT
(Must be met in the opinion of Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer)

MEDICAL
DIAGNOSTICTESTS &
NVESTIGATIONS

MAP allows future claims to be considered for payment where a medical classification system, diagnostic test or investigation
or medical surgery or procedure relating to a claim definition becomes outdated and is no longer a standard of clinical care due
to medical advancements.

MEDICAL
CLASSIFICATION
SYSTEM

The specific claim definition is eligible for MAP coverage (as shown in the Living Lifestyle Series Benefit Payout Summary section)
The old classification system, diagnostic test or investigation, surgery or procedure has been
replaced by the new classification system, diagnostic test or investigation, surgery or procedure
The diagnosis, severity level and clinical manifestation is the same as would have been required
The new diagnostic or investigative parameters must reach a level that implies a severity level that
is at least equivalent to or worse than implied by the original diagnostic or investigative test
The new surgery/procedure is not experimental and is medically necessary and medically
equivalent or superior to the original surgery or procedure
A minimum lifestyle impact still occurs – at least 1 of the following must hold:
• The new surgery or procedure must have the same or a higher level of invasiveness
• An average person would be booked off work for at least 3 weeks to undergo and recover from
the new surgery or procedure
• Similar residual impact on physical or mental abilities as the original surgery or procedure would
have caused

What is not covered through MAP?
In addition to claims which do not meet the stated
requirements, the following will not be covered:
• New medical or surgical procedures, which are
considered, in the opinion of Liberty’s Chief Medical
Officer, preventative or prophylactic.
• An underlying condition which has been identified
as a result of screening tests (for example isolated
genetic screening tests) but where there is no clinical
manifestation of the illness or condition.
• A medical procedure or surgery which is already covered,
or an equivalent procedure or surgery is already covered,
at a lower severity level.
Will my spouse and children need to provide medical
evidence of insurability to take out this benefit?
Your spouse and child will not be required to provide medical
evidence of insurability. However, you will need to complete
a declaration that they are in good health. It is also important

to note that no claim will be admitted for claims where the
condition arose directly or indirectly from a condition or
circumstance that occurred before the inception of the
benefit.

The sum assured for this benefit is directly related to the

the Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector benefit the
Income Protector benefit must be present on the policy.
Only one Living Lifestyle Dependant Protector benefit is
allowed per life assured across all Liberty policies.

4.3 LIFESTYLE PROTECTION PILLAR
LIVING LIFESTYLE
Benefit Description

Living Lifestyle is an accelerated benefit that provides a lump sum payable on diagnosis of any critical
illness that is covered under the Benefit Categories.

Top-Up Option

This is an option that can be selected with Living Lifestyle on policies that do not have term benefits
and is automatically included on policies that have term benefits. 100% of your sum assured will be paid
for certain Benefit Categories where the severity is such that only 25%, 50% or 75% of the sum assured
would have been paid if this option was not selected.

Extended Option

This is an option that can be selected with Living Lifestyle on policies that do not have term benefits and
is automatically included on policies that have term benefits. If this option is selected, either 5% or 10% of
the Living Lifestyle sum assured is paid in the event of certain specified claim events occurring.
The Extended option has been upgraded for new business and introduced as an opt-in upgrade for
existing policies with Extended option. This covers early cancer cover and covers mild illness with a payout
increased to 10% of the Living Lifestyle sum assured amount.

Medical Advancements
Protection

The Medical Advancements Protection (MAP) feature is automatically included and provides enhanced
protection against the risk that specified claim definitions linked to medical classification systems, medical
diagnostic tests and investigations or medical procedures or surgeries become outdated due to medical
advancements.

Benefit Type

This is an accelerated benefit that can be added to either or both of the Life Cover and the Renewable
Life Cover(1) benefits on the policy.
If you have selected a term benefit, it will reduce the total of your Life Cover (Term) sum assured by the
amount of any claim made. Your other accelerated benefits may also be affected by claiming since the
maximum accelerated benefit cover allowed is restricted to the amount of your Life Cover (Term) sum
assured.
If you have selected the To end of the month of the Life Assured’s 65th birthday or Whole of Life cover
period this benefit reduces the total of your Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(1) by the amount of
any claim made. Your other accelerated benefits may also be affected by claiming since the maximum
accelerated benefit cover allowed is restricted to the amount of your Life Cover plus your Renewable
Life Cover(1).

Cover Period

Whole of Life
To policy anniversary immediately preceding to the Life Assured’s 65th birthday.
Term: Minimum - 5 years.
Maximum - Lesser of 70 minus age next birthday and 25 years.

Benefit Limits

Minimum: R100 000
Maximum: R9 mil

What is the Living Lifestyle benefit?
Living Lifestyle is an accelerated benefit that pays a lump
sum for a claim arising from any critical illness that is covered
under the Benefit Categories.
How do the Top-Up and Extended options work?

Top-Up and Extended are automatically included on policies
that have term benefits.

The Top-Up and/or Extended options can be selected with
Living Lifestyle. A claim under these options will therefore
also accelerate the Life Cover or the Renewable Life
Cover(1) sum assured. The Top-Up option provides a 100%
payout of the sum assured if the severity of the claim is such
that less than 100% of the sum assured would be paid for

What are the Benefit Categories and claim percentages
applicable to different severity levels?

EXAMPLE 1

Detailed information is included at the end of the Living
Lifestyle benefit section. However, you should always refer
to your policy document to see which Benefit Categories
and claim percentages are applicable to your policy.

Mr Jones has purchased a Lifestyle Protector policy
with Life Cover of R1 000 000. To this he adds Living
Lifestyle with a sum assured of R1 000 000.

What type and level of cover does the Living Lifestyle
benefit provide?
Your level of cover (i.e. your sum assured) will increase
every year, provided you have selected an annual automatic
benefit increase.
Living Lifestyle has 30 Benefit Categories. Every claim you
submit is assessed based on the severity of your condition
using specified payment criteria. Any claim for the Living
Lifestyle benefit will reduce the level of life cover by the
amount of the claim. In addition, all future Living Lifestyle
claims will also be based on the reduced level of cover.
Once you have claimed under a particular Benefit Category,
you will qualify for another claim under the following
circumstances:
1.

2.

Your condition worsens or you suffer a related claim and
now qualify for a higher percentage payout level.
The new claim will be based on the new percentage
payout level that you qualify for applied to your sum
assured at that time. (Note that the sum assured would
have been reduced as a result of the previous claim.)
The cause of your subsequent claims of the same stage
or severity already claimed for is totally unrelated to any
prior claims.
The new claim will be based on the new percentage
payout level that you qualify for applied to your sum
assured at that time. (Note that the sum assured would
have been reduced as a result of the previous claim.)

Mr Jones is diagnosed with Stage I Liver Cancer. Under
his Living Lifestyle benefit he qualifies for 25% of his
sum assured of R1 000 000. He receives a payment
of R250 000. Both his Life Cover and his Living
Lifestyle sum assured decrease by this amount to
R750 000. His premium is recalculated based on the
new sum assured of R750 000 for Life Cover and
Living Lifestyle.
A year later, Mr Jones’s condition has worsened and he
now has Stage II Liver Cancer. He now qualifies for 50%
of his reduced sum assured of R750 000 under the
Cancer and Leukaemia Benefit Category. He receives
a payment of R375 000 (i.e. 50% of R750 000). Both
his Life Cover and his Living Lifestyle sum assured
decrease by this amount to R375 000. His premium is
recalculated based on the new sum assured of
R375 000 for the Life Cover and Living Lifestyle.
Top-up option scenario
If Mr Jones had the Living Lifestyle with sum assured
as above, but had also selected the Top-Up option, he
would have qualified for a payment of 100% of his sum
assured when diagnosed with Stage I Liver Cancer. He
would have received a payment of R1 000 000 and
both his Life Cover and Living Lifestyle would cease
(i.e. no subsequent claims would have been paid).

EXAMPLE 2

If you qualify for a claim under more than one Benefit
Category as a result of a single cause or event, you will be
paid under the Benefit Category which provides the highest
payout (see Example 2).
Once a 100% percentage payout level claim is admitted,
your sum assured will reduce to zero and your Living
Lifestyle benefit expires.
How long will I be covered for?
You are covered for the duration of the cover period that
you have selected (i.e. to Policy Anniversary immediately
preceding the Life Assured’s 65th birthday or Whole of Life).
Is there a survival period before I can claim after
suffering a critical illness?
There is no survival period for the accelerated Living
Lifestyle benefit.

Mr Jones has purchased a Lifestyle Protector policy
with Life Cover with a sum assured of R1 000 000 and
Living Lifestyle with a sum assured of R1 000 000.
Mr Jones is involved in a motor vehicle accident
in which he loses a hand and also suffers kidney
damage requiring a kidney transplant. Under his
Living Lifestyle benefit, losing his hand qualifies for a
payment of R700 000 (i.e. 50% of the sum assured)
while he qualifies for R1 000 000 (i.e. 100% of the
sum assured) for the kidney transplant. As his claims
arose from the same event, Mr Jones would receive
the higher payment of R1 000 000 (i.e. he will be paid
for the kidney failure and no claim will be admitted for
the loss of the hand). Both his Life Cover and Living
Lifestyle would cease and no subsequent claims would
be paid.

What is Medical Advancements Protection and how
does it work?
The Living Lifestyle benefit covers specific medical
conditions using specific claim definitions. These definitions
help to determine the level of severity of the conditions, as
to whether a payout should be made and how much will be
paid out. To ensure that the definitions are objective and
transparent, they are often based on a specified medical
classification system, medical diagnostic test, medical
surgery or procedure.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

While these are appropriate at the time the policy is bought,
there is a risk that medical advancements might result in the
criteria becoming outdated over time. To protect you from
this, we include the Medical Advancements Protection
(MAP) feature.
MAP allows future claims to be considered for payment
where a medical classification system, diagnostic test or
investigation or medical surgery or procedure relating to
a claim definition becomes outdated and is no longer a
standard of clinical care due to medical advancements.

What are the requirements to claim through MAP?

The specific claim definition is eligible for MAP coverage (as shown in the Living Lifestyle Series Benefit Payout Summary section).
The old classification system, diagnostic test or investigation, surgery or procedure has been
replaced by the new classification system, diagnostic test or investigation, surgery or procedure.
The diagnosis, severity level and clinical manifestation is the same as would have been required.
The new diagnostic or investigative parameters must reach a level that implies a severity level
that is at least equivalent to or worse than implied by the original diagnostic or investigative test.
The new surgery/procedure is not experimental and is medically necessary and medically
equivalent or superior to the original surgery or procedure.
A minimum lifestyle impact still occurs – at least 1 of the following must hold:
• The new surgery or procedure must have the same or a higher level of invasiveness
• An average person would be booked off work for at least 3 weeks to undergo and recover
from the new surgery or procedure
• Similar residual impact on physical or mental abilities as the original surgery or procedure
would have caused.

How does Living Lifestyle compare to the standardised
critical illness (SCIDEP) definitions?
The Living Lifestyle benefits comply with the critical
illness definitions recommended by the Association for
Savings and Investment SA (ASISA) applicable at the date
of commencement of the benefit. This ensures that the
Living Lifestyle benefit criteria are objective and consistent
with the minimum industry standards adopted. Not all

www.asisa.org.za)

at that time the Rai medical classification system
is considered obsolete and is no longer a standard
medical classification system. Provided the claim meets
the requirements, MAP allows Simon’s claim to be
considered on the new classification system.

This is an option that can be selected with Living Lifestyle Plus on policies that do not have term benefits
and is automatically included on policies that have term benefits. 100% of your sum assured will be paid

Extended Option

This is an option that can be selected with Living Lifestyle Plus on policies that do not have term benefits

occurring.
The Extended option has been upgraded for new business and introduced as an opt-in upgrade for
existing policies with Extended option. This covers early cancer cover and covers mild illness with a payout
increased to 10% of the Living Lifestyle sum assured amount.

Critical Illness Booster
Option

This is an option that can be selected with Living Lifestyle on policies that do not have term benefits and
is automatically included on policies that have term benefits. If this option is selected, it pays an additional
25%, 50%, 75% or 100% for select events.

Cancer Claims Booster
Option

This is an option that can be selected with Living Lifestyle on policies that do not have term benefits and
is automatically included on policies that have term benefits. If this option is selected, it pays an additional
25%, on diagnosis of a late-stage cancer or when it progresses or recurs after remission.

Medical Advancements
Protection

The Medical Advancements Protection (MAP) feature is automatically included and provides enhanced
protection against the risk that specified claim definitions linked to medical classification systems, medical
diagnostic tests and investigations or medical procedures or surgeries become outdated due to medical
advancements.

Benefit Type

This is a core risk benefit and is a non-accelerated benefit.

BENEFITS | LIFESTYLE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

Survival Period

There is no explicit survival period for the Living Lifestyle Plus benefit. The following will apply when
assessing the claim:
• The condition must have been diagnosed prior to death, with objective medical evidence that the Life
Assured has met all the required criteria defined within the claim definition in the policy document.
• No claim will be payable under the Living Lifestyle Plus Benefit if the Life Assured dies before Liberty
can establish that the condition qualifies under any benefit category or the degree of severity has not
been established.
• No BI-1663 (notice of death) or equivalent, autopsy or any other death related reports will be used to
assess the claim under the Living Lifestyle Plus benefit.
• Where the definition under the benefit category requires some time to elapse for the assessment of the
claim, no claim will be assessed if these timelines have not yet elapsed/passed as at the date of death.

Eligibility

Specific exclusions may apply to certain Benefit Categories. Please refer to your policy document for a
complete list of exclusions.

What is the Living Lifestyle Plus benefit?
Living Lifestyle Plus pays a lump sum for a claim arising
from any critical illness that is covered under the Benefit
Categories.
How do the Top-Up and Extended options work?
The Top-Up and/or Extended options can be selected with
Living Lifestyle Plus. The Top-Up benefit provides a 100%
payout of the sum assured for certain conditions if the
severity of the condition is such that less than 100% of the
sum assured would be paid under Living Lifestyle Plus.
(Note that not all conditions paying less than 100% of the
Living Lifestyle Plus sum assured are increased to 100%
by the Top-Up option.) If the Extended benefit is selected,
between 5% and 10% of the Living Lifestyle Plus sum assured
is paid in the event of certain specified claim events occurring.
The Living Lifestyle Series – Benefit Payout Summary section
illustrates the benefits payable for different severities
depending on the combination of Living Lifestyle Plus, the
Top-Up option and the Extended option selected.
Top-Up and Extended are automatically included on term
policies with Living Lifestyle Plus benefits.
What are the Benefit Categories and claim percentages
at different severity levels?
Detailed information is included in the Living Lifestyle Series –
Benefit Payout Summary section. However, you should always
refer to your policy document to see which Benefit Categories
and claim percentages are applicable to your policy.
What type and level of cover does the Living Lifestyle
Plus benefit provide?
Your level of cover (i.e. your sum assured) will increase every
year, provided you have selected an annual automatic
benefit increase.
Living Lifestyle Plus has 30 Benefit Categories. Every
claim you submit is assessed based on the severity of your
condition using specified payment criteria. Any claims which
are paid will not reduce your Life Cover or Renewable Life
Cover(2) benefits.
Once you have had a claim admitted, you may only qualify
for another claim in the following circumstances:

Your condition worsens or you suffer a related claim and
now qualify for a higher percentage payout level.
The benefit that you will receive will take into account
the proportion of the benefit already paid. A maximum
of 100% of your sum assured will be paid for related
conditions.
2. You suffer a new condition which is totally unrelated to
the previous claims which have already been admitted.
Claims arising under the Trauma Benefit Category which
do not arise from the same single cause or event will
automatically be considered unrelated for the purpose
of this benefit.
If you qualify for a claim under more than one Benefit
Category as a result of a single cause or event, you will be
paid under the Benefit Category which provides the highest
payout.
1.

EXAMPLE

Mr Jones has purchased a Lifestyle Protector with
Living Lifestyle Plus with a sum assured of
R1 000 000.
Mr Jones is diagnosed with Stage 1 Liver Cancer. Under
his Living Lifestyle Plus benefit he qualifies for 25% of
his sum assured of R1 000 000. He receives a payment
of R250 000.
A year later Mr Jones’s condition has worsened and he
now has Stage II Liver Cancer. He now qualifies for 50%
of his sum assured. As he has already been paid 25%
under this Benefit Category he is now paid a further
25% of the sum assured. He receives a payment of
R250 000 (i.e. 25% of R1 000 000).
Top-up option scenario
If Mr Jones had the Living Lifestyle with sum assured as
above, but also selected the Top-Up option, he would
have qualified for a payment of 100% of his sum assured
(i.e. R1 000 000) when diagnosed with Stage I Liver
Cancer. After the payment of R1 000 000 cover for any
conditions related to the Liver Cancer would cease.
Two years later Mr Jones has full thickness burns
involving more than 30% of the body surface area. The
burns were not caused by or connected to the Liver
Cancer. He now receives 100% of the sum assured (i.e.
R1 000 000) because this is an unrelated condition.

BENEFITS | LIFESTYLE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

How long will I be covered for?
You are covered for the duration of the cover period that
you have selected (i.e. to Policy Anniversary immediately
preceding the Life Assured’s 65th birthday or Whole of Life).
Is there a survival period before I can claim after
suffering a critical illness?
There is no explicit survival period for the Living Lifestyle
Plus benefit. The diagnosis of the claim condition must
have occurred prior to death. There is a need for objective
medical evidence that the Life Assured meets all the
required criteria for a valid claim pay-out. The validity of a
claim is dependent on whether it can be established, at time
of claim assessment, that the condition does qualify under
any one of the listed benefit categories, or that it meets the
required degree of severity within a benefit category. The
substantiating medical evidence does not include a BI-1663
(notice of death) or equivalent, autopsy or any other death
related reports. The accepted medical evidence are results
confirming the diagnosis of the condition prior to death.
Some of the listed benefit categories require timelines that
need to be met before a claim can be assessed. These timelines will need to elapse before an assessment can be initiated,
and a claim will not be considered if these are not met.
(1)
(2)

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What is Medical Advancements Protection and what
does it cover?
The Living Lifestyle Plus benefit covers specific medical
conditions using specific claim definitions. These definitions
help to determine the level of severity of the conditions, as
to whether a payout should be made and how much will be
paid out. To ensure that the definitions are objective and
transparent, they are often based on a specified medical
classification system, medical diagnostic test, medical
surgery or procedure.
While these are appropriate at the time the policy is bought,
there is a risk that medical advancements might result in the
criteria becoming outdated over time. To protect you from
this, we include the Medical Advancements Protection
(MAP) feature.
MAP allows future claims to be considered for payment
where a medical classification system, diagnostic test or
investigation or medical surgery or procedure relating to
a claim definition becomes outdated and is no longer a
standard of clinical care due to medical advancements.

The Renewable Premium Pattern is closed to New Business from July 2022.
The Renewable Life Cover option is closed to New Business from July 2022.

What are the requirements to claim through MAP?

MEDICAL SURGERIES
AND PROCEDURES

REQUIREMENT
(Must be met in the opinion of Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer)

MEDICAL
DIAGNOSTIC TESTS
AND INVESTIGATIONS

For a claim to be considered through MAP it must meet a few requirements:

MEDICAL
CLASSIFCATION
SYSTEM

The specific claim definition is eligible for MAP coverage (as shown in the Living Lifestyle Series Benefit Payout Summary section).
The old classification system, diagnostic test or investigation, surgery or procedure has been
replaced by the new classification system, diagnostic test or investigation, surgery or procedure.
The diagnosis, severity level and clinical manifestation is the same as would have been required.
The new diagnostic or investigative parameters must reach a level that implies a severity level
that is at least equivalent to or worse than implied by the original diagnostic or investigative test.
The new surgery/procedure is not experimental and is medically necessary and medically
equivalent or superior to the original surgery or procedure.
A minimum lifestyle impact still occurs – at least 1 of the following must hold:
• The new surgery or procedure must have the same or a higher level of invasiveness
• An average person would be booked off work for at least 3 weeks to undergo and recover
from the new surgery or procedure
• Similar residual impact on physical or mental abilities as the original surgery or procedure
would have caused.

What is not covered through MAP?

•

In addition to claims which do not meet the stated
requirements, the following will not be covered:
•

New medical or surgical procedures, which are
considered, in the opinion of Liberty’s Chief Medical
Officer, preventative or prophylactic.

•

An underlying condition which has been identified
as a result of screening tests (for example isolated
genetic screening tests) but where there is no clinical
manifestation of the illness or condition.
A medical procedure or surgery which is already
covered, or an equivalent procedure or surgery is
already covered, at a lower severity level.

BENEFITS | LIFESTYLE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

How does Living Lifestyle compare to the
standardised critical illness (SCIDEP) definitions?
The Living Lifestyle benefits comply with the critical
illness definitions recommended by the Association for
Savings and Investment SA (ASISA) applicable at the date
of commencement of the benefit. This ensures that the
Living Lifestyle benefit criteria are objective and consistent
with the minimum industry standards adopted. Not all
heart attacks, CABG, Stroke and Cancers are covered by
SCIDEP, only specified severities, therefore this table is not
prescriptive and does not constitute the claims criteria in
isolation. All claims will be assessed according to the detailed
criteria contained in this document as well as the detailed
criteria within SCIDEP. (This is available on the ASISA website,
www.asisa.org.za)

The Living Lifestyle Plus benefits comply with the critical
illness definitions recommended by the Association for
Savings and Investment SA (ASISA) applicable at the date of
commencement of the benefit. This ensures that the Living
Lifestyle Plus benefit criteria are objective and consistent
with the minimum industry standards adopted. Not all
heart attacks, CABG, Stroke and Cancers are covered by
SCIDEP, only specified severities, therefore this table is not
prescriptive and does not constitute the claims criteria in
isolation. All claims will be assessed according to the detailed
criteria contained in this document as well as the detailed
criteria within SCIDEP. (This is available on the ASISA website,
www.asisa.org.za)

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

1. CANCER AND LEUKAEMIA
Cancer is defined as the presence of a malignant tumour rated according to the appropriate staging criteria characterised by the
uncontrolled growth and spread of malignant cells with the invasion of normal tissue. Unequivocal histological evidence and, where
appropriate, clinical evidence of invasive malignancy must be provided. The classifications are based on the latest edition of the AJCC
Cancer Staging Manual.
Unless otherwise stated, Liberty will only pay for stage I to stage IV cancers.
The following conditions are excluded from this definition:
•
•
•
•
•
•

All conditions histologically described as benign, pre-malignant, low malignant potential or cancer-in-situ including CIN 1, CIN 2 and
CIN 3 of the cervix (unless specifically defined in benefits
Any tumour classified as carcinoma in-situ (Tis) or (Ta) by the latest edition of the AJCC Cancer Staging Manual
All non-melanoma skin cancers are specifically excluded unless specified below.
All myelodysplastic syndromes and myeloproliferative neoplasms including but not limited to, essential thrombocythemia, primary
myelofibrosis, polycythemia vera unless specified in this specific benefit category below
Primary cutaneous lymphoma and dermatofibrosarcoma which are confined to the skin and which have not spread to the lymph
nodes or distant sites
All cancers only identified from tumour cells, pieces of DNA, or any other biomarkers, any of which may be present in the blood,
saliva, urine, or other bodily fluids, including, but not limited to, tests known as “liquid biopsies”.

Grade I brain tumours are not assessed under this category and may be assessed, where applicable, under benefit category 9.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

3. CARDIAC AND CARDIOVASCULAR
All cardiac events that occur within 30 days of each other will be classified as a single event.
The conditions covered under this Benefit Category are:
• Valvular and septal diseases of the heart
• Peripheral arterial disease
• Coronary artery disease
• Carotid arterial disease
• Myocardial infarction
• Aneurysm
• Cardiomyopathy
• Cardio-pulmonary dysfunction
• Pericardial disease
• Heart transplant
• Arrhythmias
The diagnosis under each of the conditions is to be confirmed by a cardiologist using the appropriate special investigations.
Within the cardiovascular section reference is made to the New York Heart Association (NYHA) classification and the Metabolic
Equivalents of Task (METS) and Ejection Fraction (EF) measurement systems.

3.3 Myocardial infarction
Myocardial infarction must be due to obstructive coronary heart disease and is defined as the death of heart muscle, due to inadequate
blood supply, as evidenced by the benefit descriptions in the table below.
The evidence must show a definite acute myocardial infarction due to obstructive coronary heart disease and be diagnosed by an
appropriate registered medical specialist.
Other acute coronary syndromes not covered by this benefit category, including but not limited to:
• Any form of angina without myocardial cell death
• Coronary artery spasms
• Elevations of troponin in the absence of objective ischemic heart diseases (including but not limited to myocarditis, apical ballooning,
cardiac contusion etc.)
Myocardial infarction evidenced by all of the following
criteria (1), (2) and (3) indicated below:
1. Compatible clinical symptoms; and
2. Characteristic ECG changes either (a) or (b) below
indicative of myocardial ischaemia or myocardial
infarction:
a. Patients with ST-segment elevation
− New or presumed new ST segment elevation at
the J point in two or more contiguous leads with
the cut-off points greater than or equal to 0.2mV
in leads V1, V2, or V3, and greater than or equal to
0.1mV in other leads.
− Contiguity in the frontal plane is defined by the
lead sequence AVL, I and II, AVF, III.
b. Patients without ST-segment elevation
− ST-segment depression of at least 0.1mV;
− T-wave abnormalities only; and
3. Raised cardiac biomarkers defined as any one of the
following Troponin Markers:
Trop T or Trop I as per Biomarkers Table (i) in the
Assessment Tables section below.

Cardiomyopathy must fulfil ALL the criteria below:
•
diagnosis must be confirmed by a cardiologist, and
•
heart muscle is abnormally enlarged, thickened and/or stiffened, and
•
requires ongoing treatment in line with best practices for Cardiomyopathy, and
•
the resulting cardiac impairment must be measured over a period of at least three months and must be irreversible and permanent,
and
the diagnosis is not related to the use of alcohol or the use of non-therapeutic drugs or the use of medication not as prescribed
•

4. CEREBROVASCULAR INCIDENT (STROKE)
A stroke refers to the death of brain tissue due to inadequate blood supply or haemorrhage within the brain resulting in neurological
deficit, confirmed by a neuro-imaging investigation and appropriate clinical findings by a specialist neurologist. Symptoms and signs as
well as imaging must confirm a new stroke. The severity of any permanent neurological impairment is to be assessed after 3 months.
Diagnostic investigations must show a recent infarction or bleed.

5. MULTIPLE SCLEROSIS
Multiple sclerosis is defined as a chronic progressive nervous disorder involving loss of myelin sheath around certain nerve fibres.
The diagnosis of multiple sclerosis must have been confirmed by a neurologist using objective radiological evidence such as MRI.
Two separate clinical events must have occurred resulting in permanent neurological sequelae. These sequelae must have involved a
mini- mum of two major neurological areas – i.e. the optic nerves, motor, sensory or cerebellar.

7. PARKINSON’S DISEASE
Parkinson’s disease is a progressive, degenerative disease of the central nervous system of unknown aetiology. The unequivocal diagnosis
of Parkinson’s disease must be confirmed by a neurologist. The impairment must be persistent despite optimal treatment.
Similar symptoms due to other causes are not covered.

8. MYASTHENIA GRAVIS
Myasthenia gravis is a chronic progressive disease characterised by chronic fatigue and muscular weakness (especially in the face and
neck). It is caused by a deficiency of acetylcholine at the neuromuscular junctions. The unequivocal diagnosis of myasthenia gravis must
be confirmed by a neurologist. The impairment must be persistent despite optimal treatment.
Similar symptoms due to other causes are not covered.

11. COMA
A coma is a state of unconsciousness with a Glasgow Coma Scale score of 10 or less with no reaction to external stimuli and requiring
external life support. The person is comatose because of the progression of a medical condition.
The term maximum medical improvement used in the definitions means the condition has stabilised over a period of time sufficient
to allow optimal tissue repair and is unlikely to change despite further medical or surgical treatment.

15. CHRONIC LIVER DISORDERS
Chronic and irreversible liver failure, that is not alcohol or substance abuse related, as confirmed by clinical, laboratory and histological
evidence. The diagnosis is to be confirmed by a gastroenterologist.
(Child-Pugh refers to a medical grading system, which covers conditions such as: primary biliary cirrhosis, sclerosing cholangitis and
cirrhosis of the liver amongst others).

16. ENDOCRINE CONDITIONS
The endocrine system is a system of glands, each of which secretes a type of hormone directly into the bloodstream to regulate the
body functions. Any disease of the endocrine system must be confirmed by an endocrinologist with appropriate laboratory markers or
radiological evidence.

17. BLOOD DISORDERS
This benefit category covers the following blood disorders: Polycythaemia Vera, Essential Thrombocythemia, Primary Myelofibrosis,
Myelodysplastic Syndrome and Aplastic Anaemia
The diagnosis of a blood disorder is to be confirmed by a haematologist. The diagnosis must be confirmed using current internationally
recognized diagnostic criteria, and supported by appropriate radiological, histological and laboratory investigations.
For the purpose of this benefit, major organs are brain, lungs, kidney, liver and heart.

20. MUSCULAR DYSTROPHY
Muscular dystrophies are a group of genetic diseases characterised by progressive weakness and degeneration of the skeletal muscles
which control movement. The diagnosis of muscular dystrophy must be confirmed by an appropriate registered specialist.

22. CONNECTIVE TISSUE DISEASES
The benefit covers the following connective tissue disorders: Dermatomyositis and polymyositis, Polyarteritis nodosa, Wegener’s
granulomatosis, Giant cell arteritis, Sarcoidosis, Rheumatoid arthritis, Progressive systemic scleroderma, Systemic Lupus Erythematosus.
The unequivocal diagnosis of a connective tissue disorder will only be accepted from a specialist rheumatologist, registered with the
HPCSA, who has been treating the claimant for at least 6 months. The diagnosis must be confirmed using current internationally
recognized diagnostic criteria, and supported by appropriate radiological, histological and laboratory investigations.
Large joints for the purpose of this benefit category are defined as the knee; hip; elbow; shoulder; or ankle. The number of joint surfaces
involved in that large joint are not considered individually as a single joint.
Where indicated, ‘unresponsive to treatment’ is defined as simultaneous clinical synovitis (swelling) of more than 3 large joints and an
abnormal CRP (C-reactive protein) or abnormal ESR (erythrocyte sedimentation rate) despite compliance with continuous, ongoing
clinically accepted medical treatment for more than 6 months.
For the purpose of this benefit, the evidence of impairment of major organ systems (the neurological, respiratory/lungs, hepatic, cardiac,
gastrointestinal or renal function/kidneys) must be confirmed with at least two of the following:
i) Objective imaging appropriate for the deficit/disease process;
ii) biopsy findings;
iii) laboratory tests;
iv) clinical functional investigations
Notes:
• All connective tissue disorders will be deemed related, therefore a maximum of 100% will be paid for this benefit category.
• None of the below claim definitions will be paid twice.
• Only the connective tissue disorders mentioned in this benefit category will be considered under the Connective Tissue disorder
benefit category.
• All organ systems mentioned within each definition below will be considered related to this benefit category. No claim will be paid
under any other benefit category where a claim has been paid for under any organ system for Connective Tissue Disorders and vice
versa.

23. EAR, NOSE AND THROAT DISORDERS
Hearing loss must be total and irreversible (tested with hearing aids when applicable, or post-surgical or medical intervention) and
demonstrated in 2 measurements at least 6 months apart. Measurements will be performed over the range 500, 1000, 2000, and
3000 Hz, and must be confirmed by an appropriate registered specialist.

26. TRAUMATIC INJURIES INCLUDING TRAUMATIC BRAIN INJURIES, HEAD AND NECK INJURIES,
SPINAL INJURIES AND COMA
Traumatic injury that is caused by an external physical force, for example a fall or motor vehicle accident. The injury or condition and
treatment thereof must be confirmed by an appropriate registered specialist within 24 months of the traumatic event and must be
directly attributable to the traumatic event.
A coma is a state of unconsciousness with a Glasgow Coma Scale score of 10 or less with no reaction to external stimuli and requiring
external life support. Diagnosis is to be confirmed by an appropriate registered specialist with radiological evidence or surgical reports as
appropriate.
The term maximum medical improvement used in the definitions means the condition has stabilised over a period of time sufficient to
allow optimal tissue repair and is unlikely to change despite further medical or surgical treatment.

BENEFITS | LIFESTYLE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

27. LOSS OF AND LOSS OF USE OF LIMBS
All impairment/s must be permanent, irreversible and at Maximal Medical Improvement (MMI).
The term Maximum Medical Improvement (MMI) used in the definitions means the condition has stabilised over a period of time sufficient
to allow optimal tissue repair and is unlikely to change despite further medical or surgical treatment.
“Loss of use” refers to the total and complete motor and sensory nerve loss. Partial “Loss of use” is not covered. “Loss of” refers to
amputation.
Digits includes thumb/s.
Fingers do not include thumb/s.
Whole hand is defined as the part of the arm beyond the wrist including the whole palm and all digits.
Whole foot is defined as the part of the leg beyond the ankle joint.
Limb refers to the whole upper extremity beyond the shoulder or the whole lower extremity beyond the hip.
MCP refers to the Metacarpophalangeal joint/s.
Complete sensory nerve deficit means no sensation including pain, from any nerve supply.
Complete motor nerve deficit means no motor function remaining.

29. ACCIDENTAL HIV
The contraction of HIV due to:
• Accidental needlestick injury. A payment under this benefit category
will be payable in the event of the Life Assured, registered with the
HPCSA or South African Nursing Council, becoming accidentally
infected by any Human Immunodeficiency Virus contracted during
the execution of his/her medical or nursing duties

• Rape or indecent assault or violent crime. The offence must
have been reported to the South African Police Service
(SAPS) and a criminal case opened. An HIV test must have
been performed within 24 hours of the assault to confirm
an HIV negative status at the time of the assault a medical
examination must have been performed within 24 hours
after the incident. There must be proof that the patient has
been started on a course of anti-retroviral drugs known
as post-exposure prophylaxis (PEP) within 72 hours of the
event.
• Receiving an organ transplant where the transplanted organ
was previously infected with the HIV virus. There must be
proof of HIV negative status within 3 months before the
transplant.
• Transfusion of infected blood or blood products from
a transfusion service recognised by Liberty, after
commencement of the policy.
The transfusion must be identified as the cause of the HIV
infection.

Payment due accidental needlestick injury is subject to the following
conditions:
–	The accident must be reported to the medical facility within 24
hours. A Needle-stick cover claim form must be completed and
submitted to Liberty Group Ltd together with an incident report
within a month to confirm an HIV negative status at the time of
the needle stick injury.
–	Should the Life Assured test positive, the claim will not be paid
and this benefit will expire.
–	If the result of the first test was negative, two further HIV tests
will be done in 3 month intervals; should any of these test results
prove positive the Sum Assured will be paid. Should all these tests
be negative, the policy will continue as if this accident did not
occur.
Written reports from a registered medical laboratory, confirming the identity of the patient and that the tests for assessment under this
category were done on this patient, as well as the result thereof, must be provided to the Liberty Group Ltd. There must be further proof that
the patient has been started on and continued for the prescribed period on a course of anti-retroviral drugs (PEP). Only registered medical
laboratory HIV test results will be accepted by Liberty.

CANCER CLAIM BOOSTER

In order for Liberty to consider a claim under the Cancer Claim Booster, it is essential that the condition diagnosed fully
complies with the applicable definition set out in the Qualifying cancer criteria table below. The claim amount will be equal
to 25% of the Living Lifestyle Plus Sum assured at the time of the claim.
QUALIFYING CANCER CRITERIA:

The Life Assured must have been diagnosed with cancer meeting the following criteria.
• Hodgkins or Non-Hodgkins lymphoma (stage III or IV on Ann Arbor classification system)
• Acute myeloblastic leukaemia
• Chronic lymphocytic leukaemia (stage III or IV on the Very High Risk CLL-IPI, Binet C or Rai classification system)
• Chronic myeloid leukaemia (having undergone bone marrow transplant)
• Acute lymphoblastic leukaemia
• Multiple myeloma (stage III on the Durie-Salmon Scale)
• Brain tumour (grade 3 or 4 on WHO classification)
• Basal cell skin carcinoma (stage IV)
• Squamous cell skin carcinoma (stage IV)
• Prostate cancer - T4N0M0 (any grade) or prostate cancer with lymph node involvement or metastases (At least N1 in TNM
classification system)
• Any other stage III or IV cancer, unless included in any of the criteria set out in any of the rows above
ADDITIONALLY ONE OF THE FOLLOWING OCCURS:
• The cancer has progressed despite completion of all lines of available treatment as per gold standard treatment protocols. Progression,
for the purpose of this benefit, is defined an increased tumour burden; or
• There is a recurrence of the cancer, previously meeting the criterion above, after a period of remission. Remission, for the purpose of
this benefit, is defined as a minimum period of 12 months that the claimant has been cancer or tumour free subsequent to treatment
completion; or
• Life expectancy is reduced to less than twelve (12) months.
The progression, recurrence or life expectancy must be confirmed by the treating specialist and supported by appropriate radiological,
histological and laboratory investigations and satisfy Liberty’s Chief Medical Officer.

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

FEMALE LIVING LIFESTYLE
Benefit Description

The Female Living Lifestyle is available to females only and covers lifestyle changing health events
specific to females due to serious pregnancy complications and/or congenital birth defects.
The Female Living Lifestyle benefit provides you with financial protection by way of a lump sum
payout. The pregnancy complications and congenital birth defects covered are provided below.

Benefit Type

This is a non-accelerated benefit.

Cover Period

This benefit ceases on the policy anniversary prior to your 40th birthday.

Benefit Limits

Minimum: R50 000
Maximum: R1.5 million
Level
Premium
Pattern

Entry Ages

Renewable
Premium
Pattern(1)

Age Rated
Premium
Pattern

Fixed
Increasing
Premium
Pattern

Minimum next
birthday

Maximum next
birthday

Rating Factor

• Age

Premium Payment Period

Corresponds to the cover period. The premium ceases when the benefit ceases.

Waiting Period

The waiting period is 9 months.

Survival Period

14 days from the date of diagnosis of the condition or conditions concerned where these apply to the
adult life. The survival period is 7 days for the child under the congenital birth defects benefit.

Specific Exclusions

Female Living Lifestyle specifically excludes claims where the condition for the claim arose directly or
indirectly as a result of the consumption of alcohol, poisons, drugs or non-prescribed medication. Claims
are also excluded if they result from an injury which is self-inflicted or an attempted suicide. Claims should
be submitted within 3 years of the birth of your child for conditions relating to congenital abnormalities.
Please refer to your policy document for more details on exclusions.

The Renewable Premium Pattern is closed to New Business from July 2022.

(1)

What is the Female Living Lifestyle benefit?
Liberty has specially tailored the Female Living Lifestyle benefit to help you cope with financial needs related to lifestylechanging health events as a result of serious pregnancy complications or if your baby is born with major congenital birth
defects. The benefit categories are described below.

BENEFITS | LIFESTYLE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

When can I add the Female Living Lifestyle benefit to
my policy?

After I claim, is there a survival period before I
canreceive my benefit?

The Female Living Lifestyle benefit can be taken at any
time and does not have to be taken at the inception of the
Living Lifestyle/Plus benefit or on the addition of a female
life to an existing policy as a result of marriage. However, the
Female Living Lifestyle can only be selected if you have a
Living Lifestyle benefit or Living Lifestyle Plus benefit.

You will have to survive at least 14 days from the date of
diagnosis of the condition for which you are claiming. In
the event of your death within the 14 days from date of
diagnosis, the benefit will cease and no benefits are payable.

What level of cover does the Female Living Lifestyle
benefit provide?
When you submit a claim, you will be paid a specified
proportion of your sum assured. The proportion payable
depends on the condition for which you have claimed.
Multiple claims under different Benefit Categories may be
made. However, in total not more than 100% of the sum
assured will be paid.
Is there a waiting period before I can claim?
There is a waiting period of 9 months from policy inception
before a claim will be paid, as this benefit covers mainly
pregnancy-related complications and congenital birth
defects.
Any claim event that takes place within 9 months of the
benefit inception will not constitute a valid claim.

A child born with any congenital birth defect will have to
survive at least 7 days from the date of birth before any claim
can be made. The 14-day survival period for the mother does
not apply to these conditions.
EXAMPLE

Mrs Smith purchases Lifestyle Protector with Living
Lifestyle. She also selects Female Living Lifestyle
with a sum assured of R200 000.
Mrs Smith has disseminated intravascular coagulation
10 months after the start of the policy. Mrs Smith
receives R100 000 as the disseminated intravascular
coagulation Benefit Category pays 50% of the sum
assured (R200 000).
A year later, Mrs Smith has a baby. Unfortunately, the
baby dies as a result of an unexplained cot death. She
is paid R10 000 for the Sudden infant death syndrome
Benefit Category.
Two years later, Mrs Smith has a hydatidiform mole
and requires a hysterectomy. The Hydatidiform
Mole Benefit Category pays 50% of her original sum
assured (R200 000). However, Mrs Smith has already
been paid R116 000 (i.e. R 100 000 for disseminated
intravascular coagulation + R10 000 for the cot death).
Therefore, Mrs Smith is paid R90 000.
Once the third claim has been paid, her Female Living
Lifestyle benefit ceases.

BENEFITS | LIFESTYLE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

CHILD LIVING LIFESTYLE
Benefit Description

Child Living Lifestyle has been designed specifically for children. This benefit will pay a lump sum to you
should your child be diagnosed with a child critical illness listed under the Benefit Categories covered.

Benefit Type

This benefit is a non-accelerated benefit.

Cover Period

The benefit for each child will cease on the earlier of the following events:
• The policy anniversary prior to that child’s 24th birthday
• On admission of any claim for that child
• The death of that child

Benefit Limits

Minimum: R50 000
Maximum: R700 000

Entry Ages

Minimum entry age: 3 next birthday
Maximum entry age: 15 next birthday

Rating Factors

No rating factors will apply.

Premium Payment Period

Corresponds to the cover period. The premium ceases when the benefit ceases.

Survival Period

The survival period is 30 days from the date of diagnosis of the condition or conditions concerned.

Specific Exclusions

Conditions for which your child either sought or should have sought treatment, or where a condition was
diagnosed or the symptoms displayed which could have led to the diagnosis of a condition, prior to the
commencement or subsequent reinstatement of this benefit.
Please refer to your policy document for more details on exclusions.

Next Gen Cover

When the Child Life Assured turns 18, Liberty will accept an application on their life, for Life Cover (limited
to R1 200 000), critical illness, impairment and/or lump sum disability benefits, without any health
underwriting. However, Liberty reserves the right to call for HIV and smoker status. The offer is subject
to the benefit limits and rules at the time of exercising the option. Normal financial, occupational and
territorial underwriting will still apply.
No claim for any pre-existing diagnosed medical condition, health impairment or a change in health
discovered prior to the inception of this new policy will be considered or payable and will also include
conditions where pre-existing symptoms and/or signs of a health change were present, but a diagnosis
was not made, whether medically investigated, or treated, or not.
To qualify for these benefits, no claim may have been paid for the Child Living Lifestyle benefit and the
Child Life Assured will have to apply for Life Cover within three months of the Child Living Lifestyle benefit
ending. The Child Living Lifestyle benefit would have to be removed from the policy once the new policy
is taken out.
Premiums for these benefits will be calculated based on Liberty’s business practices at the time.

What is the Child Living Lifestyle benefit?
Child Living Lifestyle is designed specifically for your
children. This benefit provides you with some financial
assistance towards covering your medical or other costs
if your child is diagnosed with a condition listed under the
Benefit Categories. Cover for each child must be obtained
separately, but the same sum assured will apply to all
children covered under this benefit.

This benefit is available for selection for each child, provided
that either you or your Second Life Assured has selected
Living Lifestyle or Living Lifestyle Plus. The benefit can
be added at a later date and need not be selected at the
inception of your policy.

BENEFITS | LIFESTYLE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

What are the Benefit Categories and claim percentages applicable?

CHILD LIVING LIFESTYLE BENEFIT CATEGORIES

PROPORTION OF CHILD LIVING
LIFESTYLE SUM ASSURED PAYABLE

1. Benign Brain Tumour
A non-cancerous brain tumour that is life threatening causing raised intracranial
pressure. The presence must be confirmed by Computerised Tomography (CT)
or Magnetic Resonance Imaging (MRI). The tumour must result in a permanent
neurological deficit causing a WPI of greater than 15%.

100%

2. Cancer
A malignant tumour supported by a histological diagnosis showing the spread
of malignant cells, with invasion and destruction of normal tissue for which
interventionist treatment or surgery is necessary.
Specific exclusions:
• All skin cancers including malignant melanomas of less than 1.5mm maximum
thickness using the Breslow method, unless there is evidence of metastases
• Stage 1 Hodgkins Disease
• All conditions histologically described as pre-malignant or cancer in-situ including
Cervical intraepithelial neoplasia CIN 1, CIN 2 and CIN 3 of the cervix
• Kaposi sarcoma and other AIDS-related tumours.

100%

3. Cerebra-vascular incident (CVI)
A CVI is death of brain tissue as shown on a CT or MRI scan with permanent
appropriate symptoms or signs relating to the lesion resulting in a permanent WPI of
greater than 15%.
Specific exclusions:
• Transient Ischaemic Attack
• Reversible neurological deficit
• Migraines
• Vascular disease affecting the eye or optic nerve or vestibular function.

100%

4. Chronic Liver Failure
Liver Disease with persistent jaundice, ascitis or encephalopathy resulting in a
permanent WPI of greater than 30%.
Specific exclusions:
Alcohol or drug abuse induced liver disease.

100%

5. Chronic Lung Failure
End-stage lung disease requiring extensive permanent oxygen therapy with lung
function testing compatible with a permanent WPI of greater than 30%.

100%

6. Renal Failure
Irreversible failure of both kidneys requiring regular haemo-dialysis treatment, or
renal transplantation.

100%

7. Meningococcal Meningitis
A confirmed bacteriological diagnosis of meningococcal meningitis causing
inflammation of the membranes of the spinal cord or brain resulting in a permanent
neurological deficit, with a permanent WPI of greater than 15%.

100%

Specific exclusions:
All other forms of meningitis, including viral meningitis.
8. Major Organ Transplant
Organ transplant as the recipient of a kidney, heart, lung, liver, pancreas or bone
marrow.

200%

BENEFITS | LIFESTYLE PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

PROPORTION OF CHILD LIVING
LIFESTYLE SUM ASSURED PAYABLE

CHILD LIVING LIFESTYLE BENEFIT CATEGORIES
9. Paralysis
Total, irreversible loss of the use of any two limbs as a result of injury or disease.

100%

10. Epilepsy
Epilepsy under specialist neurological supervision that is unresponsive to drug
treatment and requires the child to go to a special school and necessitates constant
supervision of the child, with a permanent WPI of greater than 15%.

100%

11. Blindness
Complete irreversible loss of sight in both eyes as a result of sickness or injury as
confirmed by a consultant ophthalmologist.
Specific exclusions:
Blindness due to alcohol or drug abuse.

100%

12. Diabetes
The diagnosis of insulin-dependent diabetes mellitus confirmed by a physician.

What level of cover does the Child Living Lifestyle
benefit provide?
A lump sum is paid on diagnosis of a condition affecting
your child. The amount paid depends on the sum assured
that is selected and to which Benefit Category the condition
belongs.
All Benefit Categories pay 100% of the sum assured (except
for the major organ transplant benefit category that pays
200% of the sum assured).
Once a claim has been made, you cannot take out another
Child Living Lifestyle benefit for that child. A claim for one
child will not affect the cover you have on your other children
or on your other benefits.
After I claim, is there a survival period before I can
receive the benefit?
Your child will have to survive 30 days from the date of
diagnosis of the condition. In the event of your child’s death
within the 30 days, the benefit will cease and no benefit will
be payable.
Next Gen Cover
When the Child Life Assured completes their education,
Liberty will accept an application on their life, for life cover,
critical illness, impairment and/or lump sum disability
benefits, with limited underwriting. The available cover
amounts for each benefit will be limited to R1 200 000
for lump sum benefits within the Life Protection, Income
Protection and Lifestyle Protection pillars. Liberty, still,
however, reserves the right to call for HIV and smoker status.
The offer, including the size of accessible cover, is subject
to the benefit limits and business rules at the time of the
exercising the option. Normal financial, occupational, and
territorial underwriting will still apply.

100%

A claim for any pre-existing diagnosed medical condition,
health impairment or a change in health discovered prior
to the inception of this new policy will not be considered or
payable and will also include conditions where pre-existing
symptoms and/or signs of a health change were present, but
a diagnosis was not made, whether medically investigated,
or treated, or not.
To qualify for these benefits, no claim may have been paid
for the Child Living Lifestyle benefit and the Child Life
Assured will have to apply for cover within three months of
the Child Living Lifestyle benefit ending. The Child Living

once the new policy is taken out.
Premiums for these benefits will be calculated based on

EXAMPLE

Mr Smith purchases Lifestyle Protector with Living
Lifestyle, and adds Child Living Lifestyle for both of
his children, Jason and Kate, with a sum assured of
R200 000.
Mr Smith makes a claim for Jason who has suffered
from renal failure and is paid R200 000. The Child
Living Lifestyle benefit for Jason will cease. This does
not affect the sum assured under Living Lifestyle or
Kate’s Child Living Lifestyle.

4.4 POLICY PROTECTION PILLAR
Future Protector
Benefit Description

Future Protector gives you the option to purchase Life Cover, Living Lifestyle or Living Lifestyle Plus
cover and/or lump sum Loss of Income Protection cover in future, without any underwriting other than
a negative HIV test. Additional Living Lifestyle cover with the Top-Up and/or Extended options or Living
Lifestyle Plus cover with the Top-Up and/or Extended options will only be available if you have already
selected the cover with the Top-Up and/or Extended options on the policy.

Benefit Type

Future Protector is a policy protection benefit that can only be added at policy inception.

Cover Period

Future Protector expires on the policy anniversary before your 65th birthday. However, the maximum
age at which options can be exercised depends on the benefit in question:
• Life Cover option: This option can be exercised until the policy anniversary directly preceding your
65th birthday.
• Lifestyle Protection option: This option can be exercised until the policy anniversary directly
preceding your 60th birthday.
• Loss of Income Protection option: This option can be exercised until the policy anniversary directly
preceding your 60th birthday.

Benefit Limits

Maximum nominated portfolio: R10 million
Minimum nominated portfolio: R700 000
Maximum amount of options that can be exercised:
1. Total across all benefits: Initial Nominated Portfolio
2. Aggregated per benefit
• Life Cover option: R6 million
• Lifestyle Protection option: R3 million
• Loss of Income Protection option: R3 million
The minimum amount of any option that can be exercised is R100 000.
The total amount of cover enjoyed after exercising the option may not exceed Liberty’s maximum level of
cover for that benefit at the time.
Option Amounts exercised in respect of Critical Illness benefits, will be subject to a maximum amount as
determined by financial underwriting. The current maximum levels are:
• Loss of Income Protection: R60 million (R9 million for Whole of Life cover period).
• Lifestyle Protection: R9 million.

What is the benefit of the Future Protector?
The Future Protector gives you the option to purchase
cover in future without further medical underwriting (other
than a negative HIV test) as follows:
•
•

•

Life Cover Option: The option allows you to enhance
your Life Cover and Renewable Life Cover(2).
Lifestyle Protection Option: The option allows you to
purchase additional Living Lifestyle or Living Lifestyle
Plus cover. Additional Living Lifestyle cover with the
Top-Up and/or Extended options or Living Lifestyle
Plus cover with the Top-Up and/or Extended options
will only be available if you have already selected the
cover with the Top-Up and/or Extended options on
the policy.
Loss of Income Protection Option: The option
allows you to purchase additional Capital Disability,
Capital Disability Plus, Impairment, Impairment
Plus, Absolute Protector, Absolute Protector Plus,
Absolute Impairment or Absolute Impairment
Plus cover.

A combination of Life Cover, Lifestyle Protection and Loss
of Income Protection cover may be purchased subject to
the maximum option amount.

Income Protection benefit, Liberty reserves the right to
refuse an application for the exercise of an Own occupation
disability option, and to offer you the Own or any reasonable
occupation disability option instead. Under the Lifestyle
Protection option, Option Amounts exercised in respect of
Critical Illness benefits, will be subject to a maximum amount
as determined by financial underwriting.
You may not exercise an option for a Lifestyle Protection
benefit or a Loss of Income Protection benefit if you
have ever claimed for this particular benefit. For example, if
you have claimed under your Living Lifestyle benefit you
will not be able to increase your cover under this benefit.
However, you are allowed to increase your cover under the
Life Cover and Loss of Income Protection benefits.
How can I purchase the additional cover?
The Future Protector is flexible. If you choose to exercise
your option, you can:
•
•
•

Future Protector provides protection against lower cover
in real terms in future due to inflation. The benefit also
provides you protection against not being able to obtain
cover in future due to you becoming uninsurable. Life Cover
becomes more difficult to obtain with age and failing health.

Example

In October 2003 Mr Jones purchases a Lifestyle
Protector with Life Cover and the Future Protector
benefit. In October 2004 the Future Protector
provides Mr Jones the option to purchase additional
cover of R700 000 without further underwriting
(other than the negative HIV test). Mr Jones elects to
add Living Lifestyle Plus (with R200 000 cover) and
Absolute Protector Plus (with R300 000 cover) to
his existing policy.

The Future Protector is also ideal for business assurance
purposes, as it allows your cover to grow alongside your
business.
Both you and your business associates are able to enjoy
a sense of financial security if the business appreciates in
value and a keyperson or partner in the partnership has
become uninsurable.

Alternatively Mr Jones could have increased his Life
Cover by R100 000 and purchased a new Lifestyle
Protector policy with Absolute Protector Plus (with
R400 000 cover) free of any underwriting (other than
the negative HIV test).

Are there any restrictions on what type of cover I can
select?
The application for the Future Protector will be fully
underwritten. You will not be able to exercise an option
under the Lifestyle Protection option or the Loss of
Income Protection option if you are declined at the

BENEFITS | POLICY PROTECTION PILLAR | OCTOBER 2025

Example

LIFESTYLE PROTECTOR TECHNICAL GUIDE

When does the benefit cease?
•

The option must be exercised at least once every three
years otherwise the option ceases.

Mr Jones purchases a new policy and selects Life
Cover and Living Lifestyle. In addition, Mr Jones
also purchases the Future Protector. Later Mr Jones
decides to increase his Lifestyle Protection cover.

•

The benefit ceases once the total value of options
exercised equals the Initial Nominated Portfolio.

•

The Loss of Income Protection option ceases when
you claim under one of these benefits.

Since his policy already has Living Lifestyle, Mr Jones
will only be able to increase his Living Lifestyle cover
with this option, and cannot select Living Lifestyle
Plus, on the same policy. He can however still purchase
an additional Living Lifestyle Plus benefit under a
different policy.

•

The Lifestyle Protection option ceases when you claim
under one of these benefits.

•

The Life Cover option ceases at the policy anniversary
prior to your 65th birthday.

•

The Loss of Income Protection option and the
Lifestyle Protection option cease on the policy
anniversary prior to your 60th birthday.

How much cover can I select?
You select an Initial Nominated Portfolio at inception of the
policy. The level of the Initial Nominated Portfolio determines
how much total cover you will be able to purchase on an
annual basis in future. On the first policy anniversary you
can exercise options of up to 30% of the value of the Initial
Nominated Portfolio. The amount of the option you can
exercise increases annually at a compound rate of 20% p.a.
The minimum option amount that can be exercised is
R100 000. You cannot exercise total options of more than
the Initial Nominated Portfolio.
You need not take up the full amount of cover. However, if
only a portion of the option is exercised the balance does
NOT accumulate to the next year.
When can I add Future Protector to my policy?
The Future Protector benefit can only be purchased at
policy inception.
Will a purchase of an accelerated version of benefit or
an increase of sum assured of an accelerated benefit
through Future Protector use part of my option amount?
Yes, purchasing accelerated cover or increasing the sum
assured on accelerated cover will use part of the option
amount on your Future Protector benefit.

Example:

On 01/10/2009 Mr Jones purchases a Lifestyle Protector
with R100 000 Life Cover and a Future Protector with an
Initial Nominated Portfolio of R1.5 million.

The Option Value is equal to R300 000 or 30% of his Initial
Nominated Portfolio on 01/10/2010. Mr Jones elects not to
exercise the option at this date.

The Option Value increases by 20% from R300 000 to
R450 000. This is the maximum amount of additional cover
that Mr Jones can purchase. Mr Jones selects to purchase
R450 000 of Life Cover. The total value of options exercised
is R450 000.

The Option Value increases by 20% from R450 000 to R432
000. Mr Jones purchases the maximum cover of
R432 000 and his Life Cover increases to R892 000. The
total value of options exercised is now R792 000.

If the Option Value increases by another 20% it will be equal
to R518 400. Mr Jones is however only allowed to exercise
options to the maximum of the value of the Initial Nominated
Portfolio (i.e. R1.5 million). The maximum amount of additional
cover that Mr Jones can purchase is R208 000 as he has
already exercised R792 000.
In this example, Mr Jones exercised no option in the first
year and the maximum possible option each year thereafter.
He could have exercised less than the full option in any year
and did not need to exercise an option every year after the
first. However, to ensure that the benefit does not cease,
he must exercise the option at least once every three years.

Financial Protector
Benefit Description

Financial Protector is a savings benefit that can be used to protect your policy in times of temporary
financial difficulty. The premium for the Financial Protector is separate from your risk benefit premiums.

Benefit Type

This benefit is a policy protection benefit. The Financial Protector cannot be selected as the sole benefit.

Death Benefit

A death benefit is payable after both you and your Second Life Assured (if included on the policy)
have passed away.
The death benefit is equal to the higher of:
• The value of the Investment Account at the time of death.
• The total premium amount paid to the Financial Protector less any advances and Automatic Policy
Loans taken.

Cover Period

You have the option of a 5-year term.

Minimum Premiums

Premium charge:
The premium charge depends on the cover period selected. The options available are:

Annually

R500 p.a.

the minimum premium for risk benefits.
Maximum Premiums

Maximum premium of 50% of the total Lifestyle Protector policy premium.

Premium Payment Period

The premium payment period corresponds with the cover period. Premiums may continue to be paid
after the compulsory premium payment period ends.

Financial Protector (continued)
Risk Profiles and Portfolio
Objectives

Charges

The risk profile and portfolio objective of each portfolio is as follows:
Lifestyle portfolio

Risk profile

Objective

Lifestyle Secured Capital

Moderately Conservative

This portfolio is aimed at
investors who require stability
and are more concerned with
protecting the current value of
their investments.
